Tidebinder and Wasp

Dimir Midrange interaction guide vs the 13 most-played Standard decks · Sept 2026 · every rules claim adversarially checked
How to read this: each deck has a ranked list of abilities to counter with Tidebinder and creatures to tap-and-blank with Wasp, the exact stack moment, what the permanent loses and keeps afterward, and the traps. Each entry was drafted, then attacked by three independent reviewers (rules lawyer, strategy skeptic, oracle-grounding checker), then revised; corrections were accepted only when backed by oracle text or the rules. Decks ordered by metagame share.

The two cards

Tishana's Tidebinder{2}{U} · 3/2 · Creature · Merfolk Wizard
Flash When this creature enters, counter up to one target activated or triggered ability. If an ability of an artifact, creature, or planeswalker is countered this way, that permanent loses all abilities for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield. (Mana abilities can't be targeted.)
If Tishana's Tidebinder leaves the battlefield before its triggered ability resolves, the target activated or triggered ability will still be countered, but the source of the ability won't lose its abilities at all.
If the affected permanent gains an ability after Tishana's Tidebinder's effect begins to apply to it, it will keep that ability.
Tishana's Tidebinder's triggered ability checks to see if the source of the ability is an artifact, creature, or planeswalker as it resolves. If it isn't, it won't lose all abilities, even if it was an artifact, creature, or planeswalker when the ability was activated or when it triggered.
The Wondrous Wasp{1}{U} · 2/1 · Legendary Creature · Human Hero
Flash Flying Wasp's Sting, When The Wondrous Wasp enters, tap up to one target creature. It loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield.

Rules you must know

  • Tidebinder counters only activated or triggered abilities: static abilities (Voice of Victory's lock, Clarion Conqueror, Momo's discount, Icetill Explorer's land permissions, Torpor Orb, Doorkeeper Thrull), replacement effects ("enters with" counters, "as this enters", Rest in Peace's exile) and anything that is a spell being cast (flashback, warp, adventure, plot, harmonize, Mayhem, the evoke alternative cost, the miracle cast itself) are untouchable; but the miracle TRIGGER (a card in hand, so no blank) and the evoke SACRIFICE trigger are triggered abilities and are legal targets, and countering Lorehold's miracle trigger means the card is never cast for {2}.
  • Mana abilities can never be targeted (printed on Tidebinder): Llanowar Elves, Treasure sacrifice, Springleaf Drum, Firdoch Core's tap, Cavern of Souls and Starting Town are all out of reach; Wasp removes a creature's mana ability directly, and Tidebinder removes it indirectly by countering any non-mana ability of the same artifact, creature or planeswalker (counter Firdoch Core's {4} animation, Tablet of Discovery's ETB mill or Ghost Vacuum's {T} exile and the blanked artifact loses its mana or exile abilities too, since the Core, Tablet or Vacuum is an artifact as the counter resolves).
  • Costs already paid stay paid when an ability is countered: a cracked Fabled Passage stays sacrificed, a Stadium Headliner or Curious Farm Animals stays dead, a ninjutsu'd attacker stays in hand, Zoraline's {W}{B} and 2 life stay spent, M.O.D.O.K.'s 3 life and Glen Elendra Guardian's removed counter are gone, and an exhaust or once-only power-up counts as used.
  • Countering an ETB never removes the permanent; it stays on the battlefield and, if it is an artifact, creature or planeswalker at that moment, loses all abilities for as long as Tidebinder remains (Sunderflock stays a 5/5, Thorin a 3/4, a hard-cast Deceit a 5/5); the one exception is an evoked Deceit or Emptiness, whose own evoke sacrifice trigger still resolves and kills it whichever ETB you counter.
  • The blank applies only if the source is an artifact, creature or planeswalker as Tidebinder's trigger resolves (ruling 3): enchantments (Unholy Annex, Cool but Rude, Case of the Uneaten Feast, Earthbender Ascension, Sheltered by Ghosts, Seam Rip, Nowhere to Run), a land (Petrified Hamlet's naming trigger, a land that has not finished animating), a spell on the stack (Sage of the Skies' cast trigger, Faebloom Trick's reflexive tap), a sacrificed source, or a card in hand or graveyard (Kaito's ninjutsu, Bloodghast, Enduring Curiosity's return, The Eagles Are Coming!, Qarsi Revenant's Renew) are countered but nothing loses abilities.
  • To blank a manland (Restless Reef, Restless Cottage, Restless Anchorage) counter its attack trigger while it is a creature; the land then loses its mana ability even after it reverts, whereas countering the animation activation blanks nothing because the land is not yet a creature.
  • Ward never taxes Tidebinder because it targets the ability on the stack rather than the permanent (CR 702.21a), and the ward trigger itself is a legal Tidebinder target: counter it and your Sheriff, Hex, Drowner or Kaito -2 resolves untaxed while the warded creature (Amalia, Raubahn, Emeritus, Stallion, Vren, Sunset Saboteur) is blanked.
  • Hexproof, Snakeskin Veil, Shore Up, Warg Tactics, Dawn's Truce, "this spell can't be countered" and Cavern of Souls protect the creature or the spell, never its abilities: Tidebinder counters the ETB of a Cavern-cast Thorin, Excruciator or Dragon and is not fizzled by hexproof; against Wasp only hexproof matters, because Wasp targets a creature on the battlefield and uncounterability is a property of the spell, so Surrak, Elusive Hunter and every Cavern-cast creature are legal Wasp targets (Surrak's trigger draws them a card whenever Wasp targets one of their creatures; Tidebinder can counter that draw and blank Surrak).
  • An ability already on the stack exists independently of its source (CR 113.7a): Wasp flashed in response to a landfall, Sunderflock, Crab, Casey or Deceit trigger does not stop it, only Tidebinder countering the trigger (or killing the trigger's target so it fizzles) does.
  • A blank ends the instant the blanker leaves the battlefield (dies, bounced, exiled, blinked, airbended, returned to hand for ninjutsu), and only then: your Tidebinder or Wasp losing its own abilities to their Wasp or Tidebinder does not end the effect (CR 611.2a), and the same protects their locks from yours.
  • If Tidebinder or Wasp leaves the battlefield before its own trigger resolves (Burst Lightning, Torch the Tower, Erode in response), the counter or the tap still happens but the "for as long as it remains" blank never begins (ruling 1, CR 611.2b); if the trigger itself is countered by Spider-Sense, or redirected by Return the Favor, nothing happens at all, and Bilbo's Gambit returns the Tidebinder spell to your hand before it ever enters.
  • Abilities gained after the blank began are kept (ruling 2): a later Practiced Offense, keyword counters placed afterward and Hydro-Man's granted mana ability all work, while grants that predate the blank (an Elf's indestructible activated in response, Sheltered by Ghosts' lifelink and ward) are removed by layer 6 timestamp order; an Equipment gets a NEW timestamp every time it becomes attached (CR 613.7c), so an Axe already on the creature is stripped but the same Axe moved off and back on, or any Equipment attached afterward, grants over the blank, and the only durable Equipment answer is countering the equip activation itself, which blanks the Equipment (an artifact) so it loses both its grant and its equip ability while Tidebinder lives.
  • Blanking never shrinks or kills a creature: +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters stay (Chocobo, Hydra, Mako, Moonshadow are frozen at their current size, a 0/0 Hydra with counters lives), and only a characteristic-defining P/T ability is lost (Lumbering Worldwagon becomes a 0/4).
  • A creature with no abilities when it dies has no leaves-the-battlefield trigger to look back at (CR 603.10a): blank Enduring Curiosity, Enduring Innocence or Enduring Tenacity with Wasp or a Tidebinder counter of one draw trigger, then kill it any time while the blanker is still on the battlefield and it stays dead, and Day of Black Sun kills your own Curiosity permanently the same way.
  • Wasp is legendary: casting a second Wasp forces the legend rule before its trigger goes on the stack, so keeping the old one means the new trigger taps but never blanks, and keeping the new one ends the old blank, so two Wasps are never two simultaneous blanks; likewise their second copy of a legendary (Inti, Tersa, Kaito, Momo) lets them keep the fresh one and discard the blanked one.
  • Tapping does not remove an attacker from combat: to stop an attack Wasp must resolve before their declare attackers step (upkeep, draw step or main phase all work, beginning of combat is the last legal window; your end step for a blocker), and Wasp flashed after attackers are declared only strips flying, lifelink, double strike or trample the creature had before.
  • Warp's end-step exile is a delayed trigger created while the card was a spell (CR 702.185a, 603.7c): blanking a warped Harmonizer, Nova Hellkite or Riddler does not stop the exile, and countering the exile trigger gifts them a permanent copy, so never target it.
  • A delayed trigger created by a resolved spell or ability has that spell or ability's source (CR 603.7d/e): Casey Jones's upkeep discard and Dragonhawk's end-step damage blank Casey or Dragonhawk if still on the battlefield, Morningtide's Light's end-step return belongs to the exiled sorcery (counter it when the Light exiled THEIR creatures and they stay exiled forever, leave it alone when it exiled yours), and an earthbended land's return trigger belongs to Ba Sing Se or Earthbender Ascension, so a Wasp blank on the land cannot remove it and the land still returns.
  • Loyalty abilities are activated abilities: countering any one strips the planeswalker of every ability, including statics, while Tidebinder lives (Fel cannot activate again; Kaito cannot activate again and loses hexproof, so Bitter Triumph or Erode kills him at instant speed on either player's turn), and loyalty counters already added or removed stay.
  • An unblanked Kaito on the battlefield is never a legal creature target for you (a hexproof creature on his controller's turn, a planeswalker on yours), so Wasp, Drowner, Aang, Harvester and Day of Black Sun miss their Kaito on both turns; Erode, Bitter Triumph and Together as One reach him only on YOUR turn (he is a targetable planeswalker then) unless Tidebinder has blanked him, and X=4 Day of Black Sun on your own turn kills your OWN Kaito (a 3/4 creature then), never theirs.
  • Torpor Orb (Mono-Green, Selesnya and Jeskai sideboards) and Doorkeeper Thrull (Boros Dragons sideboard, flash) turn off both Tidebinder's and Wasp's ETBs and cannot be countered once resolved; Orb is an artifact spell with mana value 2, so Annul, Spell Snare, Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay! answers it, while Thrull is a creature spell that Annul and Spell Pierce cannot target, so hold Spell Snare or We Say Thee Nay! on their turn, and once either has resolved Requiting Hex kills the Thrull (MV 2) or you accept a 3/2 and a 2/1 flash body.
  • Wan Shi Tong is mana value 2 on the battlefield because X is 0 anywhere but the stack (CR 202.3b): Requiting Hex kills a 5/5 Wan Shi Tong for {B}, so Tidebinder on its ETB is for the denied counters and cards, not the body; Dyadrine, Synthesis Amalgam ({X}{G}{W}) is a Hex target for the same reason.
  • Day of Black Sun destroys your own blankers at X=2 (Wasp, Drowner, Siren, Beavers, Wan Shi Tong) and X=3 (Tidebinder), and every blank they held ends when they die, so choose X below your lock pieces or accept the reset.

Universal traps

  • Flashing Tidebinder in response to a creature SPELL (Excruciator, Spider-Man, Thorin, Sunderflock, Harmonizer): a spell is not an ability, Tidebinder's trigger finds nothing, and the ETB then resolves unopposed; let the spell resolve and hit the trigger (the one exception is a "when you cast" trigger such as Sage of the Skies', which is on the stack while the spell is).
  • Flashing Wasp (or a second blanker) in response to a trigger already on the stack expecting to stop it: the trigger resolves regardless of its source; Wasp only stops future triggers, so cast it at their upkeep or end step, before the land drop or the cast.
  • Targeting a static or replacement effect: Voice of Victory's lock, Clarion Conqueror, Explorer's extra land drop, Kili's free equip, Momo's discount, Dragonfire Blade's hexproof, Riddler's extra draw, "enters with a counter", shock-land life payments; reach these only by countering a trigger or activation of the same permanent (Momo's +1/+1 trigger, Kili's draw trigger, an equip activation) or with Wasp.
  • Countering the warp end-step exile to keep a blanked Harmonizer, Nova or Riddler as a vanilla body: you hand them a permanent creature that wakes up the moment Tidebinder dies.
  • Countering an activation whose cost was a sacrifice, discard or graveyard exile expecting a blank (fetch lands, Headliner, Farm Animals, Case of the Uneaten Feast, Mako cycling, Harvester's hand mode, Renew, Ghost Vacuum's {6}): the source is gone or is not a permanent, so only the one effect is denied; Ghost Vacuum's {T} exile mode is different, the Vacuum stays and is blanked.
  • Countering a manland's animation activation to blank the land: the land is not a creature when the counter resolves, so it keeps every ability; only the attack trigger while animated blanks it.
  • Waiting for the reflexive or second trigger when the parent is the one that matters: Thorin's damage fires after the Equipment already moved, Inti's "when you do" fires after the discard already fed every payoff, Casey Jones's random discard comes after the three cards were drawn (counter the ETB), Spider-Man's Mind Swap exile is a decoy beside the copied Excruciator ETB.
  • Countering an evoke sacrifice trigger (Deceit, Emptiness): the bounce, discard or -1/-1 ETB still resolves and they keep a 5/5 or 3/5 body for two mana; always target the ETB effect (the evoke sacrifice then kills the body for you).
  • Countering a delayed trigger that only helps them: Mobilize tokens' end-step sacrifice (they keep the Warriors), Casey Jones's random discard when the cards are worth more than the fuel, Morningtide's Light's return when it exiled YOUR creatures (when it exiled theirs, countering the return is a permanent exile of their team and one of the best Tidebinder targets in the Momo matchup).
  • Returning the Wasp or Tidebinder that is holding a blank to hand for ninjutsu, or attacking with it into Settle the Wreckage, reach Treefolk, first strike or a Resolve fight: the blank ends the moment it leaves; use Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers as the ninjutsu carrier.
  • Wasping into open protection or cheap removal mana when the blank is the point: Snakeskin Veil, Warg Tactics, Shore Up, Dawn's Truce fizzle the Sting; Torch, Erode, Burst Lightning or Prismari Charm in response to the trigger means the tap happens but no blank ever begins; Spider-Sense counters the trigger outright.
  • Skipping Wasp on Surrak, Gigantic Big Bear's neighbours or a Cavern-cast creature because "the spell can't be countered": uncounterability protects the spell, not the permanent; Surrak (no hexproof) is a legal Sting, only Gigantic Big Bear's printed hexproof and hexproof grants stop Wasp, and remember Surrak draws them a card when Wasp targets any of their creatures.
  • Casting Wasp #2 for a second simultaneous blank: the legend rule resolves first, so at most one Wasp blank exists at a time; the second copy is a tap, a blank transfer or redundancy after the first dies.
  • Believing Cavern of Souls, "this spell can't be countered", hexproof or ward turns Tidebinder off: all of them protect the spell or the permanent, not the ability on the stack; Cavern-cast Thorin, Excruciator and Dragons are exactly where Tidebinder is the only out.
  • Blanking THEIR Wasp or Tidebinder to free your locked creature: their effect lasts as long as the blanker remains on the battlefield, not as long as it has abilities; kill, bounce or Drowner-shuffle the blanker instead.
  • Casting Tidebinder or Wasp under a resolved Torpor Orb or into open Doorkeeper Thrull mana expecting the ETB: nothing triggers; kill the Thrull (Requiting Hex, MV 2) first, and for the spells keep the counter map straight, Annul or Spell Pierce hit the Orb but never the Thrull, Spell Snare and We Say Thee Nay! hit both.
  • Treating a Wasp blank on an equipped Dwarf as permanent: the Equipment already attached is stripped, but the next equip activation (free with Kili's enduring story, or Thorin's and Raubahn's attach effects) gives the Equipment a new timestamp and its double strike, trample or hexproof works again; counter the equip activation with Tidebinder to blank the Equipment itself.
  • Kaito -2, Drowner's stun or a tap on Voice of Victory or Clarion Conqueror to lift the lock: static abilities work while tapped or stunned; only Wasp's blank, Day of Black Sun or removal ends it, and both die to two-mana removal you already run (Voice, MV 2 Human Bard: Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph; Conqueror, MV 3 Dragon: Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph, never Hex).
  • Holding Tidebinder for Voice of Victory's Mobilize trigger: Voice attacking means an un-blanked Voice on its controller's turn, whose static forbids casting Tidebinder; that window never exists, and a second attacking Voice locks you out the same way.
  • Treating a countered ETB as removal: a blanked Sunderflock, Harmonizer, Excruciator or Deceit is still a 5/5, 4/4, 6/6 or 5/5 that needs Drowner, Kaito -2, Sheriff or Bitter Triumph, and killing a real Doomsday Excruciator feeds their Spider-Men unless Ghost Vacuum is online.
  • Countering the first Curious Farm Animals activation aimed at a creature-form Enduring Curiosity, or killing an Enduring card without blanking it: the creature version returns as an enchantment that still draws; blank first, then kill, or counter the return trigger.
  • Spell Pierce on a creature spell (Mightform Harmonizer, Sunderflock, Excruciator, Doorkeeper Thrull) or Flashfreeze on a colorless or white spell (Torpor Orb, Voice, Practiced Offense): the counter has no legal target; keep the counterspell map straight before deciding Tidebinder is free.
  • Counting Requiting Hex as the answer to a blanked legendary they run two of: it kills Inti (MV 2) but not Tersa Lightshatter ({2}{R}, MV 3), who needs Shoot the Sheriff (Orc Wizard, not an outlaw) or Bitter Triumph.
  • Sweeping with Day of Black Sun at X=2 or higher while a Wasp or Tidebinder blank is load-bearing: your own blanker dies and the blanked creature regains everything, so pick X=1 or accept the reset.

Top Tidebinder targets in the format

#1Mightform Harmonizer Mono-Green Landfall (18.4%) and Selesnya Landfall (3.5%)
Landfall: whenever a land you control enters, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn.
The largest deck in the format kills with this one trigger, often off a fetch cracked after blockers, and countering it both stops the doubling on the stack and, if the Harmonizer was hard-cast, blanks the kill multiplier for as long as Tidebinder lives; nearly 22% of the field combined, highest impact per counter.
#2Sunderflock Izzet Spellementals (12.2%)
When this creature enters, if you cast it, return all non-Elemental creatures to their owners' hands.
Second-largest deck; the counter saves your whole board from a one-sided bounce and strips flying from their only flyer, the one body that blocks the Enduring Curiosity engine, and it is the trigger Cavern-free counterspells cannot reach once they refuse to cast into open mana.
#3Icetill Explorer Mono-Green Landfall and Selesnya Landfall (about 22% combined)
Landfall: whenever a land you control enters, mill a card (the counter is the only door to its static extra land drop and graveyard land plays).
Always hard-cast so the blank is real; countering a near-worthless mill halves their landfall count for as long as Tidebinder lives by deleting both static permissions, and it is the default target when Harmonizer is not on the table.
#4Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel activation Mono-Green Landfall and Selesnya Landfall
{T}, Sacrifice this land: search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle.
Countering the fetch denies every landfall trigger of that land at once (Chocobo, Harmonizer, Hydra, Explorer, Nursery) plus the land itself, which is the right answer to the deck's mid-combat kill line and to any turn a warped Harmonizer is leaving anyway; eight copies in the biggest deck.
#5Kaito, Bane of Nightmares loyalty activation Dimir Midrange (mirror)
+1 emblem, 0 surveil and draw, or -2 tap and two stun counters (any loyalty ability).
The mirror is the deck you play and expect often; one counter strips Kaito of all abilities including the hexproof static, so he never ticks again while Tidebinder lives and Bitter Triumph kills him at instant speed on either turn; counter the -2 aimed at your engine first, then the 0.
#6Doomsday Excruciator ETB, including Superior Spider-Man entering as a copy Dimir Excruciator (5.7%)
When this creature enters, if it was cast, each player exiles all but the bottom six cards of their library face down.
The single most game-deciding trigger in the format and Tidebinder is the only card in our 75 that stops it through Cavern of Souls; smaller share than the top four but the counter wins the game outright, and the blank grounds the 6/6 as a bonus.
#7Inti, Seneschal of the Sun Mardu Discard (4.7%)
Whenever you attack, you may discard a card, when you do put a +1/+1 counter on an attacking creature (or the discard-impulse trigger).
Their repeatable card engine on a creature with no hexproof or ward; countering the attack trigger before the discard denies the turn's fuel for Mako and the Class, blanks Inti while Tidebinder lives, and leaves an untapped 3/2 blocker in the same combat; post-board Monument to Endurance's discard trigger outranks it, since one counter blanks the artifact and their whole engine with it.
#8Thorin, Mountain-king ETB, or an equip activation (Leyline Axe, Dragonfire Blade) Boros Dwarves (4.1%)
When Thorin enters, attach any number of target Equipment you control to target creature you control, then that creature deals damage equal to its power to up to one target creature. Equip {3} / Equip {4}.
Thorin's first trigger is their mass re-equip plus their only main-deck removal, and when cast off Cavern of Souls Tidebinder is the only out; counter the first trigger, never the reflexive damage, and Sheriff the 3/4 afterward. When Thorin is not in play, counter an equip activation instead: the Equipment is an artifact as the counter resolves, so it loses both its grant and its equip ability while Tidebinder lives, which is the only Equipment answer a re-equip cannot undo.
#9Wan Shi Tong, Librarian ETB at X of 2 or more Dimir Midrange (mirror)
When Wan Shi Tong enters, put X +1/+1 counters on him, then draw half X cards rounded down.
The biggest single swing in the mirror: their six mana buys a 1/1 with no flying and no cards, the denial is permanent even if Tidebinder dies next turn, and the body is then a Requiting Hex target; also protects against the same play from their Tidebinder when you hold {2}{U} on your own end step.
#10Mossborn Hydra Selesnya Landfall (3.5% main) and Mono-Green Landfall (18.4%, sideboard)
Landfall: whenever a land you control enters, double the number of +1/+1 counters on this creature.
Exponential growth with trample, always hard-cast; the one countered doubling is denied for good and the Hydra stops growing and loses trample for as long as Tidebinder lives (doubling resumes from its current counters the moment Tidebinder leaves), and post-board it shows up in the biggest deck in the format alongside Harmonizer.

Top Wasp targets in the format

#1Mightform Harmonizer (hard-cast) Mono-Green Landfall (18.4%) and Selesnya Landfall (3.5%)
Blank it at their end step the turn they tap out for it (no green for Snakeskin Veil) and no land ever doubles anything while Wasp lives; useless against a warped copy, which leaves at end step regardless, and with Surrak on their board the Sting draws them a card.
#2Icetill Explorer Mono-Green Landfall and Selesnya Landfall
Wasp removes the two static land permissions with no trigger window needed, cutting their landfall count roughly in half for two mana, and Explorer has no flying or reach so it can never block the 2/1.
#3Mossborn Hydra / Sazh's Chocobo at their upkeep Selesnya Landfall and Mono-Green Landfall
On the draw their turn 3 comes before Tidebinder is castable, so Wasp at their upkeep freezes the doubling target before the land drop, leaving Harmonizer a 1-power body to double; both keep their counters but stop growing and Hydra loses trample.
#4Marauding Mako, then Iron-Shield Elf / Hardened Academic once Cool but Rude is level 2 Mardu Discard (4.7%)
Wasp is premium here: no hexproof or ward anywhere, Mako freezes at its current size and a tap before attackers wastes Practiced Offense's double strike; once the Class is level 2 the free instant-speed discard outlets are the burn engine and become the target instead.
#5Clarion Conqueror and Voice of Victory (lock pieces) when no kill spell is in hand Orzhov Lifegain (4%), Boros Tokens (2.1%), Boros Dragons (1.6%), Azorius Tempo (1.5%), Mardu sideboard
Both locks are statics Tidebinder cannot touch and Voice forbids casting either card on their turn, so everything happens on OUR turn; the first choice is removal you already run (Voice dies to Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph; the MV-3 Dragon Conqueror dies to Sheriff or Triumph, never Hex), and Wasp's blank is the fallback when none is in hand, because Erode or Torch on the 2/1 restores the lock.
#6Kili the Resourceful Boros Dwarves (4.1%)
Wasp at their upkeep removes both the once-per-turn draw trigger and the static free equip, the two things that make the deck run, for two mana and no trigger window; Hex it instead when Hex is in hand since the blank ends when the 2/1 dies, and remember a blanked equipped Dwarf regains its Equipment's grants the next time it is equipped.
#7Sunderflock (resolved) Izzet Spellementals (12.2%)
Their only flyer; grounding it lets Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren and Wasp connect every turn for Curiosity draws and lets ground bodies block the 5/5, and a tap before attackers stops one flying attack; skip it if Tidebinder already blanked the ETB.
#8Enduring Curiosity (creature version), or a resolved Wan Shi Tong Dimir Midrange (mirror) and Azorius Tempo (1.5%)
Blank removes both the combat-damage draw and the dies-return trigger, so Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph kills it for good at instant speed any time while Wasp lives; a Stung Wan Shi Tong loses flying, vigilance and the search trigger; fire it when they are tapped below Hex or Spell Snare mana, not into open removal.

Hold or fire

  • Hold Tidebinder only when a named engine is already on their battlefield (Explorer, hard-cast Harmonizer, Hydra, Kili, Inti, Moseo, Kaito, Momo, Firdoch Core, Monument) or a known trigger is one turn away (Sunderflock live off any Elemental, Chamber off five mana, Excruciator in a graveyard); against decks whose engine is static, a spell or a mana ability (Golgari's spells, Boros Tokens, Boros Dragons) develop instead and treat it as the spare-mana play.
  • Holding is cheap when the same {2}{U} becomes Floodpits Drowner, Enduring Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong at their end step if nothing worth countering appears; it is expensive on the turn Kaito wants {1}{U}{B} for ninjutsu, and on those turns Kaito usually wins because he survives bounces, blanks and sorcery-speed removal.
  • Keep the mana up through their COMBAT, not just their main phase: fetches cracked after blockers, Smaug's, Inti's, Raubahn's, Emeritus's and Dyadrine's attack triggers, Reef and Cottage all fire in declare attackers, and Tidebinder entering there can still block.
  • On the draw you have two lands during their turn 3, so Tidebinder can never answer that turn; Wasp or Drowner at their upkeep or beginning of combat is the turn-3 tool and the Tidebinder hold starts on their turn 4, while on the play their turn 3 is the first live window.
  • Value the counter over the lock against decks with many cheap answers to a 3/2 (mirror, Izzet burn, Orzhov Erode, Mardu Hex, Boros Torch): prefer targets whose payoff survives Tidebinder dying (a denied Wan Shi Tong ETB, a Curiosity return, a fetch crack, a Chamber Demon, a Casey draw-three, a Morningtide's Light return of their team) and treat every blank as lasting about one turn cycle.
  • Distinguish hard-cast from warped: a hard-cast Harmonizer's blank lasts, a warped one leaves at end step whatever you do, so against warp counter the fetch activation or the payoff's trigger for the one turn and never expect a durable lock.
  • Counter the parent trigger, not the reflexive one, and counter a creature's or artifact's trigger over an enchantment's, spell's or land's whenever both are on the stack, because only an artifact, creature or planeswalker gets blanked.
  • When a discard trigger (Deceit, Oildeep Gearhulk) or a hand-attack would take Tidebinder from your hand, fire it at that trigger even as your only copy; a Tidebinder in hand while you are tapped out is itself a discard target.
  • Check for lock pieces before anything else: under Voice of Victory or Jennifer Walters both cards are our-turn plays, so kill Voice on our turn first (Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph; Wasp only without those) and Conqueror with Sheriff or Triumph, and under High Noon spend our turn's one spell on development and their turn's on Tidebinder or removal.
  • Never spend the blank on a legendary they run two of (Inti, Tersa, Momo) when the right kill spell is in hand (Hex for Inti and Momo, Sheriff or Triumph for Tersa); the second copy resets the blank under the legend rule, so save Wasp and Tidebinder for non-legendary engines.
  • Wasp timing rule of three: their END STEP on the turn they tap out for the engine creature (no protection mana), their UPKEEP before the land drop for landfall or draw engines, and any point before DECLARE ATTACKERS (beginning of combat at the latest) to stop exactly one attacker; never in response to a trigger and never into open Veil, Warg Tactics, Shore Up, Dawn's Truce, Spider-Sense or one-mana removal when the blank is the point.
  • Convert every blank into a kill while the window is open: Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex, Kaito -2 into Drowner's shuffle, or Day of Black Sun with X below your blankers; protect the body with Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay! against Erode, Resolve and Daydream.
  • Fire Tidebinder as a bare 3/2 only when its targets are gone or unreachable (engine dead, hand empty, Torpor Orb resolved, a lock piece live with no our-turn window) and never before the deck's known kill turn against landfall, Excruciator or Dwarves.
  • Do not ninjutsu Kaito off a Wasp or Tidebinder holding a blank, and post-board keep a second creature on the battlefield so Strategic Betrayal cannot force you to exile the blanker.

Sideboarding the extra Tidebinders

Prerequisite: the MTGGoldfish stock sideboard in the data file carries ZERO Tidebinder (3 Duress, 2 Day of Black Sun, Annul, Ghost Vacuum, 2 Spell Pierce, Strategic Betrayal, Vren, 2 Flashfreeze, 2 Faebloom Trick); the Karsten Sept 3 aggregate carries 2. If yours is the Goldfish side, the entries recommend replacing the 2 Flashfreeze with 2 Tidebinder before the event (Flashfreeze is dead in the mirror, vs Excruciator, Orzhov, Boros Tokens and Azorius, and hits only red or green spells elsewhere). Main-deck construction: the 18.4% Mono-Green share is the strongest argument for a second main Tidebinder over a Spell Snare, never over Shoot the Sheriff (Sheriff and Bitter Triumph are also your only two-mana answers to Clarion Conqueror and Tersa, which Requiting Hex cannot kill); the Izzet entry defers that call to a cross-matchup decision, so treat it as an option, not a rule. BRING BOTH SB TIDEBINDERS IN (3 total): Mono-Green Landfall (cut Spell Snare tier, never Sheriff; keep one Spell Snare or Annul live for their 2 sideboard Torpor Orb, and expect Surrak to draw off every Wasp), Selesnya Landfall (same Torpor Orb note; Dyadrine is a Hex target on the battlefield), Boros Dwarves (cut 2 Wan Shi Tong and 1 Bitter Triumph, add Annul; the equip activation is the durable Tidebinder target), Dimir Excruciator (all three main, cut 2 Spell Snare, 3 Requiting Hex, 2 Wan Shi Tong; Ghost Vacuum first in), Izzet Spellementals (with +2 Spell Pierce, +2 Flashfreeze, out 4 Requiting Hex and BOTH Wasps), the Dimir mirror (cut 2 Spell Snare, keep all 4 Hex and both Wasps; Drowner's shuffle activation and Vren's end-step trigger are extra targets), and Golgari Midrange conditionally as cards seven and eight after 3 Duress, 2 Spell Pierce and Annul (cut 2 Wasp, 2 Spell Snare, then 2 Hex; Firdoch Core's {4} animation is a real Tidebinder target that blanks the mana rock). BRING ONE IN (2 total): Mardu Discard (3 only if they show Monument to Endurance and no Voice of Victory, since one countered Monument trigger blanks the artifact; always bring Annul for Cool but Rude, Flashfreeze x2, Day of Black Sun x2, leave Duress out since it feeds their discard payoffs) and Azorius Tempo (second slot goes to Spell Pierce, Duress or Annul; keep Spell Snare and Hex; Bilbo's Gambit returns your Tidebinder spell to hand and Aven Interrupter's ETB is a counter target). BRING NONE (stock 1): Orzhov Lifegain (2 at most if available, never 3, because 3 Voice plus Erode and {B}{B} Emptiness gate the counter; in Day of Black Sun, Vren, Strategic Betrayal), Azorius Momo (in 3 Duress, 2 Spell Pierce, 1 Day of Black Sun; cut Spell Snare and 2 Hex; the one Tidebinder is for Momo's +1/+1 trigger, Glen Elendra's activation or a Morningtide's Light return of their own team, and their Spider-Sense counters your ETB), Boros Tokens (in Day of Black Sun and Duress; Hex, Sheriff or Triumph is the Voice answer) and Boros Dragons (in Day of Black Sun, Annul, Duress; skip Flashfreeze, never side out Hex; hold Spell Snare or We Say Thee Nay! for their 2 sideboard Doorkeeper Thrull, which Annul and Spell Pierce cannot touch, and Hex it if it resolves). WASP: keep 2 everywhere and never add a third; the two places it comes OUT are Izzet Spellementals (their only flyer is Sunderflock, the 2/1 dies to every damage spell including Prismari Charm's 1 damage, and Stallion taxes it) and Golgari Midrange (its Sting targets are an animated Firdoch Core, which Tidebinder handles better, and post-board Qarsi Revenant, and it dies to Hex, Sheriff, two Nowhere to Run, Cover-Up and Intimidation Tactics; keep one only as a third evasive Curiosity carrier). Companion cards that decide whether the blankers are live: Spell Snare or We Say Thee Nay! for Doorkeeper Thrull and Torpor Orb (Annul and Spell Pierce for the Orb only; both cards switch off every ETB we own), Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph for Voice of Victory (MV 2) and Sheriff or Bitter Triumph for Clarion Conqueror (MV 3, so Hex misses), with Wasp only when no kill spell is in hand, Ghost Vacuum against Spider-Man recursion, and Day of Black Sun only with X chosen below your own blankers (X=1 keeps both, X=2 kills Wasp, X=3 kills Tidebinder, X=4 kills your own Kaito on your turn and your Curiosity permanently, and never kills their Kaito, who is a planeswalker on your turn).
vs. Mono-Green Landfall18.4% TB: premiumWasp: good▼
Every land that enters is a payday: Sazh's Chocobo ("Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature") grows, Mightform Harmonizer ("Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn") turns one land entry into lethal, and Icetill Explorer ("You may play an additional land on each of your turns. You may play lands from your graveyard.") plus Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel / Shared Roots / Earthbender Ascension manufacture two to four land entries a turn. The kill turn is usually their turn 4-5: Harmonizer is warped in for {2}{G}, they attack, and AFTER blockers are declared they crack a Fabled Passage or Escape Tunnel (an activated ability, usable mid-combat) so the doubling lands on an unblocked attacker; on the play with Llanowar Elves a turn-3 warp plus Passage crack already makes a 10-power Chocobo. Trample comes from Ascension's quest payoff or Summon: Esper Maduin's chapter III. Post-board they add Mossborn Hydra (counter doubling), Torpor Orb (switches off every ETB we own, including Tidebinder's and Wasp's), Gigantic Big Bear (hexproof, can't be countered) and more Meltstrider's Resolve to fight the 3/2 or 2/1 that is holding their engine blank.
Tishana's Tidebinder
This is Tidebinder's best matchup in the format. The whole deck runs on landfall TRIGGERS from CREATURES, so one Tidebinder both counters the trigger that would kill you and blanks the engine creature (Explorer, hard-cast Harmonizer, Hydra) for as long as the 3/2 lives. It also ignores their protection: Snakeskin Veil and Warg Tactics give a creature hexproof, but Tidebinder targets the ABILITY on the stack, not the creature, and Surrak's draw trigger ('Whenever a creature you control or a creature spell you control becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls') never fires off Tidebinder. Two things to know before you fire it. First, hard-cast versus warped Harmonizer: a hard-cast one (4 mana) stays blanked as long as Tidebinder lives, which is premium; a warped one ({2}{G}) is exiled at end step no matter what, so Tidebinder on its trigger only buys the turn, and if a fetchland is what delivers the doubling you counter the FETCH ACTIVATION instead (no land enters, nothing triggers). Second, the blank is only as durable as the 3/2: Meltstrider's Resolve fights it and 3/4 reach Treefolk block it, so convert a blanked engine into a dead one with Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph when you can. The only card that shuts Tidebinder off is post-board Torpor Orb ('Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger', a static ability): Annul / Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! it on the stack or Tidebinder is just a 3/2.
#1Mightform Harmonizer
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn.
WhyThis is the card that turns a 4/5 Chocobo into a 16/5 with two land entries. Countering the trigger stops the doubling on the stack AND (while Tidebinder lives) blanks Harmonizer, so every later land that turn (Explorer's second drop, a Passage crack) doubles nothing. HARD-CAST Harmonizer (they paid {2}{G}{G}, it stays): this blank is permanent and is the best thing Tidebinder does in Standard. WARPED Harmonizer (they paid {2}{G}): it is exiled at the beginning of the next end step regardless of the blank (the exile is a delayed trigger created when the warp cost was paid; it exists independently of the creature's current text, CR 603.7a/603.7c), and they may recast it from exile later for its full 4-mana cost with all its text back, because it is a new object. So against a warp, Tidebinder buys one turn plus three of their mana; if the landfall that carries the doubling is a Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel crack, counter the fetch activation instead (target 3), which denies the doubling, the Chocobo counter, the Treefolk and the land at once. Killing the doubling's target (Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph on the creature it targets) makes the trigger fizzle but leaves Harmonizer live for the next land.
WhenTheir turn, the moment a land enters: all landfall triggers go on the stack at once (Chocobo counter, Explorer mill, Harmonizer double, Nursery token, Ascension quest). They order them; you get priority with everything on the stack. Flash Tidebinder, target Harmonizer's trigger wherever they stacked it. Watch two windows: (a) the precombat land drop, and (b) a fetch cracked AFTER blockers are declared, which is where they actually kill; keep {2}{U} up through their combat, not just their main phase. They cannot respond by playing another land (a land drop needs an empty stack in their main phase). On the play, their first dangerous turn is turn 3 (Elves + two Forests = warp, then Passage into basic = two doublings on a Chocobo, about 10 power); on the draw their turn 3 comes while you have two lands, so Tidebinder is impossible there and your turn-4 hold is the first real one.
AfterHarmonizer stays a 4/4 body with no landfall trigger (its warp text is irrelevant on the battlefield). Any doubling that already resolved this turn stays until end of turn. Abilities return the moment Tidebinder leaves the battlefield, and a warped copy is exiled at end step either way.
RiskYou must keep 3 mana open on their turn while Harmonizer or Explorer is live or a warp is plausible; the fallback that makes this cheap is flashing Floodpits Drowner, Enduring Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong at their end step with the same mana if nothing worth countering happens. Real risk: with Chocobo, Nursery and Explorer also triggering, countering only Harmonizer's trigger lets those three resolve.
#2Icetill Explorer
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, mill a card. (The two statics 'You may play an additional land on each of your turns' and 'You may play lands from your graveyard' cannot be targeted; you reach them by countering the mill.)
WhyCounter a near-worthless mill trigger and Explorer loses both static abilities: no second land drop, no replaying Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel from the graveyard, and no more milling Esper Origins into flashback range. This halves their landfall count for as long as Tidebinder lives, and Explorer is always hard-cast (no warp), so the blank is real. When Harmonizer is not on the table this is the target.
WhenAny land entering while Explorer is out puts the mill trigger on the stack; counter it. Best moment is their FIRST land drop of a turn: once Explorer is blanked they cannot make a second land drop that turn (the extra-land permission is checked when they try to play the land). Countering the trigger does not undo the land already played.
AfterExplorer is a 2/4 with no text. Lands already on the battlefield stay; cards already milled stay in the graveyard (Esper Origins there is still flashback-castable, that is a spell, not an ability). Regains everything if Tidebinder dies, so follow up with Shoot the Sheriff (Insect Scout is not an outlaw) when convenient.
RiskLow. Explorer is a 2/4, so your Tidebinder cannot attack into it; the value is entirely in the blank.
#3Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel (fetch activations, 8 copies)
Fabled Passage: '{T}, Sacrifice this land: Search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle. Then if you control four or more lands, untap that land.' Escape Tunnel: '{T}, Sacrifice this land: Search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle.' (Also: Escape Tunnel's '{T}, Sacrifice this land: Target creature with power 2 or less can't be blocked this turn.')
WhyCountering the fetch means the land never enters, so EVERY landfall trigger for that land is denied at once (Chocobo, Harmonizer, Explorer, Nursery, Ascension), the sacrificed land stays sacrificed (they are down a land), and with 4+ lands they also lose the untapped basic that would have paid for Snakeskin Veil. Neither ability adds mana, so neither is a mana ability; both are legal targets. Nothing is blanked (the source is a land). This is the right target whenever the fetch is cracked mid-combat after blockers (the deck's kill line), whenever Harmonizer was WARPED this turn (it is leaving anyway, so its blank is worth little), or whenever two or more payoffs are triggering. Prefer Harmonizer's own trigger only when it was hard-cast and it is the one thing that matters, because that blank lasts. Escape Tunnel's unblockable mode is normally ignorable, but with Harmonizer on the battlefield it is a combo (make a 2-power creature unblockable, then double it twice); counter that activation only in that spot.
WhenWhen they activate it. Two habits: in their main phase with 4+ lands (untapped basic) and, the one that matters, mid-combat after blockers are declared with Chocobo or an earthbended land attacking. Respond to the activation itself; once the land has entered this window is gone and you are back to countering a single landfall trigger.
AfterPassage/Tunnel is in their graveyard with no land fetched. If Icetill Explorer is alive and unblanked they can replay it from the graveyard as a land drop and crack it again, so this is a true Stone Rain only when Explorer is dead or blanked. Side effect for us: no search happened, so Wan Shi Tong does not trigger.
RiskSpends Tidebinder on a one-shot with no lasting blank. Wrong choice when Harmonizer was hard-cast and the doubling is the only thing on the stack that matters.
#4Mossborn Hydra (sideboard, 4 copies)
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the number of +1/+1 counters on this creature.
WhyPost-board this is the second Harmonizer: 1 counter becomes 2, 4, 8, 16 with two land entries a turn. Counter the first doubling and Hydra is frozen at its current counters, loses trample, and is a 0/0-base creature kept alive only by the counters it already has. It is always hard-cast, so the blank lasts.
WhenTheir turn, first land entry after Hydra resolves. 'This creature enters with a +1/+1 counter on it' is a replacement effect and cannot be countered, so Hydra always starts as a 1/1; the first landfall trigger is the one to hit.
AfterKeeps its existing +1/+1 counters (counters are not abilities), loses trample and future doubling. A 1/1 or 2/2 for as long as Tidebinder lives; if Tidebinder dies the doubling resumes from wherever the counters are.
RiskIf Hydra already has 8+ counters, blanking it does not solve the body; Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff it and save Tidebinder for Harmonizer.
#5Summon: Esper Maduin (back face of Esper Origins, Enchantment Creature, Saga Elemental 4/4)
III, Other creatures you control get +2/+2 and gain trample until end of turn. (Also counterable: I, Reveal the top card of your library. If it's a permanent card, put it into your hand. II, Add {G}{G}. Chapter II is NOT a mana ability because it triggers off a lore counter, not off a mana ability, CR 605.1b.)
WhyChapter III is a team pump with trample on their attack turn, and it comes from a CREATURE, so countering it blanks the Saga. A Saga with no chapter abilities is not sacrificed (CR 714.4 applies only to a Saga 'with one or more chapter abilities') and gets no further lore counters (CR 714.3c), so it just sits as a 4/4 body. The moment Tidebinder leaves, its abilities return, it already has 3 lore counters, and it is sacrificed at once; the finality counter exiles it instead.
WhenChapter III triggers as their precombat main phase begins on the second turn after the Saga entered (third lore counter). Counter it there, before attackers. The Saga only ever enters via the flashback cast from the graveyard ({3}{G}) with a finality counter; the front face is a sorcery and cannot be countered by Tidebinder (Spell Snare can: mana value 2 even when flashed back).
After4/4 body with no chapters, keeps its finality counter and its 3 lore counters. Other creatures do not get +2/+2 or trample this turn.
RiskLow value if their board is one creature; the pump is what matters. Chapters I (a free card) and II (two mana) are counterable but almost never worth the card.
#6Meltstrider's Resolve (2 main, 2 more side) fight trigger
When this Aura enters, enchanted creature fights up to one target creature an opponent controls.
WhyThis is their removal, and post-board it is aimed at the 3/2 or 2/1 that is holding their engine blank: their 4/4 fights Tidebinder or Wasp, it dies, and Harmonizer / Explorer get all their text back at once. A second Tidebinder countering the fight trigger keeps the blank alive; it also saves Enduring Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong. The Aura is an enchantment, so nothing is blanked and it keeps '+0/+2 and can't be blocked by more than one creature'.
WhenIn response to the ETB trigger, on their turn. Cheaper answers to the Aura SPELL (mana value 1): Spell Pierce, We Say Thee Nay!, or Annul.
AfterAura stays on their creature; no fight happens.
RiskOnly fire if the creature being fought matters: a blank-holding Tidebinder/Wasp, Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong.
#7Lumbering Worldwagon (1 main)
'Whenever this Vehicle enters or attacks, you may search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle.' Also counterable: 'Crew 4' (an activated ability, CR 702.122a).
WhyCountering the ETB or attack trigger denies a landfall (and a Wan Shi Tong search for us), and since Wagon is an artifact it loses all abilities: the characteristic-defining 'This Vehicle's power is equal to the number of lands you control' is gone so it is a 0/4, and with no crew it can never become a creature again while Tidebinder lives. Countering a crew activation does the same: the creatures they tapped stay tapped and Wagon does not animate. Worth it on their turn 3 when Chocobo and Explorer are already down and the fetched land would trigger both; otherwise do not hold Tidebinder for a one-of.
WhenETB trigger the turn it is cast (3 mana, turn 2-3), or the attack trigger in their declare-attackers step: if it was crewed this turn it stays a creature until end of turn but attacks as a 0/4.
AfterA 0/4 artifact with no text. Lands it already fetched stay.
RiskIt is a one-of; the value is opportunistic.
#8Surrak, Elusive Hunter (1 main, 1 side) draw trigger
Whenever a creature you control or a creature spell you control becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, draw a card.
WhyPost-board there are two, and every Wasp, Drowner, Kaito -2 and Bitter Triumph you point at their creatures draws them a card while Surrak lives. Counter the draw trigger (it fires the first time you target something, including targeting Surrak himself) and Surrak is a 4/3 with no trample and no draws, so the rest of your targeted removal is free again. 'This spell can't be countered' protects the spell only, not this ability.
WhenWhen the draw trigger is on the stack, on top of whatever you just cast (your Drowner/Wasp/Bitter Triumph). A 5-mana sequence, so usually your turn or their end step with mana to spare; if you just want him dead, Bitter Triumph him and accept the one draw.
After4/3 with no text while Tidebinder lives; cards already drawn stay.
RiskLow priority against a 1-of main; real only post-board when they show the second copy and your hand is removal-heavy.
Traps
Llanowar Elves / Forest / Ba Sing Se ({T}: Add {G}): Counter the mana ability to blank Elves. Mana abilities cannot be targeted; Tidebinder's own reminder text says so. Wasp can tap-and-blank Elves; Tidebinder cannot touch it.
Icetill Explorer: Target 'You may play an additional land' or 'You may play lands from your graveyard'. Both are static abilities and never go on the stack. You get them only by countering the landfall mill trigger, which blanks the creature.
Mightform Harmonizer (warp): Counter the warp cast with Tidebinder or Spell Pierce, or counter the end-step exile to 'keep it blanked forever'. Warp is an alternative cost ('You may cast this card from your hand for its warp cost', CR 702.185a), not an ability on the stack, so Tidebinder cannot touch the cast; and Spell Pierce reads 'Counter target noncreature spell', so it cannot touch a Harmonizer spell either. The counterspells that work on the spell are We Say Thee Nay! and Flashfreeze. The exile IS a delayed triggered ability and is technically counterable, but never do it: you hand them a Harmonizer that stays, and because the trigger's source was the spell rather than the permanent, it is doubtful anything gets blanked at all. Also, blanking Harmonizer does NOT stop the exile: the delayed trigger already exists independently of the creature's text (CR 603.7a, 603.7c).
Esper Origins (front face) and its Flashback: Tidebinder the 'Surveil 2. You gain 2 life...' spell or the flashback. Both are casting a spell, not abilities. Spell Snare answers it (mana value 2 even when cast from the graveyard), as does Flashfreeze. Only the back face's chapter abilities are Tidebinder targets.
Mossborn Hydra: Counter 'This creature enters with a +1/+1 counter on it' so it dies as a 0/0. That is a replacement effect, not a trigger. It always enters as a 1/1. Hit the first landfall doubling instead.
Sapling Nursery / Earthbender Ascension landfall triggers: Counter the token or quest-counter trigger to blank the enchantment. Both sources are enchantments; Tidebinder's blank only applies to an artifact, creature or planeswalker (ruling 3). You counter exactly one token or one counter and the enchantment keeps working. Nursery's 'Affinity for Forests' is a static cost reducer and untouchable.
Sapling Nursery indestructible activation: Save Tidebinder to counter '{1}{G}, Exile this enchantment: Treefolk and Forests you control gain indestructible until end of turn' in response to Day of Black Sun. Unnecessary: Day of Black Sun says 'Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures.' Its later-timestamped ability loss strips the granted indestructible before the destroy, so the Treefolk (and any earthbended Forests, mana value 0) die regardless. Countering the activation only matters against your single-target removal, and the Nursery is already exiled as a cost either way.
Surrak, Elusive Hunter / Gigantic Big Bear ('This spell can't be countered'): Tidebinder is turned off by 'can't be countered'. That clause protects the spell, not abilities. Surrak's draw trigger is a legal target. Big Bear simply has no activated or triggered abilities; nothing to hit, and Wasp cannot target it (hexproof).
Surrak, Elusive Hunter: Tidebinder on a Harmonizer trigger gives them a Surrak draw. Surrak needs 'a creature you control or a creature spell you control' to become the target. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, so Surrak does not trigger. Wasp, Drowner, Kaito's -2 and Bitter Triumph all do trigger him.
Snakeskin Veil / Warg Tactics (hexproof): They can Veil in response to protect Harmonizer from Tidebinder. Tidebinder targets neither the creature nor its controller; hexproof on the creature is irrelevant, and the 'loses all abilities' part is not targeted. Veil only beats Wasp and Drowner.
Torpor Orb (sideboard, 2): Counter the Orb's effect, or Flashfreeze the Orb. 'Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger' is static, and the Orb is a colorless artifact spell so Flashfreeze ('Counter target red or green spell') cannot touch it. With Orb on the battlefield Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner, Siren, Beavers and Wan Shi Tong (his X counters and draw) have no ETB; Wan Shi Tong's search trigger and Enduring Curiosity's draw still work. Their side is untouched (Hydra's counter is a replacement effect, Ascension and Nursery are enchantments, Worldwagon is not a creature when it enters). Answer the spell with Annul, Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay!; once it resolves, Tidebinder and Wasp are only flash bodies.
Earthbended land ('When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped'): Counter the return trigger to blank the land. The return is a delayed triggered ability created by the earthbend effect (CR 701.66a-b), and its source is whatever earthbended the land: Earthbender Ascension's ETB or Ba Sing Se's activation (CR 603.7d/603.7e). Neither is an artifact, creature or planeswalker, so Tidebinder counters the return but blanks nothing. If the land died it stays in the graveyard, where Icetill Explorer can replay it anyway; if it was EXILED it stays in exile, which is the one case this is a real land-kill (Explorer plays lands from the graveyard only).
Shared Roots: Counter the land search. It is a sorcery; Tidebinder cannot counter spells. Spell Snare (mana value 2), Flashfreeze or Spell Pierce.
Wasp in response to a landfall trigger: Flashing Wasp on Harmonizer while its doubling trigger is on the stack counters the doubling. An ability on the stack exists independently of its source (CR 113.7a). Blanking Harmonizer only stops FUTURE triggers; the one on the stack resolves. Only Tidebinder (or killing the doubling's target so it fizzles) stops the current one.
Not worth it
Sazh's Chocobo landfall: freezing a 1-drop is poor value when Shoot the Sheriff, Requiting Hex (mana value 1) or Drowner just remove it; save Tidebinder for the engine.Sapling Nursery landfall: you deny one 3/4 Treefolk and the enchantment keeps making more.Earthbender Ascension ETB ('earthbend 2. Then search your library for a basic land...'): denies a land and a 2/2 haste land, but Ascension is an enchantment (nothing blanked) and spending your one Tidebinder on turn-3 ramp while Harmonizer is still in their deck is how you lose the turn-5 landfall stack. Fire only when you hold two.Earthbender Ascension quest-counter trigger and its reflexive 'When you do': one +1/+1 counter and trample, enchantment keeps working.Keen-Eyed Curator '{1}: Exile target card from a graveyard': countering one activation does blank the 1-of and switch off the +4/+4 static, but Shoot the Sheriff (Raccoon Scout is not an outlaw) or Requiting Hex (mana value 2) kills it for less; only if it is about to turn on and you have nothing else.Ba Sing Se '{2}{G}, {T}: Earthbend 2': they lose 3 mana and a land tap, but a land is not blanked and Tidebinder is worth more later.Escape Tunnel's unblockable activation: ignore it unless Harmonizer is on the battlefield and the 2-power target is about to be doubled (see target 3).Demolition Field '{2}, {T}, Sacrifice this land: Destroy target nonbasic land an opponent controls. That land's controller may search their library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield, then shuffle. You may search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield, then shuffle.': note it also gives THEM an untapped basic (a landfall trigger for every payoff) and a search (Wan Shi Tong draws). Countering the activation denies both, but it is a 1-of; fire only if the landfall would be lethal or Restless Reef / Soulstone Sanctuary is your win condition.Surrak's draw trigger pre-board: a 1-of that Bitter Triumph handles; it becomes a real target only post-board with two copies and a removal-heavy hand (target 8).Summon: Esper Maduin chapters I and II: a card and two mana; wait for III.The warp end-step exile trigger: never counter it, you would be gifting them a permanent Harmonizer.Llanowar Elves: nothing to target (mana ability). Wasp's job if anything.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp is a reliable blank for the two engine creatures (hard-cast Harmonizer, Explorer) and a tempo tap for a lethal Chocobo, but it is the card their sideboard is aimed at: Snakeskin Veil and Warg Tactics fizzle its trigger (hexproof), Warg Tactics' first mode 'Destroy target creature with flying' kills it outright, Surrak draws them a card the moment Wasp targets anything, 3/4 reach Treefolk block and kill it (Explorer, with no reach, cannot block a flyer), Meltstrider's Resolve fights it, and Torpor Orb switches off its ETB. Fire it at an engine creature at THEIR END STEP on the turn they tap out to cast it (no green mana for Veil, land drop already done), not in their upkeep when every Forest is untapped; hold it for beginning-of-combat only when a single tapped attacker is the whole problem.
#1Mightform Harmonizer (hard-cast)
WhyBlank it and no land this turn or any later turn doubles anything while Wasp lives. Tapping is irrelevant; the blank is the whole point.
WhenTHEIR END STEP on the turn they hard-cast it (they just spent {2}{G}{G}, usually tapped out, so no Snakeskin Veil / Warg Tactics), or on your own turn while their lands are still tapped. Fall back to their upkeep only if you had no mana at end step, and then only if they are short of {G}. Do not wait for the landfall trigger: Wasp in response to a trigger on the stack does not stop it (CR 113.7a). Against a WARPED Harmonizer Wasp is nearly useless: it is exiled at end step anyway and they play their land right after it resolves, before you ever get priority.
AfterA 4/4 with no text; any doubling that already resolved stays until end of turn. Everything returns if Wasp dies, is bounced, or is ninjutsu'd back to hand for Kaito.
#2Icetill Explorer
WhyBlank removes the extra land drop and graveyard land plays for as long as Wasp lives, cutting their landfall count roughly in half. 2 mana for that is a fine trade, and Explorer (no flying, no reach) can never block Wasp.
WhenTheir end step the turn it is cast, or your turn, while they are tapped out. If you are late and they already played one land this turn, blanking Explorer in response to the first landfall stack still denies the second land drop that turn, because the permission is checked when they try to play it.
After2/4 with no text. Lands already played stay.
#3Sazh's Chocobo
WhyTwo jobs: tap it in their beginning of combat so it cannot attack this turn, and blank it so it stops growing. It keeps every +1/+1 counter it already has (counters are not abilities), so this is damage control, not a kill. Note a blanked Chocobo can still be the TARGET of Harmonizer's doubling off a mid-combat fetch; only the tap stops the attack.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, AFTER their main-phase land drops have resolved (the counters it would have gotten are already there and no more come) and BEFORE attackers are declared. Tapping an already-attacking Chocobo does nothing; a tapped attacker still deals damage. This is the play on the draw when their turn 3 arrives before you have Tidebinder mana.
AfterAn X/X+1 with no landfall trigger, tapped this turn only; it untaps normally next turn but stays frozen at that size while Wasp lives.
#4Mossborn Hydra (post-board)
WhyBlank freezes the counter count and removes trample. Same logic as Chocobo but the growth is exponential, so the earlier the better.
WhenTheir end step the turn it resolves (it already has 1 counter from the replacement effect) while they are tapped out. If it already doubled once or twice, tapping it before attackers is still fine tempo.
AfterKeeps its counters, loses trample and doubling.
#5Lumbering Worldwagon (once crewed)
WhyWhile crewed it is an artifact creature until end of turn, so Wasp can target it: it is tapped (no attack if you are before attackers), it loses the characteristic-defining power ability (0/4), the attack trigger and Crew 4, so it can never animate again while Wasp lives.
WhenTheir beginning of combat after the crew resolves and before attackers are declared. Before it is crewed it is not a creature and Wasp cannot target it.
AfterA 0/4 artifact with no text for as long as Wasp lives; lands it already fetched stay.
#6Summon: Esper Maduin (4/4 creature Saga)
WhyA blanked Saga loses its chapters, is not sacrificed (CR 714.4) and stops getting lore counters (CR 714.3c). This is a PAUSE, not a kill: while Wasp lives it is a plain 4/4; when Wasp leaves it resumes from its current lore count, so chapters II and III still happen later. It is exiled on Wasp leaving only if it already had 3 lore counters when blanked (the Tidebinder chapter-III line).
WhenTheir end step or your turn, before the precombat-main lore counter that would trigger chapter II or III.
After4/4 body, no chapters, frozen lore count, keeps its finality counter.
#7Treefolk token (3/4 reach) or Surrak, Elusive Hunter
WhyBlanking a Treefolk removes reach so Wasp, Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers and Wan Shi Tong fly past it into Enduring Curiosity draws; tapping it removes a blocker on your turn. Surrak: blank removes trample and the draw trigger, but targeting him with Wasp lets him draw one card first (his trigger resolves before Wasp's).
WhenYour own beginning of combat (remove the blocker) or their beginning of combat (remove an attacker).
AfterTreefolk: 3/4 with no reach. Surrak: 4/3 with no text.
#8Earthbended land (0/0 land creature with two +1/+1 counters and haste)
WhyBlanking it strips the Forest's mana ability and the haste the earthbend granted (Wasp's effect has the later timestamp), so they lose a land's mana and a hasty attacker while Wasp lives. It does NOT remove the 'return it to the battlefield tapped' clause: that is a delayed trigger owned by the earthbend effect (CR 701.66a-b), not an ability of the land, so the land still comes back tapped if it dies or is exiled.
WhenAfter the earthbend resolves (Ba Sing Se's activation is sorcery-speed; Ascension's earthbend is an ETB trigger that resolves in their main phase after the enchantment enters) and before attackers.
AfterA 2/2 land creature with no mana ability and no haste; keeps its two +1/+1 counters, stays a land, and still returns tapped if it dies or is exiled.
#9Llanowar Elves / Keen-Eyed Curator (only when nothing above is in sight)
WhyElves: tap-and-blank in their turn-2 upkeep delays a warp or four-drop by a turn, but it is one of four one-drops in a deck with 14 Forests and 8 fetches, so the blank is worth little. Curator: blank removes the +4/+4 static (7/7 becomes 3/3) and the graveyard-exile ability, but Shoot the Sheriff or Requiting Hex kill the 1-of outright.
WhenElves: their upkeep on turn 2 (on the play). Curator: any time; beginning of combat if it is the attacker.
AfterElves: a 1/1 with no mana ability while Wasp lives. Curator: 3/3 with no text; cards already exiled stay exiled but no longer count.
Traps
Gigantic Big Bear: Wasp it to blank hexproof/haste. It has hexproof; Wasp cannot target it at all. Nothing in either card interacts with the Bear; you need blockers or Day of Black Sun (X=7).
Snakeskin Veil / Warg Tactics mode 2: Wasp the Harmonizer into open green mana. 'It gains hexproof until end of turn' in response makes Wasp's trigger fizzle for having no legal target; you keep a 2/1 flyer and nothing is blanked. Two Veil and one Warg Tactics in the 75, mostly post-board. Warg Tactics mode 1 'Destroy target creature with flying' also kills Wasp itself, which ends the blank. This is why the window is their end step when they are tapped out.
Surrak, Elusive Hunter: Wasp's target is free. 'Whenever a creature you control ... becomes the target of ... an ability an opponent controls, draw a card' triggers off Wasp (and Drowner, Kaito -2, Bitter Triumph). They draw before Wasp's trigger resolves.
Any attacking creature: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to tap the attacker out of combat. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat; it still deals damage. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step or earlier.
Mightform Harmonizer with a landfall trigger on the stack: Wasp counters the doubling. The trigger exists independently of Harmonizer (CR 113.7a). Only Tidebinder counters it.
Kaito ninjutsu returning Wasp: Ninjutsu Kaito in off an unblocked Wasp for free. Returning Wasp to hand ends 'for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield': the Harmonizer or Explorer gets all its text back at once. Do it only if you plan to re-flash Wasp the same turn or the blank no longer matters.
Torpor Orb on the battlefield: Wasp still taps something. 'Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger': Wasp's Sting never triggers. It is a 2/1 flash flyer and nothing else.
Earthbended land: Wasp stops it from returning when it dies. The return is a delayed trigger created by the earthbend effect (CR 701.66b), not an ability the land has; 'loses all abilities' cannot remove it. Wasp takes its mana and haste, nothing more.
Wasp blank plus your own Day of Black Sun: Sweep their board with X=4 while Wasp holds Harmonizer blank. Day of Black Sun kills every creature with mana value X or less on BOTH sides: Wasp (2), Tidebinder (3), Drowner, Siren, Beavers, Wan Shi Tong (X counts as 0 on the battlefield, mana value 2) and Enduring Curiosity (4), and it strips their abilities first, so Curiosity's 'When Enduring Curiosity dies ... return it' never triggers (CR 603.10a, leaves-the-battlefield abilities look back at a creature that had no abilities). The blank ending does not matter because the blanked creature dies too, but count your own losses before casting.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Landfall stack with Harmonizer + Chocobo: Tidebinder counters Harmonizer's trigger (blanks it if hard-cast), then Kaito's -2 ('Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it') or Floodpits Drowner's ETB stuns the now-frozen Chocobo. Cheaper alternative when Tidebinder is not in hand: Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff the creature Harmonizer's trigger targets; with no legal target the doubling fizzles, but Harmonizer stays live for the next land.
  • The mid-combat fetch: they attack with Chocobo, you declare blocks, they crack Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel. Respond to the ACTIVATION with Tidebinder: no land enters, so no doubling, no Chocobo counter, no Treefolk, and they are down a land. If instead a basic Forest was their precombat land drop, you never get a window before the triggers, and you counter Harmonizer's trigger (hard-cast) or chump the attacker (warped).
  • Protect the blank. After Tidebinder or Wasp blanks Harmonizer or Explorer, hold Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! / Annul for Meltstrider's Resolve (an Aura spell, mana value 1) and do not attack Wasp into an untapped 3/4 reach Treefolk. Better still, convert: Shoot the Sheriff (Insect Druid / Insect Scout are not outlaws) or Bitter Triumph the blanked creature at leisure, then the 3/2 or 2/1 is free to attack or be ninjutsu'd away.
  • Kaito and Day of Black Sun do not mix. Day of Black Sun is a sorcery, so it is cast on your turn, and Kaito is a 3/4 creature during your turn while he has loyalty (mana value 4): X=4 destroys your own Kaito (losing abilities in layer 6 does not undo the layer-4 type change that already made him a creature, CR 613). X=4 also kills your Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner, Siren, Beavers, Wan Shi Tong (mana value 2 on the battlefield) and Enduring Curiosity, and Curiosity does NOT come back, because it lost all abilities before it died (CR 603.10a). Their side: X=4 kills Chocobo (1), Harmonizer (4, even if warped), Explorer (4), Curator (2), Hydra (3), Surrak (3), Elves (1), Treefolk and earthbended lands (0), and strips any indestructible Sapling Nursery granted in response. Cast it with Kaito in hand or at X=3 (misses Harmonizer, Explorer and Kaito) when Kaito must live; treat it as a reset, and rebuild with Tidebinder and Wasp afterwards.
  • Two-Tidebinder line (post-board only, 3 copies): blank Explorer first via the mill trigger, then a later Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel activation countered by the second Tidebinder is a true Stone Rain, because they can no longer replay the fetch from the graveyard. It holds only while the first 3/2 survives. With one Tidebinder, pick one or the other.
  • Wan Shi Tong ('Whenever an opponent searches their library, put a +1/+1 counter on Wan Shi Tong and draw a card') feeds on every fetch crack, Shared Roots, Ascension ETB, Demolition Field and Worldwagon trigger you let resolve; countering a fetch activation costs you that draw. Flash him in at their end step when the Tidebinder hold turned out to be unnecessary.
  • Post-board Torpor Orb: keep Annul / Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! for the Orb on the stack (Flashfreeze cannot, the Orb is colorless). If it resolves, stop holding Tidebinder and Wasp as answers; run them out as 3/2 and 2/1 flash bodies at end of turn and lean on Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Requiting Hex and Day of Black Sun (Drowner's ETB and Wan Shi Tong's ETB are off too; his search trigger still works).
  • Wasp blanks one Treefolk's reach or taps it in your beginning of combat so Wasp, Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers and Wan Shi Tong connect for Enduring Curiosity draws ('Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card').
  • Counterspell map for their spells (none of which Tidebinder can touch): Spell Snare hits Esper Origins (mana value 2 even flashed back), Shared Roots, Curator, Torpor Orb, Warg Tactics; Flashfreeze hits any green spell except Surrak and Gigantic Big Bear ('This spell can't be countered'), including a warped or hard-cast Harmonizer; Spell Pierce hits only noncreature spells (Orb, Ascension, Nursery, Resolve, Shared Roots, Origins, Veil, Warg Tactics), never Harmonizer; We Say Thee Nay! hits anything counterable. Countering the Origins flashback means the Saga never enters, so no chapter III to Tidebinder later.
  • Do not ninjutsu Kaito by returning Wasp or Tidebinder while their blank is holding a Harmonizer or Explorer off; return Siren, Beavers or Drowner instead.
Hold or fire
Hold {2}{U} on their turn when Icetill Explorer, a hard-cast Harmonizer or a Hydra is on their battlefield, or when they are on four or more lands with a fetch in play or Explorer likely (their turn 4 onward). Holding is cheap because your deck is full of flash: if their turn passes with nothing worth countering, the same mana becomes Floodpits Drowner, Enduring Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong at their end step, and Kaito arrives by ninjutsu on your own turn for 3 rather than a hard-cast 4. Keep the mana up THROUGH THEIR COMBAT, not just their main phase: the fetch cracked after blockers is where the doubling lands. Fire Tidebinder on: a Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel activation whenever Harmonizer was warped this turn or two or more payoffs are triggering; Harmonizer's own trigger when it was hard-cast (the blank lasts); Explorer's mill trigger the first time a land enters if neither of those is live; Hydra's first doubling post-board. Never run Tidebinder out as a plain 3/2 while Explorer, hard-cast Harmonizer or Hydra is on their side; a 3/2 does nothing against 3/4 reach Treefolk and a growing Chocobo. On the play you are a turn ahead: their turn 3 (Elves + two Forests) is the first turn a warp plus Passage crack can make a 10-power Chocobo, so hold on their turn 3 only when Elves and Chocobo are both down, with Drowner at their end step as the fallback. On the draw their turn 3 comes while you have two lands, so Tidebinder is impossible: answer that turn with Wasp or Drowner on Chocobo in their beginning of combat (before the fetch crack) or Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff, then hold from their turn 4. Wasp: fire at THEIR END STEP the turn they tap out for a hard-cast Harmonizer or Explorer (land drop done, no green for Veil), or in their beginning of combat at the one attacker that is lethal; never fire it in response to a landfall trigger expecting to stop that trigger, and never into open green mana once you have seen Snakeskin Veil or Warg Tactics. Once a blank is on, protect the body (counter Meltstrider's Resolve, do not attack into Treefolk) and convert it into a kill with Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph when you can. Post-board, if Torpor Orb resolves, flip the rule: both cards become flash bodies, deploy them at end of turn and stop holding.
How many to run
Tidebinder: bring in both sideboard copies for 3 post-board, and this deck's 18.4% share is the strongest argument for a second main-deck copy. Cut a Spell Snare for it (Snare's targets here are Esper Origins, Shared Roots, Curator, Torpor Orb, Warg Tactics, all replaceable), NEVER Shoot the Sheriff: 'Destroy target non-outlaw creature' kills Harmonizer, Explorer, Chocobo, Hydra, Curator and Surrak at instant speed for 2, and a dead engine beats a blank that ends when a 3/2 dies to a Resolve fight or a Treefolk block. Every one of their engine cards (Harmonizer, Explorer, Hydra, Worldwagon, the creature Saga) is a creature or artifact with a triggered ability, their protection (Veil, Warg Tactics, Surrak) does nothing against Tidebinder, and it still answers fetch activations mid-combat when the board is wide. Wasp: keep 2, do not add; it is good against a hard-cast Harmonizer/Explorer at their end step but blanks into Veil/Warg Tactics, dies to Warg Tactics mode 1, Resolve fights and 3/4 reach Treefolk, draws Surrak a card, and cannot preempt a warp turn. Sideboard plan: Flashfreeze x2 (Ascension, Nursery, Harmonizer, Explorer, Hydra, Shared Roots, Origins; not Surrak or Big Bear), Annul and Spell Pierce x2 (Torpor Orb, Ascension, Nursery, Resolve), Day of Black Sun x2 with the Kaito caution above. If they show Torpor Orb, the third Tidebinder is still fine as a body but stop adding more.
Review trail: confidence high, 3 disputed, 17 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 2 (Day of Black Sun line): 'Curiosity comes back as an enchantment.' Rejected. Day of Black Sun reads 'Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures.' Enduring Curiosity's 'When Enduring Curiosity dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield' is a leaves-the-battlefield trigger; CR 603.10a makes it look back at the game state immediately before the event, when Curiosity had no abilities, so it never triggers. The fatal core of that finding (X=4 kills our own Kaito) is accepted; only the Curiosity sub-claim is corrected.
  • Reviewer 2 (hold timing): 'A turn-3 warp yields exactly one doubling on a Chocobo with at most 2 counters, nowhere near lethal.' Partly rejected. With Fabled Passage or Escape Tunnel as the turn-3 land (both '{T}, Sacrifice this land: Search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped'), a turn-3 warp gives TWO land entries and two doublings: Chocobo with 1 counter becomes 2/3 then 4/3 off the Passage, then 5/4 then 10/4 off the fetched basic. Not lethal, but a 10-power attack on their turn 3. Accepted conclusion: the hold starts at their turn 4 (on the draw you cannot Tidebinder their turn 3 anyway), with Wasp/Drowner on Chocobo in beginning of combat as the turn-3 answer, and on the play hold their turn 3 only when Elves and Chocobo are both down.
  • Reviewer 1 (warp trap): the suggestion that countering the warp exile trigger 'most likely keeps its landfall trigger immediately (no blank at all)' is stated as probable rather than certain. Neither reviewer nor I can point to an oracle ruling on whether Tidebinder blanks a permanent when a delayed trigger's source was the spell; the final text says it is doubtful and leans on nothing, since the practical advice ('never counter it') is the same either way.
Changes after review
  • FATAL fixed: Day of Black Sun combined line now warns that X=4 destroys our own Kaito (a 3/4 creature with mana value 4 during our turn, CR 613 layer order keeps him a creature) and our whole flash board, and that Enduring Curiosity does NOT return (CR 603.10a). Added the X=3 / Kaito-in-hand alternative and a matching Wasp trap.
  • Earthbended land: corrected both entries.
