The return clause is a delayed triggered ability created by the earthbend effect (CR 701.66a-b) whose source is Ascension's ETB or Ba Sing Se's activation (CR 603.7d/e), so Wasp's blank does not remove it (land still returns tapped) and Tidebinder countering it blanks nothing because the source is an enchantment or a land, not because the land is 'a card in a graveyard'. Added the exile case as the one real land-kill. Removed the earlier changes_made claim that the clause 'belongs to the earthbended land'.
  • Removed the false claim that Icetill Explorer 'blocks and kills Wasp': Explorer has no flying or reach and cannot block a flyer.
  • Summon: Esper Maduin under Wasp rewritten as a pause, not a kill: a blanked Saga gets no lore counters (CR 714.3c) so it never reaches chapter III while blanked, and resumes when Wasp leaves; it is exiled on the blank ending only in the Tidebinder chapter-III line where it already has 3 counters. Rule citation corrected from 714.3b to 714.3c in both entries.
  • Removed Spell Pierce as an answer to Mightform Harmonizer everywhere (creature spell; Pierce is 'Counter target noncreature spell'); replaced 'casting permission' with 'alternative cost' for warp (CR 702.185a). Added a full counterspell map line so Pierce/Snare/Flashfreeze/Nay are each matched to the right spells.
  • Warp exile trap and Harmonizer target rewritten: blanking does not stop the exile because the delayed trigger already exists independently of the creature's text (CR 603.7a, 603.7c); dropped the 603.7d citation and the promise that countering the exile produces a lasting blank.
  • Torpor Orb: removed Flashfreeze as an answer (colorless artifact spell), added Wan Shi Tong's ETB to what the Orb turns off and noted his search trigger and Curiosity's draw still work.
  • Ascension earthbend timing corrected from 'on cast' to 'ETB trigger that resolves after the enchantment enters'.
  • Added the deck's real kill window to how_they_win, target 3 and hold_or_fire: fetchland cracked AFTER blockers so the doubling lands on an unblocked attacker; counter the activation. Added the hard-cast vs warped Harmonizer distinction (blank lasts vs one-turn stop) to the summary, target 1 and target 3, with the rule for choosing fetch-counter vs Harmonizer-trigger.
  • hold_or_fire rewritten: hold from their turn 4 (or when Explorer / hard-cast Harmonizer / Hydra is down), flash fallbacks (Drowner, Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong) make holding cheap, Kaito via ninjutsu, keep mana through combat; on-the-draw turn 3 handled by Wasp/Drowner on Chocobo. Corrected the 'turn-2 warp' wording (turn-1 Elves enables a turn-2 warp with no landfall behind it; turn 3 is the first real warp turn).
  • Wasp timing for Harmonizer/Explorer/Hydra moved from their upkeep to THEIR END STEP the turn they tap out (no green mana for Veil / Warg Tactics).
  • Added 'protect the blank': Meltstrider's Resolve reframed as the card that ends the blank (Tidebinder target 6, new combined line), plus convert-to-kill advice with Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph.
  • counts_recommendation: second main Tidebinder over Spell Snare, never over Shoot the Sheriff (kills Harmonizer, Explorer, Chocobo, Hydra, Curator, Surrak; none are outlaws).
  • Two-Tidebinder Stone Rain line downgraded to an explicit post-board, two-copy note.
  • Added Lumbering Worldwagon as a Wasp target once crewed (0/4, no crew, no attack trigger while Wasp lives); added Surrak's draw trigger as a conditional post-board Tidebinder target; added Escape Tunnel's unblockable mode as a conditional (Harmonizer on board) inside target 3; expanded Demolition Field to its full oracle text (gives them an untapped basic and a search).
  • Moved Keen-Eyed Curator and Earthbender Ascension ETB from Tidebinder's numbered targets to dont_bother with caveats; merged Llanowar Elves and Curator into one low-priority Wasp entry.
  • Re-verified against the deck file: all card counts (main and side), type lines, P/T and oracle text for every card named, plus our own Kaito, Day of Black Sun, Spell Pierce, Flashfreeze, Annul, We Say Thee Nay!, Shoot the Sheriff, Wan Shi Tong, Enduring Curiosity, Floodpits Drowner, Requiting Hex, Spell Snare, Bitter Triumph, Dream Beavers and Spyglass Siren from opponents.json.
vs. Izzet Spellementals12.2% TB: goodWasp: situational▼
Turns 1-3 they cycle cheap cantrips and burn (Opt, Sleight of Hand, Prismari Charm, Burst Lightning, Traumatic Critique) to stock the graveyard, which makes their Elementals nearly free: Eddymurk Crab ("This spell costs {1} less to cast for each instant and sorcery card in your graveyard", Flash, 5/5, ETB taps up to two creatures) and Hearth Elemental ("costs {X} less... where X is the number of cards in your graveyard that are instant cards, sorcery cards, and/or have an Adventure", 4/5). Once any Elemental is on their battlefield, Sunderflock ({7}{U}{U}) costs "{X} less... where X is the greatest mana value among Elementals you control": {U}{U} with a Crab (MV 7) or another Sunderflock out, {1}{U}{U} with Hearth (MV 6), and post-board {4}{U}{U} off a Stallion (MV 3) or {5}{U}{U} off Hydro-Man as a creature (MV 2). Its ETB "return all non-Elemental creatures to their owners' hands" is one-sided because all their creatures are Elementals, and it is the only flyer in their 75, so it is also the one body that blocks our 1/1 fliers and shuts off the Enduring Curiosity engine. The realistic Sunderflock window is their turn 5-6 (turn 4 only on a perfect cantrip start), with the counters (Get Out, Spell Pierce, Spell Snare) protecting the tempo. Tidebinder and Wasp cannot touch the 28-spell half of the deck; their whole value is the two ETB creatures, plus Stallion and Hydro-Man post-board.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Eight main-deck targets (4 Sunderflock + 4 Eddymurk Crab ETBs) and every one is a real swing. The Sunderflock ETB is the trigger that matters: countering it keeps our board and, more importantly, leaves them a 5/5 with no Flying, so our fliers keep connecting for Curiosity draws. Post-board Stallion adds two more targets (its Ward {1} trigger and its Opus trigger). Not premium because ~45 cards of their 75 (all 28 spells, the lands, Hearth Elemental) give Tidebinder nothing, Get Out counters the Tidebinder spell, Burst Lightning/Sear/Traumatic Critique kill the 3/2 in response to its trigger (bounce still countered, but Sunderflock keeps Flying), and Prismari Charm mode 3 bounces Tidebinder to end any blank at instant speed. Tidebinder does survive Charm's 1 damage, which Wasp, Siren, Beavers and Drowner do not.
#1Sunderflock
"When this creature enters, if you cast it, return all non-Elemental creatures to their owners' hands."
WhyTwo prizes in one counter. First, Sunderflock (a creature as the trigger resolves) loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives, and Flying is the ability that decides the matchup: it is the only creature in their 75 that can block or race Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren, Wan Shi Tong and Wasp, so a grounded 5/5 means our fliers connect every turn and Enduring Curiosity keeps drawing. Second, we skip the tempo hit of the bounce: all their creatures are Elementals, so the bounce would return Curiosity (to hand, not dies, so no enchantment rebuy), Wan Shi Tong (loses every +1/+1 counter), Beavers, Siren, Drowner, Wasp and Tidebinder, ending any Wasp/Tidebinder blank and removing Drowner's shuffle option (stun counters already placed stay). The bounce is survivable if you cannot stop it: Siren, Beavers and Drowner rebuy their ETBs on recast, Curiosity has flash and comes back at their end step, Kaito is a planeswalker on their turn and is never bounced, and an animated Soulstone Sanctuary has all creature types (it is an Elemental) and stays. Do not warp the game around never being bounced; do hold Tidebinder for the flyer. Note the trigger goes on the stack even when we control no non-Elemental creature (intervening 'if you cast it' is the only condition), so an empty-board Sunderflock is still a Tidebinder target purely to strip Flying, and that is usually still correct.
WhenTheir main phase only (no flash on Sunderflock), realistically their turn 5-6, turn 4 only after a fast cantrip start put a Crab/Hearth out on turn 3. Sunderflock is live the moment ANY Elemental is on their battlefield (see hold_or_fire for the cost table). Either counter the spell with We Say Thee Nay! or let it resolve; when it enters, the ETB goes on the stack. With that trigger on the stack and priority to us, flash Tidebinder targeting the trigger; Tidebinder's own ETB resolves first and counters the bounce. On the draw that means having {2}{U} up on their turn from your turn 4 onward once an Elemental is out; on the play you usually have one more development turn. Expect a good Izzet pilot to refuse to cast Sunderflock into three open blue mana and to flash Crabs at your end step instead; holding still delays the flyer a turn, but do not skip your own development for more than one turn waiting for it.
AfterLoses: Flying, the cost-reduction static (irrelevant on the battlefield) and the ETB. Keeps: 5/5 body, any counters. It still needs answering: Floodpits Drowner stun then '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries', Kaito -2 (two stun counters), Shoot the Sheriff (Elemental is not an outlaw), or Bitter Triumph. A blanked Sunderflock is still an Elemental with mana value 9, so their next Sunderflock costs {U}{U}; its bounce would return Tidebinder and restore the first one's Flying, so keep a second Tidebinder or a teamwork Nay for copy number two.
Risk(a) Burst Lightning (2, or 4 kicked), Sear (4) or Traumatic Critique (X>=2) on Tidebinder in response to its trigger: the bounce is still countered (ruling 1) but Sunderflock keeps Flying. Impractical Joke is a sorcery and cannot do this; it kills Tidebinder later on their main phase, which ends the blank. (b) Prismari Charm mode 3 ('Return target nonland permanent to its owner's hand') on Tidebinder at any later point is the cheapest and most common way they get Flying back; Charm's 1 damage does not kill the 3/2. (c) Get Out mode 1 ('Counter target creature or enchantment spell') on the Tidebinder spell lets the bounce happen; Spell Snare (MV 2) or Nay answers Get Out. (d) Get Out mode 2 or Charm mode 3 on their own Sunderflock in response to Tidebinder's trigger: the bounce is still countered, but the source is gone so nothing loses abilities (ruling 3), and they recast it next turn with a fresh bounce, for {U}{U} if another Crab/Sunderflock remains, {1}{U}{U} with only Hearth, or the full nine if it was their only Elemental. Spell Snare covers Get Out here too.
#2Eddymurk Crab
"When this creature enters, tap up to two target creatures."
WhyThe Crab's whole non-body value is the two taps, and they pick the window. The most dangerous one is OUR end step: they flash it in, tap our two best blockers, and those creatures stay tapped through their entire turn (we do not untap until our own untap step) while the Crab, which entered tapped because it was not their turn, untaps in their untap step and attacks. Countering the trigger keeps our blockers up for their turn. Second window: our beginning of combat, tapping Curiosity plus a ground attacker to shut off the combat-damage draws. Third: their own precombat main, tapping our blockers before a 5/5 swing. Countering leaves a vanilla 5/5 and our creatures untapped. Kaito cannot be a Crab target on our turn (hexproof creature during our turn while he has loyalty).
WhenWhenever they flash it with real targets: the ETB goes on the stack with its targets chosen; respond with Tidebinder targeting the trigger. Judge an end-step Crab by whether the tapped creatures were needed as blockers on their turn, not by whether we needed them untapped on ours. A Crab cast on our turn 'enters tapped if it's not your turn' regardless; Tidebinder does not change that. An end-of-turn Crab with zero targets chosen (they may pick none) puts a trigger on the stack that does nothing; countering it blanks nothing that matters, so let it go.
AfterLoses: nothing that still matters (Flash, the cost reduction and the enters-tapped replacement only work on the stack or on entry). Keeps: 5/5 body, Elemental type and mana value 7, so a blanked Crab still makes Sunderflock cost {U}{U}. The win is the untapped creatures, not the blank.
RiskThe Crab itself is the Elemental that turns Sunderflock on: the moment it resolves, whether or not you counter its ETB, Sunderflock costs {U}{U} on their next main phase. So firing Tidebinder at a Crab ETB always hands them a live Sunderflock with your answer already spent. Fire only if you hold a second Tidebinder or a teamwork We Say Thee Nay! for Sunderflock, or if the taps cost you lethal-relevant damage or a game-deciding Curiosity draw.
#3Colorstorm Stallion (sideboard, 4)
"Ward {1}" (triggered: whenever Stallion becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays {1})
WhyThe Stallion blank on our terms, at a window we control. Any time our Floodpits Drowner ETB, Kaito -2, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph or Wasp targets the Stallion, its Ward {1} trigger goes on the stack. Tidebinder counters the ward trigger: our targeting effect resolves untaxed AND the Stallion (a creature as the trigger resolves) loses Ward, haste and Opus while Tidebinder lives, so no more +1/+1 per spell and no more token copies. This beats waiting for a 5-mana Opus trigger they can simply decline to cast into open mana.
WhenOur turn or theirs, immediately after our spell or ability targets Stallion and the ward trigger is put on the stack; Tidebinder targets the ward trigger, resolves first, counters it, then our original effect resolves. Prefer this once Sunderflock is already answered or off the table.
AfterLoses: Ward {1}, haste, Opus. Keeps: 3/3 body plus any pump already applied this turn (until end of turn). A copy token already created is a separate object with full abilities.
RiskCosts Tidebinder plus the removal spell in one window (5-6 mana with Drowner or Sheriff); their Burst Lightning/Sear on Tidebinder in response restores Ward and Opus but our targeting effect still resolves untaxed because the ward trigger is still countered. Simply paying the {1} is fine when Tidebinder is needed for Sunderflock.
#4Colorstorm Stallion (sideboard, 4)
"Opus, Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell, this creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn. If five or more mana was spent to cast that spell, create a token that's a copy of this creature."
WhyPost-board they bring 4 hasty 3/3s that grow with every spell and clone themselves off a 5-mana spell (Traumatic Critique X=3, kicked Burst Lightning, or a full-cost {4}{U} harmonize of Winternight Stories). Countering one Opus trigger denies that pump or copy and blanks the Stallion (Ward, haste, Opus all gone) for as long as Tidebinder stays on the battlefield. Ward does not tax Tidebinder here: Tidebinder targets the trigger on the stack, not the Stallion.
WhenThe Opus trigger goes on the stack above the instant or sorcery they just cast; counter it while their spell is still on the stack (their spell still resolves). Best moment: a trigger from a spell they spent 5+ mana on, so you deny the token copy and blank the horse in one shot.
AfterLoses: Ward {1}, haste, Opus. Keeps: 3/3 body plus any +1/+1 from Opus triggers that already resolved this turn. A copy token already created stays with full abilities.
RiskDo not fire on a +1/+1 from a 1-mana cantrip while Sunderflock is uncast and you have no other answer; a blanked Stallion is still a 3/3 attacker and the blank ends when Tidebinder is burned or Charm-bounced.
#5Hydro-Man, Fluid Felon (sideboard, 1)
"At the beginning of your end step, untap Hydro-Man. Until your next turn, he becomes a land and gains '{T}: Add {U}.'"
WhyHe is a creature when this triggers on their turn (the previous land effect ended when their turn began), so countering it blanks him: he stays a 2/2 with no abilities, never becomes a mana source, and his blue-spell pump is gone. As a creature he is also an Elemental with mana value 2 (Sunderflock costs {5}{U}{U} off him alone) and is not bounced by their own Sunderflock. Low value: one sideboard copy.
WhenTheir end step, with the untap/become-a-land trigger on the stack. This is the only way to stop the land conversion once the end step has begun; Wasp cannot (see Wasp traps).
AfterLoses: both triggered abilities. Keeps: 2/2 body; he stays a creature on our turn, so Wasp, Drowner, Shoot the Sheriff (Villain is not an outlaw) and Requiting Hex (MV 2) can all hit him.
RiskOnly fire if Tidebinder has no better job in the game and you want the 3/2 body now.
Traps
Hearth Elemental: Hold Tidebinder to counter Hearth Elemental or its 'Discard your hand, then draw two cards' adventure. Hearth Elemental has no activated or triggered ability at all. Its cost reduction is static, the adventure (Stoke Genius) is a spell being cast, and 'You may cast the creature later from exile' is casting permission. Tidebinder does nothing here. Spell Snare does: the adventure half is a mana value 2 spell on the stack, and countering it sends the card to the graveyard, not exile, so they lose the 4/5 too. We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce and Flashfreeze (it is red) also counter Stoke Genius.
Eddymurk Crab / Sunderflock / Hearth Elemental: Tidebinder can stop the cost reduction so Sunderflock costs 9 again. 'This spell costs {X} less to cast' is a static ability that applies while the spell is being cast; it is never on the stack. Worse, a Tidebinder-blanked Crab or Sunderflock is still an Elemental with mana value 7 or 9 (losing abilities changes neither types nor mana value), so it still reduces the next Sunderflock to {U}{U}.
Eddymurk Crab: Counter the 'enters tapped if it's not your turn' so the Crab comes in on our terms. That is a replacement effect, not a triggered ability; it has already applied by the time the Crab is on the battlefield. Tidebinder can only hit the 'tap up to two target creatures' trigger.
Eddymurk Crab: Counter the Crab ETB after we have already declared attackers. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat; it still deals damage. If they flash Crab after attackers are declared, its taps are nearly worthless and Tidebinder should be saved.
Winternight Stories: Counter the harmonize cast from the graveyard, or the 'tap a creature to reduce the cost' part. Harmonize is casting a spell from the graveyard; tapping a creature is part of paying its cost. Neither is an ability on the stack. Counter the spell with We Say Thee Nay! or Spell Pierce, not Tidebinder.
Burst Lightning: Counter the kicker so it only deals 2. Kicker is an additional cost paid while casting; the spell's effect checks whether it was kicked. Nothing to target.
Traumatic Critique / Prismari Charm / Get Out / Opt / Sleight of Hand: Counter the 'draw two, then discard' or a Charm mode with Tidebinder. These are spell effects, not abilities. 28 of their 61 main-deck cards are instants and sorceries and Tidebinder cannot interact with any of them; that is why Tidebinder is 'good' here and not 'premium'.
Riverpyre Verge / Steam Vents / Multiversal Passage: Deny mana by countering a land ability. The Verge's conditional tap is a mana ability (untargetable); 'As this land enters' clauses on Steam Vents and Multiversal Passage are replacement effects with nothing on the stack.
Colorstorm Stallion: Pay Ward {1} when Tidebinder counters the Opus trigger. Ward triggers only when the permanent itself becomes the target of a spell or ability. Tidebinder targets the Opus trigger on the stack, not the Stallion, so no ward trigger and no tax. (Wasp, Drowner, Kaito -2 and Sheriff target the Stallion and do get taxed; that ward trigger is itself a Tidebinder target, see targets.)
Sunderflock: Countering the ETB removes Sunderflock or bounces it back. Countering an ETB never removes the permanent. You still face a 5/5 (now without Flying); it needs Drowner, Kaito -2, Sheriff or Bitter Triumph.
Sunderflock (Tidebinder lock): Once Tidebinder has blanked a Sunderflock, it stays blanked for the game. The blank lasts only while Tidebinder remains on the battlefield: Burst Lightning, Sear, Traumatic Critique, a main-phase Impractical Joke or Prismari Charm mode 3 on Tidebinder restores Flying at once. Separately, Get Out mode 2 ('Return one or two target creatures... you own to your hand') or Charm mode 3 lets them pick up their own blanked Sunderflock and recast it; a recast is a new object with all abilities and a new 'if you cast it' bounce. Keep Spell Snare (hits Get Out and Charm, both MV 2) or the second Tidebinder.
Impractical Joke: Fear Joke as an instant-speed answer to Tidebinder's trigger. Impractical Joke is a sorcery ({R}); it can only be cast in their own main phase with an empty stack. In response to Tidebinder's trigger their live kills are Burst Lightning, Sear and Traumatic Critique (X>=2). Joke kills Tidebinder a turn later and ends the blank then.
Not worth it
Hearth Elemental: nothing to target at all; treat it as a vanilla 4/5 and answer it with Drowner, Sheriff or Bitter Triumph.Hydro-Man's 'Whenever you cast a blue spell, if Hydro-Man is a creature, he gets +1/+1' trigger: a counterable trigger that does blank him (he is a creature at that moment), but a one-of sideboard 2/2 growing by one is not worth the card that stops Sunderflock; the end-step land trigger is the better Hydro-Man window if you must.End-of-turn Eddymurk Crab with no targets chosen ('up to two'): the trigger still goes on the stack, but countering it blanks nothing that matters and the Crab still counts as a mana-value-7 Elemental for Sunderflock. Save Tidebinder.Crab ETBs that tap creatures you did not need as blockers on their turn or attackers on yours: let it resolve, keep Tidebinder for Sunderflock.Stallion Ward {1} when the {1} is easy and Sunderflock is still uncast: paying {1} is cheaper than the Sunderflock answer; use the ward-trigger line once the flyer is handled.
The Wondrous Wasp
Their creatures are big bodies whose abilities are spent once they are on the battlefield, so Wasp's blank does nothing to Crab or Hearth Elemental. Its ability-value is stripping Flying from Sunderflock (our fliers connect again, our ground bodies can block it) and, post-board, shutting off Stallion's Opus and haste through the Ward {1} tax. The tap half is narrower than it looks: Crab and Hearth have no flying or reach, so Beavers, Siren, Wan Shi Tong and Wasp already fly over them; tapping a 5/5 only matters to let a GROUND attacker through (Enduring Curiosity 4/3, Kaito 3/4 on our turn, Restless Reef 4/4, animated Soulstone Sanctuary 3/3) or to stop one 5/5 attack by resolving in their beginning of combat. Fragile: Prismari Charm's 1 damage kills Wasp (often with a Siren or Beaver as the second target) and releases whatever it blanked; Burst Lightning, Sear and Critique do the same; Spell Snare and Get Out counter the Wasp spell; Shore Up in response gives hexproof (Wasp's trigger fizzles) and its 'Untap it' undoes a resolved tap.
#1Sunderflock
WhyThe only main-deck creature where the blank matters: it loses Flying, so Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren, Wan Shi Tong and Wasp itself attack over it freely and Curiosity keeps drawing, while Curiosity, Beavers, Tidebinder or a Reef can block it on the ground. The tap half also stops one 5/5 flying attack if Wasp resolves in their beginning of combat.
WhenBlank value: any time after it is on the battlefield; if Tidebinder already countered its ETB it is already blanked, so save Wasp. Tap value: their beginning of combat (before attackers are declared) to stop the swing, or our precombat main to remove the blocker for a ground attacker. Never in response to its ETB trigger: the trigger on the stack resolves independently of its source, the bounce still happens, and Wasp itself is returned to hand.
AfterLoses: Flying and the (irrelevant) cost-reduction static. Keeps: 5/5 body, Elemental type, mana value 9 (still discounts their next Sunderflock). Effect ends the moment Wasp leaves; Prismari Charm's 1 damage, Burst Lightning, or a second Sunderflock bouncing Wasp restores Flying.
#2Colorstorm Stallion (sideboard)
WhyBlanking it turns off Opus (the +1/+1 per spell and the 5-mana token copy) and haste and Ward {1}, leaving a 3/3 that our removal then hits untaxed; tapping it in their beginning of combat stops a hasty attack. Costs {1}{U} plus the {1} ward tax, so budget 3 mana. Double-lock with Tidebinder: Wasp targets Stallion, the ward trigger goes on the stack, Tidebinder counters the ward trigger (Stallion loses all abilities), and Wasp's tap-and-blank then resolves untaxed; 5 mana total for two bodies and a dead horse.
WhenTheir beginning of combat if you need to stop the attack; otherwise their end step or our main phase for the blank. Pay the Ward {1} when it triggers, or Wasp's trigger is countered and Wasp is just a 2/1 flyer.
AfterLoses: Ward {1}, haste, Opus. Keeps: 3/3 body and any pump already applied this turn. A copy token made earlier is unaffected.
#3Eddymurk Crab / Hearth Elemental
WhyTap value only, and only for ground attackers: neither has flying or reach, so our fliers are unblocked whether the 5/5 is tapped or not, and Curiosity's draws off Beavers/Siren never depended on the tap. Tap the lone untapped 5/5 in our precombat when it lets Enduring Curiosity itself, Kaito (3/4 creature on our turn, and a 5/5 block would remove his loyalty), Restless Reef or an animated Sanctuary attack; or tap it in their beginning of combat to skip one 5-power attack. The blank removes nothing relevant (Flash, cost reduction, enters-tapped are all spent).
WhenOur precombat main or beginning of combat to remove a blocker; their beginning of combat, before attackers are declared, to prevent an attack. A Crab flashed in on our turn entered tapped anyway and is not a blocker that turn. Shore Up ('Untap it') undoes the tap for {U}, so do not stake the game on it.
AfterLoses: nothing that matters. Keeps: 5/5 or 4/5. Wasp does not undo taps the Crab's ETB already put on our creatures.
#4Hydro-Man, Fluid Felon (sideboard)
WhyBlanking him while he is a creature removes both triggers, so he never becomes a land or a mana source and stays a targetable 2/2 that Requiting Hex, Drowner or Sheriff can answer on any turn. Only a 1-of, so this is opportunistic.
WhenTheir turn, during their main phase or combat, BEFORE their end step begins. Once 'At the beginning of your end step' has triggered and is on the stack, blanking him does nothing to it: the trigger resolves, he becomes a land, and the '{T}: Add {U}' it grants is gained after Wasp's effect began, so he keeps it (ruling 2). At that point only Tidebinder countering the trigger helps. On our turn he is a land and not a creature, so Wasp has no legal target.
AfterLoses both triggers; stays a 2/2 creature every turn, so Requiting Hex (MV 2) now kills him on our turn.
Traps
Sunderflock: Flash Wasp in response to Sunderflock's ETB to blank it and stop the bounce. An ability already on the stack is independent of its source; removing the source's abilities does not counter it. The bounce resolves, returns Wasp (a Human, not an Elemental) to our hand, and the blank ends. Only Tidebinder (counter the trigger) or a counterspell on the Sunderflock spell stops the bounce.
Eddymurk Crab: Wasp in response to the Crab ETB so our creatures are not tapped. Same rule: the taps still resolve. Wasp's tap on the Crab is meaningless too since a Crab cast on our turn enters tapped anyway.
Eddymurk Crab / Hearth Elemental: Tap the 5/5 so our Beavers and Siren can attack for Curiosity draws. Crab and Hearth have neither flying nor reach; our 1/1 fliers are already unblockable by them. The tap only matters for a ground attacker (Curiosity, Kaito, Reef, Sanctuary). Spending 2 mana and a card to tap a blocker that could not block anyway is a wasted Wasp.
Any attacker: Tap an attacking Crab/Sunderflock after attackers are declared to stop the damage. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared.
Colorstorm Stallion: Wasp targets Stallion for 2 mana. Ward {1} triggers because Wasp's ability targets the permanent; pay {1} (or counter the ward trigger with Tidebinder) or the tap-and-blank is countered and you have a 2/1 flyer for 2.
Any creature (Shore Up in sideboard): The blank or the tap is a lock you can lean on as removal. Shore Up ('Target creature you control gets +1/+1 and gains hexproof until end of turn. Untap it.') in response makes Wasp's trigger fizzle; cast after Wasp resolved, it untaps the creature (the blank stays, and the hexproof/+1/+1 it grants are kept because they were gained after the blank began). Prismari Charm's 1 damage to two targets kills Wasp and a Siren/Beaver and restores the creature's abilities; Burst Lightning does the same. Plan for the lock to end; use Drowner/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph for actual removal.
Hydro-Man, Fluid Felon: Wasp him on our turn, or in response to his end-step trigger. On our turn he is a land and not a creature ('Until your next turn, he becomes a land'), so he is not a legal target for 'tap up to one target creature'. In response to the end-step trigger, the trigger still resolves (independent of its source) and the granted mana ability is kept (gained after the blank began). Wasp must resolve before their end step starts.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Sunderflock enters, bounce trigger on the stack: flash Tidebinder, counter it. Sunderflock is now a ground 5/5 with no abilities and our fliers keep connecting. Next turn Floodpits Drowner stuns it ('tap target creature an opponent controls and put a stun counter on it') and the turn after '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries' deletes it. Kaito -2 (two stun counters) is the backup stun. Post-board watch Shore Up: its 'Untap it' on a stunned creature removes a stun counter instead of untapping (stun-counter rule), so a Drowner-stunned creature loses its only counter and the shuffle has no legal target, and its hexproof fizzles the shuffle if cast in response; Kaito's two counters survive one Shore Up.
  • Sunderflock on the stack and they are tapped low: We Say Thee Nay! with teamwork (tap any creatures you control with total power 2 or more, two 1/1 fliers works since they are not attacking on their turn) makes it 'counter unless its controller pays {4}'; without teamwork it is only {2}. Since Sunderflock is usually cast for {U}{U}, count their open mana after casting: 3 or fewer untapped means teamwork Nay is a hard counter and saves Tidebinder; 4+ untapped means let it resolve and Tidebinder the trigger instead.
  • They hold {U}{U} up with Get Out: when your Tidebinder spell gets targeted by Get Out mode 1, Spell Snare the Get Out (mana value 2). Spell Snare also counters Prismari Charm (the bounce that ends a Tidebinder or Wasp blank), Sear, Hydro-Man, Flashfreeze, Disdainful Stroke and the Stoke Genius adventure half of Hearth Elemental (MV 2 on the stack; countering it puts Hearth in the graveyard, no creature later).
  • Eddymurk Crab flashed at OUR end step targeting our two blockers: this is their strongest Crab window (our creatures stay tapped through their turn, the Crab untaps in their untap step and swings). Tidebinder counters the taps only if you hold a second Tidebinder or teamwork Nay for the {U}{U} Sunderflock that Crab just enabled, or if being unable to block is lethal-relevant. Otherwise take the taps, keep Tidebinder, and untap into Drowner/Kaito -2 on the Crab.
  • Our turn, they have one untapped 5/5 and we want a ground attack: Wasp in precombat main taps it (or Kaito -2 stuns it), then Enduring Curiosity and Kaito attack alongside the fliers; every connection draws. A flier that was going to be unblocked anyway is the ninjutsu body for a Kaito in hand ({1}{U}{B}), and Kaito is hexproof on our turn so a Crab flashed in mid-combat cannot tap him.
  • Wasp on a resolved Sunderflock strips Flying, so Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren and Wan Shi Tong swing over it every turn while Curiosity converts each connection into a card. If Tidebinder already blanked Sunderflock, skip Wasp and keep it to tap the flyer of a second Sunderflock or for a Stallion post-board.
  • Post-board, our Drowner (or Kaito -2, Sheriff, Wasp) targets Stallion, Ward {1} trigger on the stack: Tidebinder counters the ward trigger. Our effect resolves untaxed and Stallion loses Ward, haste and Opus while Tidebinder lives, so no more pump or token copies. Alternatively wait for an Opus trigger from a 5-mana Traumatic Critique or kicked Burst Lightning and Tidebinder that (no ward tax, no token copy). Either way Sheriff or Bitter Triumph then finishes the vanilla 3/3.
  • Kaito is your Sunderflock-proof threat: on their turn he is a planeswalker, so the bounce never touches him, and Wasp, Tidebinder, Drowner, Siren and Beavers can all be replayed after a bounce with fresh ETBs while he keeps ticking. Keep him out of Burst Lightning (2, or 4 kicked), Sear (4), Impractical Joke (3, sorcery) and Traumatic Critique range where you can; all of them hit planeswalkers.
  • Empty-board Sunderflock (we control only Kaito or nothing): the 'if you cast it' trigger still goes on the stack with nothing to bounce. Tidebinder on it is purely a 'strip Flying from the 5/5' play, and it is usually still right, because the flyer is what stops the Curiosity engine once we rebuild.
  • Second Sunderflock while Tidebinder is blanking the first: the blanked one is still a mana-value-9 Elemental, so copy two costs {U}{U} and its bounce would return Tidebinder and restore Flying on copy one. Answer the spell with teamwork Nay, or the trigger with Tidebinder number two; if neither is available, Sheriff or Bitter Triumph the first Sunderflock before their turn so at least one flyer is gone for good.
Hold or fire
Rule: Tidebinder is the Sunderflock answer first and everything else second. The trip-wire is ANY Elemental on their battlefield, and the cost table is what to read: no Elemental means Sunderflock costs nine and is not castable; a Crab or another Sunderflock (blanked or not) makes it {U}{U}; Hearth Elemental makes it {1}{U}{U}; post-board a Stallion or Stallion token (MV 3) makes it {4}{U}{U} off six lands, and Hydro-Man as a creature on their turn (MV 2) makes it {5}{U}{U}. Count their untapped mana against that number every turn. While no Elemental is out, Tidebinder is free to be a flash 3/2 at their end step to pressure, but remember the Crab problem: the Crab whose ETB you would counter IS the first Elemental, so firing on a Crab ETB always leaves them a {U}{U} Sunderflock next turn with your answer spent. Fire on a Crab ETB only with a second Tidebinder or a teamwork We Say Thee Nay! in hand, or when the taps cost you lethal-relevant damage or a game-deciding Curiosity draw. Once any Elemental is out, hold {2}{U} on their turn, with two honest caveats: a good Izzet pilot will often refuse to cast Sunderflock into three open blue mana and flash Crabs at your end step instead, so holding delays the flyer rather than guaranteeing the counter, and you should not skip your own development for more than one turn waiting. The real mana conflict is Kaito, not Curiosity: Enduring Curiosity has flash and is cast at their end step in the same pass-with-mana-up posture as Tidebinder (at 4 lands on their turn the menu is Curiosity, or Tidebinder plus 1, or Drowner plus Spell Snare), but ninjutsu Kaito ({1}{U}{B}) happens on our turn and leaves nothing for Tidebinder. On those turns prefer Kaito: he survives the bounce, and Siren, Beavers and Drowner rebuy their ETBs when replayed. Never flash either card in response to a Sunderflock ETB expecting the bounce to stop; only Tidebinder countering the trigger does that. Wasp is the opposite of Tidebinder: fire it freely as a flash flyer when tapping a 5/5 lets Curiosity, Kaito, Reef or Sanctuary attack, or when tapping a 5/5 attacker in their beginning of combat buys a turn; hold it only when Sunderflock is on the battlefield with Flying (Wasp grounds it) or, post-board, for a Stallion with 3 mana available. Do not Wasp-tap a Crab or Hearth so fliers can attack; they already fly over. On the play you get one extra development turn before the hold rule bites; on the draw assume their turn 5 Sunderflock (turn 4 only after they cantripped three or four spells into the graveyard by turn 3) and have Tidebinder or Nay mana up from your turn 4 onward once an Elemental is out.
How many to run
Game 1: play the stock configuration (1 Tishana's Tidebinder main, 2 The Wondrous Wasp main). Tidebinder has eight live main-deck targets and is the only cheap creature of ours that survives Prismari Charm; Wasp is a fine flash flyer in game 1 for grounding Sunderflock and tapping a 5/5 for a ground attack. A second main-deck Tidebinder over the fourth Requiting Hex would help this 12.2% matchup (Hex hits only Hydro-Man here: 'Destroy target creature with mana value 2 or less' misses Crab MV 7, Hearth MV 6, Sunderflock MV 9, Stallion MV 3), but main-deck construction should be decided across all matchups (Hex kills Mono-Black and Boros two-drops), so this page does not change the stock 1. Post-board vs Izzet: +2 Tidebinder (3 total), +2 Spell Pierce (hits their 28 instants/sorceries plus the Stoke Genius adventure half of 4 Hearth Elemental), +2 Flashfreeze (counters Burst Lightning, Sear, Impractical Joke, Traumatic Critique, Prismari Charm, Stoke Genius, the Hearth Elemental creature half and Stallion, all red); out: -4 Requiting Hex, -2 The Wondrous Wasp. Wasp is the cut rather than a keep post-board: their only flyer is Sunderflock, the 2/1 dies to every damage spell they have including Charm's 1 damage (often a two-for-one with a Siren or Beaver), it is countered by Spell Snare and Get Out, and Stallion taxes it. Duress can replace a Bitter Triumph on the play if you want more counter-magic protection, but Bitter Triumph and Shoot the Sheriff are your clean answers to 5/5s, so do not cut both. Net: 3 Tidebinder, 0 Wasp after sideboarding.
Review trail: confidence high, 7 disputed, 22 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 3 (oracle-grounding) concluded 'Shore Up DOES untap a stunned creature without removing the counter': rejected. The stun-counter rule (CR 122.1e) is 'If a permanent with a stun counter on it would become untapped, instead remove a stun counter from it', and it is not limited to the untap step. Shore Up's 'Untap it' on a Drowner-stunned creature removes the stun counter and the creature stays tapped, which also leaves Drowner's shuffle with no legal target. Reviewer 1 stated this correctly and the page uses that version.
  • Reviewer 1's reading that the draft claimed a bounced Drowner 'frees a stunned Crab' of its counter: the draft meant that the Crab can no longer be shuffled (Drowner's activated ability is gone from the battlefield). Counters stay per the rules brief; the sentence was ambiguous and has been rewritten, but the underlying claim was not wrong.
  • (Carried from the draft, anticipated objections.) 'Ward {1} taxes Tidebinder when it counters Stallion's Opus trigger': rejected. CR 702.21b: ward triggers 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls'; Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, not the permanent. Wasp, Drowner, Kaito -2 and Sheriff target the creature and are taxed.
  • 'Wasp in response to Sunderflock's ETB blanks it and stops the bounce': rejected. CR 113.7a: an ability on the stack exists independently of its source; removing the source's abilities does not affect it. The same rule is why Wasp cannot stop Hydro-Man's end-step trigger once it is on the stack.
  • 'Tidebinder can counter Hearth Elemental's cost reduction or the Stoke Genius adventure': rejected. Cost reduction is a static ability (CR 113.3b, never on the stack); casting an adventure is casting a spell, not an ability. Spell Snare (the adventure half is MV 2 on the stack, Hearth Elemental's oracle shows {1}{R} for Stoke Genius) is the answer.
  • 'A Tidebinder-blanked Sunderflock stays blanked if they Get Out it and recast': rejected. CR 400.7: a permanent that leaves the battlefield and returns is a new object; the loses-all-abilities effect applied to the old object. The recast is a cast, so 'if you cast it' is satisfied and the bounce triggers again.
  • 'Kaito gets bounced by Sunderflock': rejected. Kaito's oracle makes him a creature only 'During your turn, as long as Kaito has one or more loyalty counters'; Sunderflock has no flash and is cast on their turn, when he is a planeswalker.
Changes after review
  • Removed Impractical Joke from every instant-speed response list (it is a Sorcery per the deck file); added a trap entry and a note that Joke kills Tidebinder later on their main phase, ending the blank.
  • Rewrote the Wasp-on-Hydro-Man timing window: Wasp must resolve before their end step begins; once the end-step trigger is on the stack it resolves independently of its source and the granted mana ability is kept (ruling 2). Updated the Hydro-Man Wasp trap to match.
  • Deleted the 'no Elemental on their board yet' exception for firing Tidebinder on a Crab ETB everywhere (Crab risk, combined lines, hold_or_fire); Eddymurk Crab is itself an Elemental with MV 7, and a blanked Crab still discounts Sunderflock to {U}{U}. Added this to the cost-reduction trap.
  • Added OUR end step as the most dangerous Crab window (our creatures stay tapped through their turn, the Crab untaps in their untap step); rewrote the Crab target's why and timing and added a combined line for it. Reworded the dont_bother item to judge Crab taps by blocking need on their turn.
  • Rewrote the Sunderflock trip-wire as a full cost table covering any Elemental: Crab/Sunderflock {U}{U}, Hearth {1}{U}{U}, Stallion or Stallion token {4}{U}{U}, Hydro-Man as a creature {5}{U}{U}. Noted Hydro-Man is not an Elemental while he is a land on our turn and is never bounced by their own Sunderflock.
  • Reframed the Sunderflock #1 entry: the prize is at least half 'the 5/5 loses Flying' (their only flyer, the one body that blocks our Curiosity engine), and the bounce is survivable (Siren/Beavers/Drowner rebuy ETBs, Curiosity has flash, Kaito untouched). Added the empty-board Sunderflock case (trigger still goes on the stack; Tidebinder purely to strip Flying).
  • Corrected 'dies to any of their burn': Prismari Charm's 1 damage per target does not kill the 3/2 Tidebinder; Burst Lightning, Sear, Traumatic Critique X>=2 do, plus Impractical Joke at sorcery speed. Wasp dies to all of them including Charm.
  • Deleted 'for good' from the Stallion blank; the blank lasts while Tidebinder remains on the battlefield.
  • Added Prismari Charm mode 3 on our Tidebinder to the Sunderflock and Stallion risk text as the most common lock-breaker; added Burst Lightning to the list of what hits Kaito.
  • Added Get Out mode 2 / Charm mode 3 on their own Sunderflock in response to Tidebinder's trigger as a risk (bounce still countered, no permanent loses abilities, recast next turn), with the recast cost depending on which Elementals remain (not always {U}{U}).
  • Rewrote Wasp's tap value against Crab and Hearth: neither has flying or reach, so our fliers are unblocked regardless; the tap only matters for ground attackers (Curiosity, Kaito, Reef, Sanctuary) or to stop one attack in their beginning of combat. Added a Wasp trap for the wrong idea and downgraded Crab/Hearth to a merged priority-3 tap-only target.
  • Promoted Colorstorm Stallion's Ward {1} trigger from dont_bother to a ranked Tidebinder target (priority 3): counter the ward trigger when our Drowner/Kaito -2/Sheriff/Wasp targets Stallion, effect resolves untaxed and Stallion is blanked. Added the Wasp + Tidebinder double-lock to the Wasp Stallion entry and a combined line.
  • Consolidated Hydro-Man into one Tidebinder target (the end-step trigger) and moved the blue-spell pump trigger to dont_bother with a one-line note.
  • Added Shore Up's full text and both effects: hexproof fizzles Wasp's trigger and Drowner's shuffle, 'Untap it' undoes a resolved Wasp tap, and on a stunned creature the untap removes a stun counter instead (stun-counter rule), so a single Drowner stun is undone while Kaito's two counters survive one Shore Up. Noted Shore Up's hexproof does nothing against Tidebinder (it targets the ability).
  • Fixed the We Say Thee Nay! teamwork instruction: tap any creatures with total power 2 or more (two 1/1 fliers works); added the no-teamwork {2} figure, both from the oracle text now verified in opponents.json.
  • Removed Requiting Hex from the 'replayed after a bounce' list (it is an instant); replaced with Wasp, Tidebinder, Drowner, Siren, Beavers.
  • Replaced the 'do not tap out for Curiosity' advice with the real conflict: Curiosity has flash and shares the pass-with-mana-up posture with Tidebinder; ninjutsu Kaito on our turn is what prevents holding Tidebinder, and Kaito is the preferred play on those turns.
  • Stated the realistic Sunderflock window as their turn 5-6 (turn 4 only after a perfect cantrip start) and added that a good Izzet pilot will often refuse to cast it into open mana.
  • Fixed '28 of their 60' to '28 of their 61' and corrected Spell Pierce's target count to 28 plus the 4 Stoke Genius adventure halves.
  • Rewrote counts_recommendation: game 1 stock (1 Tidebinder, 2 Wasp); post-board +2 Tidebinder (3 total), +2 Spell Pierce, +2 Flashfreeze, -4 Requiting Hex, -2 Wasp; main-deck construction changes deferred to a cross-matchup decision; fixed the '3-4 total' arithmetic.
  • Added a combined line for a second Sunderflock while the first is blanked (still an MV-9 Elemental, so copy two costs {U}{U} and its bounce returns Tidebinder).
  • Verified every our-deck oracle text used on the page (We Say Thee Nay!, Requiting Hex, Floodpits Drowner, Kaito, Wan Shi Tong, Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren, Enduring Curiosity, Soulstone Sanctuary, Restless Reef, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Flashfreeze, Spell Pierce, Duress) against opponents.json cards{}; all claims match. Re-verified opponent mana costs from the deck file: Crab {5}{U}{U}, Hearth {5}{R} // Stoke Genius {1}{R}, Sunderflock {7}{U}{U}, Stallion {1}{U}{R}, Hydro-Man {U}{U}, Get Out {U}{U}, Prismari Charm {U}{R}, Traumatic Critique {X}{U}{R}.
vs. Dimir Midrange (mirror)10.8% TB: premiumWasp: good▼
Same 75 as ours: cheap evasive chip damage (Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren, Floodpits Drowner) turns on two card engines. Kaito arrives via ninjutsu on THEIR turn 3 whenever a 1-drop flier connects (play or draw, {1}{U}{B} plus an unblocked attacker), then his 0 ("Surveil 2. Then draw a card for each opponent who lost life this turn") fires every turn; Enduring Curiosity ("Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card") comes back as an enchantment when killed. Wan Shi Tong at X=3-4 is the late haymaker (counters plus cards on ETB), usually flashed in at our end step when we tap out. Their permanent answers to our Curiosity are Floodpits Drowner's stun-then-shuffle and their own Tidebinder on our dies-return trigger (post-board also Day of Black Sun, Vren's exile replacement, Strategic Betrayal); Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph and Requiting Hex permanently handle our Wan Shi Tong and Tidebinder. The mirror is decided by who keeps a card engine online while the other's is turned off.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Premium, but for a specific reason: every engine they have is an activated or triggered ability on a creature or planeswalker (Kaito's loyalty abilities, Wan Shi Tong's ETB, Curiosity's dies-return trigger, Drowner's shuffle), and Tidebinder at MV 3 dodges their 4 Requiting Hex and 2 Spell Snare. Its FRAGILITY is also highest here: the mirror has ~10 answers to a 3/2 (2 Shoot the Sheriff, 2 Bitter Triumph, We Say Thee Nay! on the spell, 4 Drowner stun-then-shuffle, Kaito -2 setting up a Drowner, their own Tidebinder on our trigger, post-board Day of Black Sun X>=3 and Strategic Betrayal if it is our only creature). So value Tidebinder for what the COUNTER permanently denies (a Wan Shi Tong ETB's counters and cards, a Curiosity return, a Kaito 0's card or -2's stun) and treat the 'loses all abilities' lock as a bonus that lasts until they find an answer. Prefer targets whose payoff survives Tidebinder dying, and use it as a counterspell to protect our own engines from THEIR Tidebinder and Wasp triggers, the most common post-board stack fight.
#1Kaito, Bane of Nightmares
Any loyalty activation: '+1: You get an emblem with "Ninjas you control get +1/+1."' / '0: Surveil 2. Then draw a card for each opponent who lost life this turn.' / '-2: Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it.'
WhyCountering one loyalty ability makes Kaito lose ALL abilities while Tidebinder lives: no more activations, and he loses the static 'During your turn, as long as Kaito has one or more loyalty counters on him, he's a 3/4 Ninja creature and has hexproof', so he never attacks, never triggers their Curiosity, and has NO hexproof on either turn (Bitter Triumph kills him at instant speed on any turn while the lock holds). Rank the activation to counter: -2 aimed at our Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong/lock-holding Tidebinder (Kaito drops to 2, no stun, a waiting Drowner has no shuffle target) > 0 (denies the card, Kaito at 4) > +1 (Kaito at 5, only take it if it is the first activation you can reach).
WhenTheir main phase (main 2 after a ninjutsu attack, or main 1 on later turns), loyalty ability on the stack. Flash Tidebinder in response with {2}{U} up; hexproof is irrelevant because Tidebinder targets the ability, not Kaito. First reachable window: on the play, their turn 3 (we have 3 lands); on the draw, their turn-3 Kaito is uncatchable (we have 2 lands) and the first catchable activation is their turn 4.
AfterKaito keeps his loyalty counters (5 after a countered +1, 4 after a countered 0, 2 after a countered -2) and stays a planeswalker you can attack or Triumph. Loses: all three loyalty abilities, ninjutsu (irrelevant on the battlefield), and the creature/hexproof static. An emblem they already made keeps working.
RiskThey unlock him by removing Tidebinder (Sheriff, Triumph, Drowner shuffle after a stun, Day of Black Sun X>=3) or by the legend rule: a second Kaito lets them keep the fresh one and bin the blank. Fix: finish the blank Kaito fast. 2 loyalty dies to two 1-power fliers; 4 to Wasp+Beaver+Siren in one swing; 5 needs Bitter Triumph or two turns. Then Tidebinder is a free 3/2.
#2Tishana's Tidebinder (theirs)
'When this creature enters, counter up to one target activated or triggered ability.'
WhyThe mirror's highest-stakes stack fight. When their Tidebinder's trigger targets OUR Kaito loyalty activation, our Wan Shi Tong ETB, our Curiosity's dies-return trigger or our Drowner shuffle, respond with our Tidebinder countering THEIR trigger. Our ability resolves untouched and their Tidebinder (a creature on the battlefield at resolution) is a blank 3/2 for as long as ours lives. Protecting a Wan Shi Tong at X>=2 or a Curiosity return is worth more than most offensive uses.
WhenTheir Tidebinder trigger on the stack above our ability. Needs {2}{U} open on OUR turn for the Kaito-activation and Wan Shi Tong cases, so on turns where our Kaito 0 or WST ETB is the play and they hold 3+ open mana, sequence to leave {2}{U} up.
AfterTheir Tidebinder: blank 3/2 (flash is irrelevant once it is in play). Our ability resolves; nothing of ours was ever locked.
RiskPost-board they may have a second Tidebinder or We Say Thee Nay! for our Tidebinder spell. Hold Nay alongside Tidebinder when you expect the fight.
#3Wan Shi Tong, Librarian
'When Wan Shi Tong enters, put X +1/+1 counters on him. Then draw half X cards, rounded down.'
WhyOne trigger carries both the counters and the cards, and denying it is permanent even if Tidebinder dies next turn. Counter it and their X+2 mana bought a 1/1 that also loses flying and vigilance. At X=4 (6 mana) this is the biggest single swing in the mirror. The body afterwards is a Requiting Hex target: Wan Shi Tong is mana value 2 on the battlefield (X counts as 0 anywhere but the stack, CR 202.3e).
WhenETB trigger on the stack. It has flash, so the usual cast is at OUR end step when we tap out; from 6 lands on, if you hold Tidebinder and they have 4+ open mana, prefer plays that keep {2}{U} up through our end step (Drowner, one-drop plus interaction) over tapping out for a 4-drop. Only worth it at X>=2; at X=1 they get 1 counter and 0 cards.
AfterKeeps: 1/1 body, Bird Spirit types (Shoot the Sheriff and Requiting Hex both kill it). Loses: flying, vigilance, the ETB (already countered) and 'Whenever an opponent searches their library' (we never search).
RiskThey can bait with X=1 into our open mana. Their own Tidebinder can counter ours in response (see target 2).
#4Enduring Curiosity
'When Enduring Curiosity dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control. It's an enchantment.'
WhyThe trigger's source is in the graveyard when it goes on the stack, so nothing is blanked, but Curiosity stays dead for good and the result does NOT depend on Tidebinder surviving. The returned enchantment version would draw forever and can never be blanked (not an artifact/creature/planeswalker).
WhenOur Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph resolves on the creature version, return trigger goes on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it. Needs 2+3 mana in the same stack. Also fires when THEY trade Curiosity in combat against our 4/3 or lose it to their own Day of Black Sun.
AfterCuriosity stays in the graveyard. Nothing on the battlefield loses abilities. Tidebinder is a free 3/2.
RiskPost-board their Ghost Vacuum can exile the dead Curiosity to rescue it for a later {6} mass return as a 1/1 flier with all its abilities; slow and sorcery-speed, not nothing.
#5The Wondrous Wasp (theirs)
'Wasp's Sting: When The Wondrous Wasp enters, tap up to one target creature. It loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield.'
WhyTheir Wasp aimed at our Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong (tap it before attackers, or blank Curiosity so Sheriff/Triumph kills it for good) is a creature's triggered ability. Counter it: our creature is neither tapped nor blanked, and their Wasp is a blank 2/1 with no flying while Tidebinder lives.
WhenTheir Wasp trigger on the stack, usually flashed in at our beginning of combat or in their main phase right before a kill spell.
AfterTheir Wasp: 2/1 ground creature, no flying, no Sting. Our engine untouched.
RiskSpending 3 mana on their 2; worth it only when the target is Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong or a Tidebinder holding a lock.
#6Floodpits Drowner
'When this creature enters, tap target creature an opponent controls and put a stun counter on it.'
WhyWhen the ETB targets our Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong or lock-holding Tidebinder, counter it: no tap, no stun counter, Drowner is permanently blank while Tidebinder lives, and any second Drowner already on their board has no stunned target to shuffle. Better than waiting for the shuffle, because waiting costs our engine an attack and hands them a same-turn shuffle option.
WhenETB trigger on the stack, typically flashed in at our beginning of combat or end step. Our Kaito is never a legal target for their Drowner (not a creature on their turn, hexproof on ours), so this only matters when Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong or Tidebinder is on our board.
AfterKeeps: 2/1 body, untapped. Loses: vigilance and the shuffle ability for as long as Tidebinder lives.
Risk3 for their 2, and a blank 2/1 still chips. Skip it when the target is a Beaver or Siren.
#7Floodpits Drowner
'{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries.'
WhyThe fallback when we lacked mana at the stun step. Counter it: our creature stays, they lose 2 mana, Drowner stays tapped (costs paid) and loses all abilities so it never tries again while Tidebinder lives.
WhenInstant speed: their main phase, our end step, or in response to our removal on the Drowner. The stun does NOT always come a turn ahead: Kaito -2 (two stun counters, zero mana) or a second Drowner's ETB lets a Drowner that was already on their board when their turn began shuffle in the same main phase (a Drowner cannot use its {T} ability the turn it enters, CR 302.6). Hold {2}{U} on their turn whenever a stun counter sits on one of our engines, or whenever both an active Kaito and an untapped Drowner are out.
AfterKeeps: 2/1 body, still tapped. Loses: vigilance, shuffle ability. Our creature keeps its stun counter (one counter = it skips its next untap step).
RiskIf the stunned creature is our only Tidebinder, we need a second copy in hand.
#8Enduring Curiosity
'Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card.'
WhyCountering one draw trigger blanks the creature version: no more draws AND no dies-return trigger while Tidebinder lives. Then Sheriff/Triumph it and it is gone forever (a permanent with no abilities immediately before it died has no leaves-the-battlefield trigger to look back on, CR 603.10a, exactly how their own Day of Black Sun kills it). Ranked below the return-trigger line because it needs Tidebinder to survive until the kill resolves.
WhenTheir combat damage step, trigger on the stack. If two creatures connect there are two separate triggers already on the stack and Tidebinder counters only one; the other still draws. Best when exactly one creature connects and we hold the kill spell for the same turn.
AfterKeeps: 4/3 Enchantment Creature body, can attack and block. Loses: draw trigger, dies-return trigger, flash.
RiskIf they kill Tidebinder before our removal resolves, Curiosity regains its abilities and returns when it dies.
#9Kaito, Bane of Nightmares
Ninjutsu '{1}{U}{B}, Return an unblocked attacker you control to hand: Put this card onto the battlefield from your hand tapped and attacking.'
WhyPure tempo: costs are paid, so the attacker is already back in hand, no damage comes through, Kaito stays in hand (a card in hand is not a permanent, nothing is blanked) and they cannot 0 for a card this turn; they must hard-cast him for 4 next turn into We Say Thee Nay!. Conditional call versus letting it resolve and countering the loyalty activation in main 2 for the same {2}{U}: counter the NINJUTSU when we cannot punish a blank Kaito (no Bitter Triumph, fewer than two fliers), when their Curiosity is already out (Kaito connecting draws), or when the 3 damage matters. Let it RESOLVE and counter the 0/-2 when we hold Triumph or enough fliers to finish a blank 4-loyalty Kaito within a turn.
WhenDeclare blockers step, after blocks, ninjutsu ability on the stack. Free alternatives first: block their lone flier with our flier (a blocked creature is not an 'unblocked attacker', so ninjutsu is impossible), or Requiting Hex the flier in the declare attackers step (no creature is unblocked until blockers are declared, CR 509.1h).
AfterKaito in hand with all abilities; the returned attacker in hand, re-castable. Our Tidebinder is a 3/2 with no lock to protect.
RiskYou have spent the Tidebinder and Kaito is still coming.
#10Vren, the Relentless (SB)
'At the beginning of each end step, create X 1/1 black Rat creature tokens with "This token gets +1/+1 for each other Rat you control," where X is the number of creatures that were exiled under your opponents' control this turn.'
WhyThis triggers every end step (no intervening if; X may be 0), so there is a free window each turn. Countering it blanks Vren: no ward, no 'If a creature an opponent controls would die, exile it instead', no Rats. That replacement is the reason to act BEFORE our Curiosity dies: under their Vren our Curiosity is exiled instead of dying, never returns, and feeds them Rats. Ward does not tax Tidebinder (see disputed). Primary answer is still Bitter Triumph paying ward {2} (4 mana, one card); Tidebinder is the fallback when Triumph is not in hand. Shoot the Sheriff cannot (Rat Rogue is an outlaw regardless of abilities) and Hex cannot (MV 4).
WhenAny end step (ours or theirs) with the trigger on the stack.
AfterKeeps: 3/4 body, existing Rat tokens (their pump is their own ability). Loses: ward, exile replacement, token trigger. Triumph now kills him for 2.
RiskSideboard 1-of; only relevant post-board.
#11Restless Reef
'Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards.' (or the activation '{2}{U}{B}: Until end of turn, this land becomes a 4/4 blue and black Shark creature with deathtouch.')
WhyCounter the ATTACK trigger: Reef is a creature at resolution, so it loses all abilities: deathtouch gone (our blocker survives), and it loses its mana ability and animation for as long as Tidebinder lives, a dead land. The mill itself is irrelevant in the mirror; the value is the dead land and the lost deathtouch. Counter the ANIMATION instead: 4 of their mana wasted and no attack, but Reef is a land at resolution and nothing is blanked (ruling 3).
WhenAttack trigger: declare attackers step. Animation: whenever they pay {2}{U}{B}, usually precombat.
AfterAttack-trigger version: 4/4 Shark until end of turn without deathtouch, then a land that cannot tap for mana or animate. Animation version: untouched land, 4 mana lost.
RiskLate-game only; usually the Tidebinder is better held for Kaito/Wan Shi Tong.
#12Ghost Vacuum (SB)
'{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard.'
WhyTheir Vacuum's job in the mirror is to exile OUR Curiosity from the graveyard while its return trigger is on the stack. Counter the tap ability: our Curiosity returns, Vacuum stays tapped and loses all abilities (artifact) while Tidebinder lives.
WhenIn response to their Vacuum activation, typically stacked above our Curiosity's dies-return trigger.
AfterVacuum: blank artifact, both abilities gone. Our Curiosity comes back as an enchantment.
RiskSideboard 1-of. The {6} mode is a legal target too and countering it stops the mass return, but Vacuum was sacrificed as a cost so nothing is blanked.
Traps
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares: Target 'he's a 3/4 Ninja creature and has hexproof' or the emblem to turn Kaito off. The creature/hexproof clause is a static ability and an emblem is not a permanent; neither can be targeted. The only way to strip Kaito is to counter a loyalty activation; countering ninjutsu blanks nothing because Kaito is still a card in hand when the trigger resolves.
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares: Countering ninjutsu keeps their attacker attacking or makes them lose it. Returning the unblocked attacker is a cost; it is already in hand when we respond. The countered ninjutsu just means Kaito stays in hand too. Net: they lose 3 mana and this turn's damage, keep both cards.
Wan Shi Tong, Librarian: A resolved 5/5 Wan Shi Tong is out of Requiting Hex range because they paid X=4. X is 0 for an object not on the stack (CR 202.3e), so Wan Shi Tong on the battlefield is mana value 2 at any size. Hex kills it for {B}; Day of Black Sun X=2 sweeps it. Tidebinder on the ETB is still the play when the trigger is on the stack (it denies the cards and counters, which Hex cannot undo), but the body afterwards is a 1-mana problem, not a Sheriff-or-Kaito problem. Spell Snare, by contrast, only counters it if cast at X=0.
Enduring Curiosity (returned enchantment): Blank it with Tidebinder once it has come back as an enchantment. After it returns 'It's an enchantment. (It's not a creature.)', so countering its draw trigger is a one-shot counter only; only artifacts, creatures and planeswalkers lose abilities. The permanent answers are: counter the dies-return trigger, or blank the creature version and kill it, or our own Drowner shuffle.
Enduring Curiosity: One Tidebinder stops all the draws when several creatures connect. Each connecting creature makes a separate trigger; triggers already on the stack exist independently of their source, so blanking Curiosity only removes the one you targeted and future triggers.
Vren, the Relentless: Counter 'If a creature an opponent controls would die, exile it instead.' Replacement effect (static), not targetable. Reach it by countering the end-step Rat trigger, which then blanks Vren, or just Bitter Triumph him paying ward {2}.
Restless Reef / Soulstone Sanctuary: Countering the animation ability turns the land off for good. At resolution the land has not become a creature yet, so per ruling 3 it loses nothing; you only fizzle that activation. Reef is blanked via its attack trigger while animated; Sanctuary has no trigger, so it is blanked only by Wasp after it is a creature, or, edge case, if they re-activate {4} while it is ALREADY a creature and you counter that second activation (creature at resolution, ruling 3 cuts both ways).
Watery Grave / We Say Thee Nay! / Requiting Hex / Bitter Triumph: Counter 'you may pay 2 life', Teamwork, blight, or the discard/3-life cost. Watery Grave's clause is a replacement effect; Teamwork, blight and Triumph's cost are additional costs of casting a spell. None are abilities on the stack. All their counterspells, removal, Duress, Annul and Day of Black Sun are spells, which Tidebinder cannot touch.
Spyglass Siren's Map token / Ghost Vacuum {6} mode: Counter the activation and blank the artifact. Both sacrifice the artifact as a cost; the source is gone when the trigger resolves, so nothing is blanked. The Map's explore is not worth 3 mana anyway.
Tishana's Tidebinder / The Wondrous Wasp (theirs, on ours): If they Wasp or Tidebinder-blank OUR Tidebinder, their Kaito/Curiosity wake up. Our blank is a continuous effect created by a resolved ability with duration 'for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield' (CR 611.2a). Our Tidebinder losing its own abilities does not end it (same reason Humility does not return a Banisher Priest's exiled card). Only removing our Tidebinder from the battlefield ends it. What their Tidebinder CAN do is counter our ETB trigger while it is on the stack, so no lock is ever created; our answer to that is a second Tidebinder on their trigger or We Say Thee Nay! on their Tidebinder spell.
Floodpits Drowner (shuffle): The shuffle is always visible a turn ahead because the stun comes from Drowner's ETB. Kaito -2 puts two stun counters for zero mana, and a second Drowner's ETB stuns too; a Drowner that was on their board when their turn began can shuffle in the same main phase. Counter the stun source (the -2 or the ETB) when it targets an engine; the shuffle counter is the fallback.
Not worth it
Dream Beavers ETB ('each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life. Scry 1'): a life point and a scry; the only edge case is when it is the sole life loss enabling a Kaito 0 draw, still just one card.Spyglass Siren ETB (create a Map token): a land or a +1/+1 counter at best.Floodpits Drowner ETB aimed at a Beaver or Siren: let it happen, hold for engines.Soulstone Sanctuary '{4}: This land becomes a 3/3 creature': a land at resolution, nothing blanked; wasting their 4 mana is not worth our 3 and the Tidebinder.Wan Shi Tong at X<=1: 1 counter, 0 cards; save it (and Hex the 1/1 or 2/2 later if it matters).Wan Shi Tong's 'Whenever an opponent searches their library': our deck never searches.Faebloom Trick's reflexive tap ('When you do, tap target creature an opponent controls'): counterable, but the source is an instant so nothing is blanked and they keep two 1/1 fliers; 3 mana to save one attack, hold.Ghost Vacuum {6} sacrifice mode: counters the return but blanks nothing and is rarely activated in the mirror.Kaito +1 when a 0 or -2 is coming next turn anyway: the emblem does nothing without a Ninja creature attacking, and countering it leaves Kaito at 5; take it only as the first reachable activation.Body plays (either card): when the 3/2 or 2/1 flier is the actual value (chip damage for our Curiosity draws, a blocker), cast it at their end step with no target or an incidental one; never HOLD for a Beaver/Siren/Faerie/Rat-token tap.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp can never touch Kaito (a hexproof 3/4 creature on their turn, not a creature at all on ours), which caps it. What it does well: tap-and-blank Enduring Curiosity (loses both the draw trigger and the dies-return trigger, so a follow-up Shoot the Sheriff/Bitter Triumph kills it for good, all at instant speed), pre-empt Floodpits Drowner's shuffle, and tap an animated land or attacker at their beginning of combat. Its weakness is fragility: Requiting Hex, Spell Snare, Sheriff, Triumph and Drowner all answer a 2-mana Wasp, and the blank ends the moment it leaves. A resolved Wan Shi Tong is a Hex target (MV 2 on the battlefield), not a Wasp target. Body plays: Wasp is also a fine flash flier for our own Curiosity draws or to swing at a blank Kaito; take incidental taps (a Beaver/Siren/Faerie/Rat blocker so our fliers connect) but never hold it for those.
#1Enduring Curiosity (creature version)
WhyBlank = no 'Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card' and no 'When Enduring Curiosity dies ... return it', so Sheriff/Triumph then removes it permanently for 4 total mana. Tapping it at their beginning of combat also stops the 4-power attack that turn.
WhenWasp, Sheriff and Triumph are all instants, so the blank-then-kill line runs on THEIR turn. Fire it when they have fewer untapped mana than a Hex ({B}) or Snare ({U}) needs: most often their own main phase right after they tap out for a hard-cast Kaito, Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong, or at our end step after they already spent mana on our turn. Instant-speed removal on Wasp mid-stack restores the return trigger, so do not run it into open Hex/Snare mana when the blank is the point. Tap-only value: their beginning of combat, before attackers.
AfterKeeps: 4/3 body, can block. Loses: draw trigger, return trigger, flash. Both come back if Wasp leaves before the kill resolves.
#2Floodpits Drowner
WhyA blank Drowner cannot activate '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it', saving our stunned Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong/Tidebinder. Wasp must land BEFORE they activate; Wasp in response to a shuffle already on the stack does nothing to it.
WhenWindows where Drowner cannot respond with the shuffle: (a) in response to its own ETB or any time before their next turn begins (it cannot use its {T} ability until it has been under their control since the start of their turn, CR 302.6; the stun still resolves, but Drowner is blank before it ever untaps); (b) when nothing of ours carries a stun counter (no legal shuffle target); (c) when they have fewer than 2 untapped mana. Never at their upkeep: they have already untapped and will shuffle in response.
AfterKeeps: 2/1 body. Loses: vigilance and the shuffle ability while Wasp lives.
#3Restless Reef / Soulstone Sanctuary once animated
WhyOnly while they are creatures. Wasp taps the animated land before attackers and it loses all abilities for as long as Wasp lives: no mana ability, no re-animation, Reef loses deathtouch, Sanctuary loses vigilance. A dead land is real tempo. (Requiting Hex also kills an animated land outright: land creatures are MV 0.)
WhenReef: their beginning of combat after they animate precombat; Sanctuary: any time after it has been animated (it stays a creature).
AfterKeeps: creature status and P/T from the resolved animation (types and P/T are not abilities). Loses: mana ability, animation ability, deathtouch/vigilance.
#4Vren, the Relentless (SB)
WhyFallback only. Bitter Triumph paying ward {2} (4 mana, one card) is the primary answer. Wasp plus ward is also 4 mana, blanks Vren (no ward, no exile-replacement, no Rat trigger) and then Triumph kills him for 2, but that is 6 mana across two spells and hands them a Hex/Snare/Sheriff target in between. Use it when Triumph is not in hand and our Curiosity is about to die into his exile replacement.
WhenAny time we have {1}{U} plus {2} for ward; if we skip the ward payment the trigger is countered and Wasp is just a 2/1 flier.
AfterKeeps: 3/4, existing Rats. Loses: all three abilities.
Traps
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares: Wasp him on either turn. On their turn he is a creature with hexproof; on our turn he is not a creature. Wasp never has a legal target. (Same reason their Drowner and Kaito -2 can never hit our Kaito.)
Wan Shi Tong, Librarian (resolved): Wasp a resolved Wan Shi Tong to take away flying and shrink the threat. It is mana value 2 on the battlefield (X is 0 off the stack, CR 202.3e), so Requiting Hex kills it at any size for {B}. Wasp only if you hold no Hex/Sheriff/Triumph and need to stop a lethal swing (tap at their beginning of combat).
Enduring Curiosity (returned enchantment): Wasp it after it has come back. It is no longer a creature, so it cannot be targeted; the Wasp window is only the creature version.
Floodpits Drowner / Wan Shi Tong: Flash Wasp in response to the ETB or shuffle activation to stop it. Blanking a creature does not counter an ability already on the stack (CR 113.7a). Only Tidebinder does that. Responding to Drowner's ETB does still blank Drowner for the future; responding to Wan Shi Tong's ETB does not stop the counters or the cards.
Any attacker: Wasp after attackers are declared to stop the hit. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. Tap in their beginning of combat step.
Their Tidebinder / their Wasp: Wasp their lock piece to free our Kaito or Curiosity. Their effect already exists with duration 'remains on the battlefield'; stripping abilities does not end it. Kill, bounce or shuffle the lock piece instead (Sheriff, Triumph, Hex on a Wasp, our Drowner).
Our own Kaito ninjutsu: Return the Wasp that is holding Curiosity blank to ninjutsu Kaito. Wasp leaving the battlefield ends its effect immediately; Curiosity's draws and return trigger come back. Ninjutsu off a Beaver or Siren instead.
Floodpits Drowner: Wasp it at their upkeep 'while they cannot pay {1}{U}'. They untap before upkeep, so they have full mana and an untapped (vigilance) Drowner; if anything of ours has a stun counter they respond with the shuffle. Use the windows in target 2.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Permanent Curiosity kill A: Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph on the creature Curiosity, then Tidebinder counters the 'When Enduring Curiosity dies ... return it' trigger. Tidebinder-independent; also works when they trade Curiosity into our 4/3 in combat.
  • Permanent Curiosity kill B (all instants): Wasp taps and blanks Curiosity, Sheriff/Triumph kills it in the same stack or turn; no abilities immediately before death means no return trigger. Fire on THEIR turn right after they tap out for a 4-drop, or at our end step once they have spent mana; never into open Hex/Snare mana.
  • Permanent Curiosity kill C (post-board): with our Vren on the battlefield, their Curiosity is exiled instead of dying ('If a creature an opponent controls would die, exile it instead'), so any Sheriff/Triumph is permanent with no Tidebinder needed. Mirror image: blank or Triumph THEIR Vren before our Curiosity dies.
  • Kaito shutdown: Tidebinder counters a loyalty activation (-2 aimed at our engine > 0 > +1); Kaito is a blank planeswalker at 2/4/5 loyalty who is never a creature and has no hexproof on any turn. Bitter Triumph him at instant speed or swing fliers into him before they find a Tidebinder answer or a second Kaito (legend rule reset).
  • Free anti-ninjutsu: block their lone 1/1 flier with our 1/1 flier (no unblocked attacker, ninjutsu impossible), or Requiting Hex the flier in the declare attackers step (nothing is 'unblocked' until blockers are declared, CR 509.1h). Costs no Tidebinder.
  • Stack fight for our engines: when their Tidebinder's trigger targets our Kaito activation, Wan Shi Tong ETB or Curiosity return, our Tidebinder counters THEIR trigger (their Tidebinder blanked, ours resolves). Hold We Say Thee Nay! for their Tidebinder spell (MV 3, Snare misses it) and Spell Snare for their Wasp (MV 2). Their Annul counters our Curiosity spell (it is an enchantment spell); Spell Pierce hits our hard-cast Kaito but never Tidebinder or Wasp.
  • Engine protection vs Drowner: counter the STUN when it targets Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong/lock Tidebinder (Drowner ETB or Kaito -2), because an on-board Drowner can shuffle the same turn. Fallback: counter the shuffle activation (Drowner blank, our creature stays). Pre-emptive: Wasp the Drowner before their next turn begins or while nothing of ours is stunned.
  • Our Curiosity's return vs their Ghost Vacuum: when they tap Vacuum at our return trigger, Tidebinder counters the Vacuum activation; Curiosity returns and Vacuum is a blank artifact while Tidebinder lives.
  • We Say Thee Nay! Teamwork: tap Tidebinder (power 3) as the additional cost to make Nay 'counter unless pays {4}' against their hard-cast Kaito or Wan Shi Tong. A tapped Tidebinder still holds its lock.
  • Wan Shi Tong denied: Tidebinder counters its ETB (1/1, no flying, and the 1-2 cards never come), then Requiting Hex ({B}; it is MV 2 on the battlefield), Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph or our Kaito -2 handles the body. Even a resolved 5/5 Wan Shi Tong dies to Hex or Day of Black Sun X=2; spend Tidebinder on the ETB only for the denied cards.
  • Wasp as Curiosity fuel: flash Wasp at their end step, attack over their ground with Wasp + Beavers + Siren, each connection draws off our Curiosity; ninjutsu Kaito by returning a Beaver/Siren, never the Wasp that holds a blank.
Hold or fire
Pre-board you have 1 Tidebinder in 60 (in hand by turn 4 in roughly 1 game in 6), so do not slow your own Kaito or Curiosity to hold a card you usually do not have; post-board with 3 copies the hold becomes a real plan. When you do hold it, hold for: (1) a Kaito loyalty activation (-2 at our engine > 0 > +1), (2) a Wan Shi Tong ETB at X>=2, (3) Enduring Curiosity's dies-return trigger with the kill spell in the same stack, (4) THEIR Tidebinder or Wasp trigger aimed at our Kaito activation, Wan Shi Tong ETB, Curiosity or Curiosity return. Fire earlier only to counter a stun (Drowner ETB or Kaito -2) or shuffle aimed at Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong. Deploy it as a bare 3/2 at their end step only when Kaito is dead or blanked, Wan Shi Tong is out of range, and we hold a second copy or Bitter Triumph. Value the counter over the lock: in the mirror the blank lasts until they find one of ~10 answers, so prefer targets whose payoff survives Tidebinder dying. Ninjutsu: counter it when we cannot punish a blank Kaito (no Triumph, fewer than two fliers), when their Curiosity is already out, or when the 3 damage matters; otherwise let it resolve and counter the 0/-2 in main 2. First try the free plays: block their lone flier with ours, or Hex it in declare attackers. On the play: the decision is our turn 3, ninjutsu our own Kaito if a flier connected, else pass with {2}{U} up for their turn-3 ninjutsu or first activation. On the draw: their turn-3 Kaito is uncatchable; our turn-3 pass covers their turn-4 activation. Our own end step: from 6 lands on, when they have 4+ open mana, prefer Drowner or one-drop plus interaction over tapping out for a 4-drop, because Wan Shi Tong and Curiosity come in at our end step; a countered Wan Shi Tong at X>=2 is worth a delayed Curiosity, a single Kaito 0 is not.
