Our own turn: when our Kaito 0 or Wan Shi Tong ETB is the play and they hold 3+ open, keep {2}{U} up to answer their Tidebinder. Wasp: it never hits Kaito, so its jobs are (1) blank Curiosity when they are tapped below Hex/Snare mana (usually their main phase after a 4-drop) and kill it the same turn, all at instant speed; (2) blank Drowner before their next turn begins or while nothing of ours is stunned; (3) tap an animated Reef or attacker at their beginning of combat; (4) evasive flash body for our Curiosity draws. A resolved Wan Shi Tong gets Hexed, not Wasped. Never cast Wasp for the blank into open Requiting Hex or Spell Snare mana; as a body it is fine.
How many to run
The featured Goldfish 75 has 1 Tidebinder main and NONE in the sideboard (side: 3 Duress, 2 Day of Black Sun, 1 Annul, 1 Ghost Vacuum, 2 Spell Pierce, 1 Strategic Betrayal, 1 Vren, 2 Flashfreeze, 2 Faebloom Trick). If your sideboard carries the 2 Tidebinder from the Karsten Sept 3 aggregate, bring both in for 3 total; if it does not, replace the 2 Flashfreeze (dead in the mirror) with 2 Tidebinder when you build the sideboard. Keep both Wasp in. Make room by cutting 2 Spell Snare: in the mirror it counters only MV-2 spells (Drowner, Wasp, Sheriff, Triumph, Nay, a Wan Shi Tong cast at X=0) and misses Kaito, Curiosity, Tidebinder and any real Wan Shi Tong; its live targets are covered by Hex and Nay. Keep all 4 Requiting Hex: Wan Shi Tong is mana value 2 on the battlefield (X is 0 off the stack, CR 202.3e), so Hex kills it at any size for {B}; Hex also kills their Drowner or Wasp lock piece at instant speed, animated Reef/Sanctuary (land creatures, MV 0), Faerie and Rat tokens, and a lone flier in declare attackers to deny ninjutsu. Only Kaito, Curiosity, Tidebinder and Vren are out of Hex range. Keep 2 Bitter Triumph (Kaito on our turn, or any turn once blanked; Curiosity; Wan Shi Tong; Vren paying ward) and 2 Shoot the Sheriff (Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong, Drowner, their Tidebinder and Wasp; not Vren, Rat Rogue is an outlaw). Expect the opponent to show 1-3 Tidebinder depending on their build. This is a judgment call, not a rule; the rules content (Hex kills Wan Shi Tong) is not.
Review trail: confidence high, 8 disputed, 18 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Brief says 'Ward taxes ... Tidebinder's trigger.' Rejected for Vren: ward is 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays [cost]' (CR 702.21a). Tidebinder's ETB targets an activated or triggered ability on the stack, never the permanent, so Vren never becomes a target and ward does not trigger. Ward does tax Wasp, which targets the creature.
  • Possible objection: 'once their Wasp/Tidebinder blanks our Tidebinder, our lock ends.' Rejected: the lock is a continuous effect from a resolved ability with the stated duration 'for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield' (CR 611.2a), and the affected object set is fixed when it begins (611.2c); the source losing its abilities does not end it, only leaving the battlefield does (the Humility + Banisher Priest precedent).
  • Possible objection: 'a blanked Enduring Curiosity still returns when it dies.' Rejected: leaves-the-battlefield triggers look back at the object as it last existed on the battlefield (CR 603.10a); with no abilities at that moment there is no trigger. This is the exact mechanism of their own Day of Black Sun ('loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures').
  • Possible objection: 'Vren's end-step trigger only happens when X>0, so there is no free window.' Rejected: the ability has no intervening-if clause; it triggers at every end step and X is computed on resolution, so it is on the stack (and targetable) every turn.
  • Possible objection: 'Wasp can hit Kaito on our turn since he is not hexproof then.' Rejected: on our turn Kaito is not a creature ('During your turn, as long as Kaito has one or more loyalty counters on him, he's a 3/4 Ninja creature and has hexproof' applies only on his controller's turn), and Wasp targets a creature.
  • Reviewer 2 asked to delete the Ghost Vacuum note on their own dead Curiosity as 'helps us'. Partly rejected: Vacuum's second ability is '{6}, {T}, Sacrifice this artifact: Put each creature card exiled with this artifact onto the battlefield under your control with a flying counter on it. Each of them is a 1/1 Spirit in addition to its other types', so a self-exiled Curiosity can return as a 1/1 flying creature with both its triggers. Kept as a one-line low-priority note rather than a risk (reviewer 1's version).
  • Reviewer 2 wrote that Drowner is 'only summoning-sick the turn it entered'. Refined, not adopted verbatim: CR 302.6 requires continuous control since the start of the controller's most recent turn, so a Drowner flashed in on OUR turn cannot shuffle for the rest of our turn but can on their next turn; the Wasp window is 'before their next turn begins', not 'the turn it entered'.
  • Reviewer 1 listed Rat tokens and Faebloom Trick's tap as missed targets. Judged trivial: Rat tokens are Wasp-legal but blanking one token's own pump shrinks only that Rat; Faebloom's reflexive trigger is counterable but its source is an instant, so nothing is blanked. Both moved to dont_bother/body-play notes rather than the priority list.
Changes after review
  • FATAL fixed: Wan Shi Tong is mana value 2 on the battlefield (CR 202.3e); rewrote the 'Wan Shi Tong denied' combined line, added a Tidebinder trap and a Wasp trap on it, and removed the false 'Requiting Hex cannot' claim everywhere.
  • Rewrote counts_recommendation: featured sideboard has zero Tidebinder (Karsten aggregate caveat added, Flashfreeze named as the slot); cut 2 Spell Snare instead of 2 Requiting Hex, with Hex's full mirror target list; deleted the 'do not go up to more Wasp' sentence; flagged as judgment call.
  • Added target: their Tishana's Tidebinder ETB (protect our Kaito activation / Wan Shi Tong ETB / Curiosity return), priority 2.
  • Added target: their The Wondrous Wasp ETB (Wasp's Sting) aimed at our Curiosity / Wan Shi Tong / lock Tidebinder, priority 5.
  • Reordered Drowner: ETB counter (when aimed at an engine) now above the shuffle counter; rewrote shuffle timing to include Kaito -2 and second-Drowner same-turn stun sources with CR 302.6 note; removed 'in response to our attack' (stunned creatures are tapped).
  • Fixed Kaito loyalty math: 5 after countered +1, 4 after 0, 2 after -2; ranked -2 > 0 > +1; added that a blanked Kaito has no hexproof on any turn so Bitter Triumph works at instant speed.
  • Made the ninjutsu-vs-loyalty rule conditional (counter ninjutsu when we cannot punish a blank Kaito, their Curiosity is out, or damage matters) and added the free anti-ninjutsu plays (block flier with flier; Hex in declare attackers, CR 509.1h).
  • Fixed play/draw math in hold_or_fire: Kaito lands their turn 3 via ninjutsu either way; on the play the turn-3 decision is our ninjutsu vs holding; on the draw the first activation is uncatchable; added the own-end-step hold from 6 lands vs flash Wan Shi Tong/Curiosity, the own-turn hold vs their Tidebinder, and the pre-board 1-copy rarity caveat.
  • Rewrote Wasp target 1 timing: all-instant line, fire on their turn after they tap out, not 'our main phase when tapped out'.
  • Rewrote Wasp target 2 timing: responding to Drowner ETB is a valid pre-emptive window; removed the impossible 'their upkeep while they cannot pay {1}{U}'; listed the three no-response windows and added a trap about upkeep.
  • Reframed Tidebinder summary: value the counter over the lock in the mirror; full list of their answers to a 3/2 (added We Say Thee Nay!, Kaito -2 into Drowner, Strategic Betrayal, DoBS X>=3).
  • Fixed 'two untaps' to one stun counter = one skipped untap step (CR 122.1e mechanics).
  • Fixed 'only permanent answer' in how_they_win and Drowner text; listed their Tidebinder on our return trigger and post-board DoBS/Vren/Betrayal, and Sheriff/Triumph/Hex for Wan Shi Tong and Tidebinder.
  • Fixed Ghost Vacuum {6} wording (targetable, blanks nothing) and replaced the 'irrelevant' risk on target 4 with the actual {6}-mass-return note.
  • Vren: Bitter Triumph paying ward named as primary answer; Tidebinder/Wasp demoted to fallbacks; added the Curiosity exile-replacement interaction both ways (combined line C).
  • Restless Reef: dropped the mill as a benefit; value is dead land and lost deathtouch; added the redundant re-activation edge case to the Sanctuary trap.
  • Moved Faebloom Trick, resolved Wan Shi Tong (Wasp), Beaver/Siren/Faerie/Rat taps and Kaito +1 into dont_bother/body-play notes; added Annul (counters our Curiosity spell) to the counter-war line.
  • Added trap: Drowner shuffle is not always visible a turn ahead.
vs. Dimir Excruciator5.7% TB: premiumWasp: situational▼
A removal-and-discard control shell (3 Duress, 4 Requiting Hex, 3 Bitter Triumph, 2 Archenemy's Charm, 2 Day of Black Sun, Deadly Cover-Up, 4 Deceit) buys time while three discard outlets put Doomsday Excruciator into a graveyard: Winternight Stories ("discard two cards unless you discard a creature card"), Bitter Triumph's additional cost ("discard a card or pay 3 life", an instant, so it can happen at OUR end step), and Undercity Sewers' surveil 1 (plus Restless Reef milling themselves). Harvester of Misery's hand mode "{1}{B}, Discard this card" only yards Harvester itself, which matters because it hands Superior Spider-Man a second -2/-2 sweep to copy. The engine is Superior Spider-Man ({2}{U}{B}): "You may have Superior Spider-Man enter as a copy of any creature card in a graveyard" and, per its Scryfall ruling, the copied "When this creature enters" ability works, so a turn-4 Spider-Man IS a cast Excruciator: "each player exiles all but the bottom six cards of their library face down" (turn 2 Triumph or turn 3 Stories pitching Excruciator into turn 4 Spider-Man is their fastest line; the {B}{B}{B}{B}{B}{B} hardcast is the backup, often through Cavern of Souls naming Human or Demon). With both libraries at six they close by casting Emeritus of Ideation's exiled Ancestral Recall copy ("Target player draws three cards", {U}) at us, attacking with Restless Reef ("Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards"), and swinging 6/6 flyers, racing their own Excruciator upkeep draw that decks them in about three turns.
Tishana's Tidebinder
This is the best Tidebinder matchup in the format. Their whole game is one triggered ability, the Excruciator ETB, and Tidebinder is the only card in our 75 that stops it through Cavern of Souls (Cavern makes the creature SPELL uncounterable; the trigger is not a spell). Reserve Tidebinder #1 for that trigger, with two named exceptions: fire it at a Deceit or Oildeep Gearhulk discard trigger that would take the Tidebinder out of your hand, and (with a second copy in hand) at a Harvester ETB. Spare copies have real secondary jobs: Spider-Man copying Harvester or Deceit out of the yard, Restless Reef's mill trigger (game-saving once libraries are at six, and it turns the Reef into a dead land while Tidebinder lives), Emeritus's re-prepare attack trigger, Deceit's ETB, and even a ward trigger when our removal targets Emeritus or Gearhulk.
#1Doomsday Excruciator (hardcast, or Superior Spider-Man entering as a copy of it)
"When this creature enters, if it was cast, each player exiles all but the bottom six cards of their library face down." On Spider-Man the copied ETB works because Spider-Man was cast (Scryfall ruling on Superior Spider-Man: 'Any ... When this creature enters abilities of the chosen card will work').
WhyIt is their entire win condition. Countering it keeps our library intact; their Recall and Reef mills become harmless. Cavern of Souls does not protect it, and removal on Tidebinder in response does not save it (ruling 1: the ability is still countered). What you do with the body afterwards depends on WHICH body it is: a Spider-Man copy is safe to Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff (it goes to the yard as a Spider-Man card). A REAL Excruciator with a countered ETB is dangerous to kill: the Doomsday Excruciator card lands in their graveyard, which is exactly the fuel their four Spider-Men need for a fresh ETB. Leave it blanked under Tidebinder and Kaito -2 it (tap, two stun counters), or Floodpits Drowner ETB stun then '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries' (puts it back in their library, out of Spider-Man reach), or kill it only when you can Ghost Vacuum the card out of the yard or every Spider-Man is accounted for.
WhenLet the creature spell resolve. When the creature enters, the ETB trigger goes on the stack and they get priority first; when they pass, cast Tidebinder with the trigger on the stack, Tidebinder resolves, its own trigger targets the Excruciator trigger and resolves first. NEVER cast Tidebinder while the Excruciator/Spider-Man SPELL is on the stack: a spell is not an ability, the trigger does not exist yet, and Tidebinder's trigger will have nothing to hit. On Spider-Man two triggers appear at once (the library exile and Mind Swap's 'When you do, exile that card'); make them identify which is which and target the library one. Arena: hold full control on their turn 4 onward or the client passes priority through the trigger for you. Mana: see hold_or_fire for the conditional turn-3 hold; on the play we have four lands on their turn 4 and can spend one on Siren/Beavers and still hold {2}{U}.
AfterLoses: Flying and 'At the beginning of your upkeep, draw a card' for as long as Tidebinder is on the battlefield. Keeps: the body (6/6 hardcast; 4/4 for the Spider-Man copy, since 'he's a 4/4 Spider Human Hero' is part of the copy exception) and its creature types. The library exile is gone forever: killing or bouncing Tidebinder later restores flying and the upkeep draw but does not re-trigger the ETB. A second ETB needs a second Excruciator card in a graveyard (they have 3) plus another Spider-Man (they have 4), or six black mana; Deceit bouncing their own Excruciator or Archenemy's Charm returning it to hand are slow backups (a bounced Spider-Man cannot re-copy the card Mind Swap exiled). Watch their graveyard, not every Deceit.
RiskThey respond with Bitter Triumph or Archenemy's Charm on Tidebinder: the trigger is still countered (ruling 1), Excruciator just keeps its abilities. Day of Black Sun X=3 later kills Tidebinder but changes nothing about the library. The real risk is a second live ETB when we hold no second Tidebinder and no Ghost Vacuum; We Say Thee Nay! only works on the spell if they are not paying with Cavern mana.
#2Superior Spider-Man entering as a copy of Harvester of Misery
Copied "When this creature enters, other creatures get -2/-2 until end of turn." (no 'if it was cast' condition, so it always works on Spider-Man). Harvester reaches their yard via its own '{1}{B}, Discard this card' mode, a Bitter Triumph discard, Stories, or dying.
WhyA 4-mana board wipe against our Siren, Beavers, Drowner, Wasp, Faerie tokens and Tidebinder itself (3/2). Countering it saves the board and Spider-Man loses all abilities (menace) while Tidebinder lives. With a Harvester in their yard and no Excruciator, this is the T4 Spider-Man you should expect.
WhenETB on the stack after Spider-Man resolves; Tidebinder's trigger goes on top and resolves first. Two triggers again (the -2/-2 and Mind Swap's exile); target the -2/-2.
AfterLoses: menace and the copied activated ability. Keeps: 4/4 Spider Human Hero Spirit body.
#3Superior Spider-Man entering as a copy of Deceit (or a hardcast Deceit)
Copied "When this creature enters, if {U}{U} was spent to cast it, return up to one other target nonland permanent to its owner's hand" and/or "... if {B}{B} was spent to cast it, target opponent reveals their hand. You choose a nonland card from it. That player discards that card."
WhyDeceit is in their yard after every evoke, and Spider-Man's {2}{U}{B} paid as U,U,B,B turns on BOTH copied modes on a permanent 4/4 (no evoke sacrifice, since Spider-Man's evoke cost was not paid). One Tidebinder counters one trigger: take the bounce when it aims at Kaito, Curiosity or the Tidebinder holding their Excruciator down; take the discard when it would strip your last Tidebinder or counterspell (countering it also stops the hand reveal). Same logic for a 6-mana hardcast Deceit.
WhenETB triggers on the stack; they order them, we target whichever matters wherever it sits. Note the pre-emptive case: if a Deceit/Spider-Man discard trigger is on the stack and Tidebinder is the card they would take, flash it at that trigger, even when it is your only copy. You keep the 3/2 and lose nothing you were not about to lose.
AfterLoses: both ETBs (Spider-Man: also nothing else of note). Keeps: 4/4 (Spider-Man) or 5/5 (hardcast Deceit) body. The other, uncountered ETB still resolves.
#4Restless Reef (attack trigger, mainly after an Excruciator ETB has resolved)
"Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards."
WhyWith six cards left, mill four is lethal-adjacent and Tidebinder is the only card in our 75 that stops it (killing the animated Reef in response does not remove the trigger from the stack). Bonus: because Reef is a 4/4 Shark creature when Tidebinder's trigger resolves, it loses all abilities for as long as Tidebinder lives, including '{T}: Add {U} or {B}', the animation ability and the deathtouch its earlier animation granted (earlier timestamp, so removed). After end of turn it is a land with no abilities: a dead land against a deck that needs {B}{B}{B}{B}{B}{B}. Pre-ETB they sometimes attack targeting THEMSELVES to dig for Excruciator or to fill Emeritus's eight-card graveyard; with a spare Tidebinder the same counter blanks the land.
WhenDeclare attackers step, with the mill trigger on the stack, after they pass priority. Do not fire on the animation activation ({2}{U}{B}) if the goal is the ability-lock: at that moment Reef is still a land, so ruling 3 applies and nothing is blanked; countering the animation is only a 4-mana tempo hit. Arena: needs a stop on the opponent's declare attackers step or full control.
AfterLoses: mana ability, animation ability, mill trigger, deathtouch. Keeps: it stays a 4/4 Shark creature until end of turn (type and P/T from the resolved animation are not abilities), then reverts to a land that still cannot tap for mana while Tidebinder remains.
#5Emeritus of Ideation
"Whenever this creature attacks, you may exile eight cards from your graveyard. If you do, this creature becomes prepared."
WhyOnce they have cast the exiled Ancestral Recall copy, this trigger is how they get a second one. Countering it denies Recall #2 and strips Emeritus of ward {2} and flying, so Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph (Human Wizard is not an outlaw) then kills it untaxed; the Recall copy ceases to exist when Emeritus leaves (ruling: it remains in exile only 'for as long as that permanent is on the battlefield and is prepared'). Ward does not tax Tidebinder here because Tidebinder targets the ability, not the permanent. Caveat before killing Emeritus while Spider-Men remain: 'This creature enters prepared' is static, so a Spider-Man copying the Emeritus card from their yard very likely enters prepared with its own Recall copy (no ruling covers this exact combination; assume it does).
WhenDeclare attackers step with the attack trigger on the stack. If Emeritus is still prepared from entering and they have not cast Recall yet, the trigger cannot prepare it a second time (ruling: 'can't become prepared more than once at the same time'), so skip it unless you want the flying/ward strip.
AfterLoses: flying, ward {2}, the attack trigger. Keeps: 5/5 body and, crucially, its prepared status and the Ancestral Recall copy in exile (ruling: 'If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared'). Tidebinder does not stop the Recall already in exile; only a counterspell on the Recall or removing Emeritus from the battlefield does.
#6Harvester of Misery (ETB)
"When this creature enters, other creatures get -2/-2 until end of turn."
WhyIt wipes our Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers, Floodpits Drowner, Wasp, Faerie tokens and Tidebinder itself (3/2). Countering saves the board and turns Harvester into a 5/4 vanilla. Only worth Tidebinder #1 if we also hold Tidebinder #2 (or Ghost Vacuum is online) for the Excruciator.
WhenETB trigger on the stack after Harvester resolves; Tidebinder's trigger goes on top and resolves first, so the -2/-2 never happens.
AfterLoses: menace and both other abilities. Keeps: 5/4 body.
#7Deceit (evoked for {U/B}{U/B})
"When this creature enters, if {U}{U} was spent to cast it, return up to one other target nonland permanent to its owner's hand." / "When this creature enters, if {B}{B} was spent to cast it, target opponent reveals their hand. You choose a nonland card from it. That player discards that card."
WhyEvoked for two mana only one mode can be on; countering it means they paid two mana for nothing, and Deceit still dies because the evoke sacrifice trigger is already on the stack and exists independently of its source. Pick the bounce when it aims at Kaito, Curiosity or the Tidebinder holding Excruciator down; pick the discard whenever the card they would take is your Tidebinder or your only counterspell.
WhenETB triggers on the stack. They choose the order of their own triggers and may resolve the bounce/discard before the sacrifice; that changes nothing for us, we target the bounce/discard trigger wherever it sits.
AfterLoses: both ETBs and evoke text (irrelevant once on the battlefield). Keeps: nothing useful, it is sacrificed anyway. The other, uncountered ETB (if any) still resolves.
#8Ward trigger of Emeritus of Ideation (ward {2}) or Oildeep Gearhulk (ward {1})
Ward is a triggered ability (CR 702.21a): "Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays [cost]."
WhyWhen our Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff targets Emeritus/Gearhulk, the ward trigger goes on the stack. Tidebinder counters it: our removal resolves untaxed, and Emeritus/Gearhulk (creatures) lose all abilities while Tidebinder lives, moot once the removal resolves. Three mana to save two and gain a 3/2 body; a niche line for when you have exactly the mana and the kill must happen now.
WhenWard trigger on the stack, after we cast the removal and they pass priority.
AfterThe removal spell resolves. If it somehow does not, the creature is blanked (no flying, no ward, no lifelink) while Tidebinder remains.
#9M.O.D.O.K. (sideboard)
"Mental Organism, Pay 3 life: M.O.D.O.K. connives. Activate only during your turn."
WhyCountering any connive makes M.O.D.O.K. lose ALL abilities while Tidebinder lives, including the static 'Creatures your opponents control get -1/-1' that kills Siren, Beavers, Drowner and Wasp on sight. The 3 life stays paid.
WhenTheir turn, connive activation on the stack. Tidebinder enters as a 2/1 under the static and survives; once the counter resolves it is back to 3/2.
AfterLoses: the -1/-1 static, flying, lifelink, connive. Keeps: 2/2 body plus any +1/+1 counters already placed.
#10Oildeep Gearhulk (sideboard)
"When this creature enters, look at target player's hand. You may choose a card from it. If you do, that player discards that card, then draws a card."
WhySaves a card and the information; ward {1} does not tax Tidebinder since the ability, not the permanent, is targeted. Same pre-emptive rule as Deceit: if Tidebinder is the card they would take, fire it at this trigger.
WhenETB on the stack.
AfterLoses: lifelink, ward {1}. Keeps: 4/4 artifact body.
#11Wan Shi Tong, Librarian (sideboard)
"When Wan Shi Tong enters, put X +1/+1 counters on him. Then draw half X cards, rounded down."
WhyTurns an X+2 mana flash play into a 1/1 with no flying, no vigilance and no cards. Its other trigger ('Whenever an opponent searches their library') never fires: our 75 has no search effects.
WhenETB on the stack, usually at our end step since it has flash.
AfterLoses: flying, vigilance, both triggers. Keeps: 1/1 body with zero counters.
#12Quantum Riddler (sideboard, pre-Excruciator only)
"When this creature enters, draw a card."
WhyHardcast ({3}{U}{U}) it becomes a 4/6 vanilla for five. Warped for {1}{U} it is a blank 4/6 for the turn and still exiles at end step, but warp lets them cast it from exile on a later turn with a fresh ETB (CR 702.185a: 'Its owner may cast this card after the current turn has ended for as long as it remains exiled'), so against a warped Riddler this is tempo, not card denial. Countering the ETB also blanks the static 'if you would draw one or more cards, you draw that many cards plus one instead', which cannot be targeted directly. After an Excruciator ETB has resolved, let their draws happen: they are decking themselves.
WhenETB on the stack. Warp's end-step exile is a delayed trigger created while Riddler was a spell, not an ability of the permanent, so it still exiles the blanked Riddler.
AfterLoses: flying, the draw replacement, the ETB. Keeps: 4/6 body; still exiled at end step if warped, and castable again from exile later.
#13Superior Spider-Man copying our Enduring Curiosity
Copied "Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card."
WhyPre-ETB only: counter the draw with the trigger on the stack and Spider-Man is a 4/4 with no abilities while Tidebinder lives. Post-ETB let it draw; every card brings their loss closer.
WhenCombat damage step, draw trigger on the stack.
AfterLoses all copied abilities. Keeps 4/4 body.
#14Qarsi Revenant (sideboard, renew from graveyard)
"Renew, {2}{B}, Exile this card from your graveyard: Put a flying counter, a deathtouch counter, and a lifelink counter on target creature. Activate only as a sorcery."
WhyThree mana and the card are gone (the exile is a cost) and the target gets no counters. Nothing loses abilities because the source is a card in exile, not a permanent.
WhenTheir main phase with the renew ability on the stack.
AfterNo permanent is affected; the target creature keeps everything it had.
Traps
Doomsday Excruciator / Superior Spider-Man (the spell): Flash Tidebinder in response to the creature spell to 'stop it'. Casting a spell is not an ability. Tidebinder's trigger would have no legal ability to target, Tidebinder becomes a 3/2, and the ETB then resolves unopposed. Wait for the trigger.
Superior Spider-Man (Mind Swap): Counter 'enter as a copy' or the reflexive 'When you do, exile that card' trigger. Entering as a copy is a replacement effect and cannot be countered. The reflexive exile IS a trigger, but it is the wrong one: if you target it, Spider-Man loses its abilities while the copied library-exile trigger, already on the stack, still resolves. Two triggers go on the stack together; target the one that says 'each player exiles all but the bottom six'.
Doomsday Excruciator (real card, ETB countered): Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff the blanked 6/6 as soon as possible. Destroying it puts a Doomsday Excruciator CARD in their graveyard, and each of their four Superior Spider-Men can enter as a copy of it for a fresh, fully live ETB. Stun it (Kaito -2), Drowner-shuffle it into their library, or kill it only when Ghost Vacuum can exile the card in response to the Spider-Man cast. A Spider-Man COPY dies as a Spider-Man card and is safe to kill.
Deceit (evoke): Counter the evoke trigger 'When this permanent enters, if its evoke cost was paid, sacrifice it.' Legal, but it hands them a 5/5 for two mana AND the bounce/discard ETB still resolves (it is a separate ability already on the stack). Counter the bounce/discard ETB instead; the sacrifice still resolves because an ability on the stack exists independently of its source even after Deceit loses all abilities.
Emeritus of Ideation (prepared / Ancestral Recall copy): Blank Emeritus with Tidebinder or Wasp to turn off the Recall; or counter 'enters prepared'. 'This creature enters prepared' is static and the Recall is a spell cast from exile, not an ability. Scryfall ruling: 'If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared, and nothing will happen to ... the copy of its prepare spell in exile.' Only countering the Recall (We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce) or removing Emeritus from the battlefield stops it.
Cavern of Souls: Cavern-paid Spider-Man/Excruciator/Emeritus means we cannot interact. 'That spell can't be countered' is a static effect on the SPELL. Tidebinder counters the ETB trigger, which Cavern does not touch, and Ghost Vacuum can still exile the Excruciator from their yard while the Spider-Man spell is on the stack. Cavern only turns off We Say Thee Nay!/Spell Pierce on that spell.
Restless Reef ({2}{U}{B} animation): Counter the animation to blank the land. When Tidebinder's trigger resolves, Reef has not finished animating and is a land, so ruling 3 applies: the ability is countered (4 mana wasted) but Reef keeps all its abilities. The lock only happens when you counter the attack trigger while Reef is a creature.
Winternight Stories (harmonize) / Stock Up / Day of Black Sun / Deadly Cover-Up / Duress / Bitter Triumph: Tidebinder can answer their sorceries and instants, or the Triumph discard that yards Excruciator. Casting from the graveyard with harmonize is casting a spell, not an ability; sorceries and instants are spells; Triumph's discard is an additional COST paid on casting. Day of Black Sun's 'loses all abilities' is a spell effect. Only real counterspells (We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce, Negate) touch these.
Harvester of Misery (hand mode) / Qarsi Revenant (renew) / Ghost Vacuum ({6},{T}, sacrifice): Countering blanks the source. The discard, graveyard exile or sacrifice is a COST and stays paid; the source is a card in the graveyard/exile or a sacrificed artifact, not an artifact, creature or planeswalker on the battlefield, so nothing loses abilities. You only deny the one effect. (Harvester's hand mode discards Harvester itself, so countering it still leaves a Harvester in their yard for Spider-Man.)
Quantum Riddler (warp): Counter the warp delayed trigger 'exile it at the beginning of the next end step'. Counterable, but it leaves them a permanent 4/6 for {1}{U}. Whether it also loses flying is unsettled: CR 603.7d makes the source of a spell-created delayed trigger 'that spell', and no ruling says whether the permanent Riddler became counts as that source for Tidebinder's artifact/creature/planeswalker check. Assume the worst (a permanent 4/6 flyer) and do not do it. Also Riddler's 'draw that many cards plus one instead' is a replacement/static ability and cannot be targeted at all; it is only removed as collateral when you counter the ETB.
Emeritus of Ideation / Oildeep Gearhulk (ward): Ward {2}/{1} taxes Tidebinder. Ward triggers only when the permanent itself becomes a target. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, so no ward tax. Wasp does target the creature and IS taxed.
Watery Grave / Cavern of Souls / Undercity Sewers 'enters tapped': As-enters choices and enters-tapped are triggers. 'As this land enters, choose a creature type' and 'you may pay 2 life. If you don't, it enters tapped' are replacement effects; 'This land enters tapped' is static. Undercity Sewers' surveil IS a trigger but a land loses nothing and it is not worth a card.
Not worth it
Doomsday Excruciator's 'At the beginning of your upkeep, draw a card': after their ETB resolved this is the clock that decks THEM in about three turns; countering it (and stripping flying) slows their loss more than it helps us.A second Excruciator/Spider-Man ETB after the first one RESOLVED: ruling, 'A player with six or fewer cards remaining ... won't exile any cards', so it is blank; but if we countered the first, a second ETB is fully live and needs another answer.Undercity Sewers surveil 1: a land, so nothing is blanked and you trade a 3-mana card for one card of their filtering.Restless Reef animation before an Excruciator ETB: 4-mana tempo hit, no ability loss. A pre-ETB Reef attack trigger aimed at themselves is only worth a SPARE Tidebinder (it blanks a land they need for {B}x6 and denies Emeritus fuel); never Tidebinder #1.Quantum Riddler / Wan Shi Tong / Oildeep Gearhulk / Spider-Man-as-Curiosity draws after an Excruciator ETB has resolved: every card they draw brings their loss closer.Wan Shi Tong's 'Whenever an opponent searches their library' trigger: our 75 has no search effects, it never fires.Their Ghost Vacuum's '{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard': we are not a graveyard deck; blanking the Vacuum is not worth a card. (OUR Ghost Vacuum, by contrast, is the best sideboard card in the matchup, see combined_lines.)Harvester's hand mode aimed at a single 1/1 (Siren/Beavers): a 3-mana card to save a 1-drop, unless the -2/-2 is aimed at the Tidebinder locking their Excruciator's flying (then judgment).Ward trigger when our Wasp targets Emeritus: paying the {2} is cheaper than spending a Tidebinder on the ward trigger unless you want the 3/2 body anyway.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp cannot touch the Excruciator ETB (blanking the creature does not remove a trigger already on the stack), and their two relevant flyers are a 6/6 and a warded 5/5, so Wasp's default role here is a 2-mana flash flyer that pressures for Enduring Curiosity draws before the ETB. It has exactly one premium job: tapping an animated Restless Reef in their beginning of combat once libraries are at six, which both stops the mill-four attack and kills the land as a mana source for as long as Wasp lives. That window only exists on Arena if you set a stop on the opponent's beginning of combat. Never cast it into an active M.O.D.O.K.
#1Restless Reef (animated, after an Excruciator ETB has resolved)
WhyReef is a creature after '{2}{U}{B}: Until end of turn, this land becomes a 4/4 blue and black Shark creature with deathtouch' resolves. Wasp taps it so it cannot attack (no 'mills four cards' trigger) and it loses all abilities for as long as Wasp remains: mana ability, animation, mill trigger, deathtouch. After end of turn it is a land that cannot tap for mana. This is Wasp's only game-saving play in the matchup. (If you kept a Requiting Hex, it also destroys the animated Reef, MV 0, in the same window, permanently, and frees Wasp for another job.)
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, after the animation has resolved and they pass priority (both players must pass with an empty stack before attackers are declared, so the window always exists in the rules). If they animate in main phase 1, still wait for beginning of combat. Do not respond to the animation itself: Reef is not yet a creature and is not a legal target. Arena: enable the stop on opponent's beginning of combat, otherwise the client passes for you and the Reef attacks.
AfterTapped, cannot attack this turn; loses mana ability, animation ability, mill trigger and deathtouch while Wasp lives; stays a 4/4 Shark until end of turn, then a dead land.
#2Emeritus of Ideation
WhyTapping at beginning of combat stops the attack and therefore the re-prepare trigger (no second Ancestral Recall). Blanking removes flying and ward {2}, so our Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff then kills it for its printed cost, and the Recall copy disappears with it. Costs {1}{U} plus {2} for ward = 4 mana, and the blank does NOT remove prepared status or the Recall already in exile (ruling).
WhenTheir beginning of combat for the tap; any time for the blank. Pay the ward {2} or Wasp's trigger is countered (Wasp still enters as a 2/1 flyer).
AfterLoses flying, ward, attack trigger. Keeps 5/5 body, prepared status, the Ancestral Recall copy in exile.
#3Qarsi Revenant (sideboard)
Why3/3 flying, deathtouch, lifelink becomes a 3/3 vanilla our 3/2s can trade with and that no longer gains them life in the race.
WhenTheir beginning of combat to also stop an attack; end step otherwise. Keyword counters placed by a later renew after Wasp's effect began keep working (ruling 2 logic: abilities gained after the effect begins are kept), so fire Wasp after the counters land if that is the concern.
AfterLoses flying, deathtouch, lifelink, and the abilities granted by any keyword counters placed before Wasp's effect began (the counters stay on the creature and work again the moment Wasp leaves; counters placed later keep working). Keeps 3/3 body.
#4Superior Spider-Man copying one of OUR graveyard creatures (Enduring Curiosity, Floodpits Drowner, Tishana's Tidebinder) or their Harvester/Deceit
WhyA Spider-Man that copied our Curiosity is a 4/4 'Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card'; Wasp blanks it (Tidebinder can instead counter the draw trigger). Spider-Man-as-Harvester loses menace. (After an Excruciator ETB, though, their extra draws help us: leave a Curiosity copy alone.)
WhenAny time; the copy's ETB (Drowner tap+stun, Tidebinder counter, Harvester -2/-2, Deceit bounce/discard) has already resolved by the time Wasp can target the creature, so this is blank-only value.
AfterLoses all copied abilities. Keeps 4/4 body.
#5Harvester of Misery
WhyMenace to vanilla 5/4; tap once to skip an attack. Its ETB is already spent by the time Wasp can target it.
WhenTheir beginning of combat.
AfterLoses menace. Keeps 5/4.
#6Doomsday Excruciator / Spider-Man-as-Excruciator (only to survive)
WhyTap it at beginning of combat to skip one 6-point attack when that turn matters in the decking race. The blank is double-edged: it removes flying (good) but also 'At the beginning of your upkeep, draw a card', which is what decks them. Use only when the skipped attack is the difference between living and dying, and prefer to let Wasp die afterward so their upkeep draw resumes.
WhenTheir beginning of combat. It cannot stop the library exile: by the time Excruciator is a targetable creature its ETB is either still on the stack (independent of the source) or already resolved.
AfterTapped; loses flying and the upkeep draw while Wasp lives. Keeps 6/6 (or 4/4) body.
#7Deceit / Oildeep Gearhulk / Quantum Riddler / Wan Shi Tong
WhyBodies whose value was their ETB. Wasp is a one-turn tap plus a minor blank: Deceit nothing, Gearhulk loses lifelink (ward {1} tax), Riddler loses flying and its draw-plus-one static, Wan Shi Tong loses flying/vigilance but keeps its counters.
WhenTheir beginning of combat if you want the tap; otherwise treat Wasp as a flash flyer at their end step.
AfterAs listed; bodies remain.
#8Any lone blocker on OUR turn (pre-Excruciator)
WhyTap Deceit/Harvester/Emeritus (ward tax) in our beginning of combat so Kaito ninjutsu connects and Enduring Curiosity draws. Do not do this after an Excruciator ETB: Curiosity's draw is mandatory and our library is six.
WhenOur beginning of combat step.
AfterTapped blocker, minor blank.
Traps
M.O.D.O.K. (sideboard): Flash Wasp to tap and blank M.O.D.O.K. so our 1-toughness creatures live. 'Creatures your opponents control get -1/-1' applies the moment Wasp enters: Wasp is 1/0 and dies to state-based actions before anyone gets priority. Its ETB still taps the target, but the 'loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield' does nothing because the duration already ended (CR 611.2b). Answer M.O.D.O.K. with Tidebinder on a connive, Shoot the Sheriff (Villain is not an outlaw), Bitter Triumph or Annul.
Doomsday Excruciator / Superior Spider-Man: Wasp the Excruciator with its ETB on the stack to stop the library exile. A triggered ability on the stack exists independently of its source; the creature losing all abilities does not remove or counter it. Only Tidebinder counters the trigger.
Emeritus of Ideation: Blank it to remove the Ancestral Recall. Prepared status and the exiled Recall copy survive 'loses all abilities' (Scryfall ruling). Wasp only removes flying/ward and the re-prepare trigger.
Any attacking creature: Wasp after attackers are declared to remove the attacker. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared.
Harvester of Misery (ETB) / Day of Black Sun X>=2 / Requiting Hex: Wasp is a safe body to leave around. Wasp is a 2/1 with mana value 2: Harvester's ETB (-2/-2) kills it, Day of Black Sun at X=2 kills it, and Requiting Hex ('Destroy target creature with mana value 2 or less') is a clean answer they run four of. Tidebinder (MV 3) dodges Hex.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Their turn 4 Spider-Man (often uncounterable via Cavern naming Human) with an Excruciator in their yard: let it resolve, wait for the 'each player exiles all but the bottom six' trigger, Tidebinder it (make them identify it versus Mind Swap's 'exile that card'). Our turn: Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph the 4/4 (it is a Spider-Man card in the yard afterwards, safe), or Kaito -2 (tap, two stun counters) it and keep pressuring.
  • Postboard Ghost Vacuum ({1} artifact, '{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard') is the anti-Spider-Man card: with Excruciator (or Harvester) in their yard and Spider-Man on the stack, tap Vacuum in response and exile it; Mind Swap chooses as Spider-Man enters, so it copies whatever is left, often nothing. Cavern cannot stop this. It also lets you kill a real Excruciator whose ETB you countered: destroy it, then Vacuum the card before their next Spider-Man. Keep Tidebinder for the ETB whenever Vacuum is not online.
  • Deceit (evoked, {B}{B}) or Oildeep Gearhulk discard trigger on the stack when Tidebinder is the card they would take: flash Tidebinder at that trigger, even as your only copy. Needs three lands, so a turn-2 Deceit on the play beats it; that is one reason not to over-value holding Tidebinder in hand against this deck.
  • Deceit evoked at our Tidebinder or Kaito (bounce mode) with Tidebinder #2 in hand: counter the bounce ETB; the evoke sacrifice still resolves, they are down two mana and a card, we are up a 3/2.
  • Spider-Man copying Harvester (Harvester in their yard, often from its own '{1}{B}, Discard this card' mode): Tidebinder counters the copied -2/-2 ETB exactly like Harvester #1, saving Siren/Beavers/Drowner/Wasp/Faeries and Tidebinder itself; Spider-Man is a 4/4 with no abilities while Tidebinder lives.
  • Emeritus attacks after they have cast the Recall copy: Tidebinder counters the re-prepare trigger, Emeritus loses ward {2} and flying, then Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph kills it for two mana and the Recall copy in exile ceases to exist. Alternative: cast the removal first and Tidebinder the WARD trigger, same result with no tax. Before killing Emeritus, check their Spider-Man count: a Spider-Man copying the Emeritus card very likely enters prepared with a fresh Recall.
  • The Recall copy ('Target player draws three cards', {U}) is worth We Say Thee Nay! or Spell Pierce even PRE-ETB when aimed at themselves (a 3-for-1 for one mana); post-ETB, aimed at us, it is the must-counter. We Say Thee Nay! with teamwork 2 (tap Tidebinder, power 3, or Curiosity, power 4) makes it cost {4} more, which a six-card-library control deck often cannot pay in the same turn as Reef animation.
  • Postboard Strategic Betrayal ({1}{B}) exiles their graveyard (the Spider-Man fuel) plus a creature of THEIR choice; Duress takes Winternight Stories/Stock Up/Deadly Cover-Up/Day of Black Sun/Bitter Triumph. Tie-break on the draw, our turn 3 with three lands: Excruciator already in their yard, cast Betrayal (proactive, and Tidebinder stays for the next attempt); empty yard, follow the conditional hold rule instead. On the play (four lands on their turn 4) you can do both only if you skip the 1-drop.
  • If the ETB gets through: their Excruciator is now THEIR loss clock (upkeep draw + normal draw, six cards, so they lose on the fourth upkeep after the ETB). Do NOT Drowner-shuffle it post-ETB: that removes the clock and adds a card to their library. Handle the 6 damage a turn with Kaito -2 stun counters, chump blocks (Siren/Beavers/Wasp block flyers), or a Wasp tap in their beginning of combat as a last resort. Drowner shuffle is a PRE-ETB tool against a hardcast Excruciator (removes it from the battlefield without feeding Spider-Man).
  • Post-ETB self-audit for a six-card library: no Kaito 0 (its draw is mandatory if they lost life this turn), no Curiosity combat damage unless the library can afford it (draw is mandatory), no Wan Shi Tong for X above 1 (mandatory 'draw half X'), never crack a Map token (explore moves a card out of the library), and count our own Restless Reef attacks against them ('target player mills four cards') as the fastest way to end it: it cuts their clock to two turns.
  • Post-ETB defence kit: Wasp held for their animated Reef at beginning of combat (tap plus dead land), Tidebinder #2/#3 for the Reef mill trigger or Emeritus re-prepare, We Say Thee Nay!/Spell Pierce for the Recall, Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff for the Spider-Man 4/4 (not the real Excruciator card unless Vacuum is up).
  • M.O.D.O.K. postboard: never Wasp into it; wait for a connive activation on their turn and Tidebinder it, then deploy the 1-toughness creatures while Tidebinder lives, or Annul/Shoot the Sheriff it.
Hold or fire
Tidebinder #1 is a named reservation for the Excruciator ETB (hardcast or Spider-Man copy), with exactly three exceptions: (a) a Deceit or Oildeep Gearhulk discard trigger is on the stack and Tidebinder is the card they would take, fire it at that trigger; (b) a Harvester or Spider-Man-as-Harvester ETB would kill two or more of our creatures and you hold Tidebinder #2 or Ghost Vacuum is on the battlefield; (c) a Deceit bounce aimed at Kaito while you hold Tidebinder #2. Never fire it as a bare 3/2, never at the spell (wait for the trigger), never at the Excruciator upkeep draw. Once the ETB is countered, remaining copies go: live second ETB > Spider-Man-as-Harvester/Deceit ETB > Reef mill trigger (post-ETB) > Emeritus re-prepare > Deceit ETB > ward trigger to force through removal, and at their end step as a 3/2 flash beater only if you hold two and their yard has no Excruciator. Mana, on the draw: graveyards are public, so check them at the start of our turn 3. If an Excruciator card is in a graveyard, or a Harvester is and our board is wide, pass turn 3 with {2}{U} open (no Kaito ninjutsu, no 2-drop; spend leftover mana at their end step on Drowner/Wasp only if the ETB did not come). If both yards are empty of creature cards, develop normally (ninjutsu, Drowner) and start holding from our turn 4; the residual risk is a Bitter Triumph at our end step pitching Excruciator into a turn-4 Spider-Man, which needs three specific cards plus a Triumph target, and passing turn 3 every game against 3 Duress, 4 Hex and 3 Triumph costs more than that. On the play you have four lands on their turn 4, so one 1-drop is affordable while holding {2}{U}, and the hold is unconditional from their turn 4. Arena: hold full control from their turn 4 and set stops on their beginning of combat and declare attackers, or none of the trigger windows exist. Wasp: fire freely pre-ETB as an end-step flash flyer for pressure and Curiosity draws, but once an Excruciator has resolved keep one in hand for the Reef beginning-of-combat tap; never cast it into an active M.O.D.O.K., a Harvester ETB on the stack, or before their Requiting Hex is spent if you need it to stick.
How many to run
Tidebinder: 3 in the 75 and all 3 in the main against this deck. Prerequisite: the Goldfish stock sideboard in the data file has ZERO Tidebinder (Duress x3, Day of Black Sun x2, Annul, Ghost Vacuum, Spell Pierce x2, Strategic Betrayal, Vren, Flashfreeze x2, Faebloom Trick x2); only the Karsten aggregate carries 2. If yours is the Goldfish side, replace the 2 Flashfreeze (dead here: they cast no red or green spells) with 2 Tidebinder before the event. It is the only card we own that beats a Cavern-protected Spider-Man/Excruciator ETB, and copies #2/#3 have real jobs (Spider-Man-as-Harvester/Deceit, Reef mill, Emeritus, Deceit, hand-protection). Wasp: keep the stock 2, do not add more; it cannot touch the win condition, dies to Harvester/Day of Black Sun/Requiting Hex, and its one premium job (Reef) needs only one copy in hand. Sideboard plan (7 out, 7 in): OUT 2 Spell Snare (their only MV-2 spells are Bitter Triumph, Strategic Betrayal and a postboard Negate; Flashfreeze cannot target our spells; Stock Up and Stories are MV 3, evoked Deceit is MV 6, Spider-Man MV 4, the Recall copy MV 1), OUT 3 Requiting Hex (no creature card in THEIR 75 has MV 2 or less; keep 1 Hex for its live targets: an animated Restless Reef in their beginning of combat, MV 0 and destroyed permanently as a land; Wan Shi Tong, MV 2 on the battlefield; and a Spider-Man that copied one of our 1- or 2-drops, which takes the copied mana cost), OUT 2 Wan Shi Tong (a 5-mana do-little into Hex/Triumph/Day of Black Sun, and post-ETB its mandatory draw is a liability); IN 2 Tidebinder, 3 Duress, 1 Ghost Vacuum (first card in: instant-speed exile of Excruciator/Harvester from their yard in response to the Spider-Man cast, and it lets us kill a blanked real Excruciator safely), 1 Strategic Betrayal (one-shot graveyard exile, second to Vacuum). If you want the 4th Hex out, Spell Pierce is the 8th card in; its {2} tax is weak against a 26-land deck by the time the Recall matters, which is why Duress (proactive) ranks ahead of it. Keep both Shoot the Sheriff and both Bitter Triumph: Demon, Human Wizard, Spider Human Hero, Elemental Incarnation and Spirit are not outlaws, and Triumph is the unconditional answer for the one case Sheriff misses, a Spider-Man that copied our Spyglass Siren (Siren Pirate, an outlaw).
Review trail: confidence high, 6 disputed, 19 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Brief's rule 'Ward taxes Wasp's trigger and Tidebinder's trigger': rejected for Tidebinder, and both reviewers confirmed. CR 702.21a defines ward as 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays [cost].' Tidebinder targets an activated or triggered ability on the stack, never the permanent, so Emeritus's ward {2} and Gearhulk's ward {1} do not trigger against it. Wasp does target the creature and is taxed.
  • Brief's Spider-Man hint tag lists only 'When you do, exile that card' as its trigger: incomplete. Scryfall ruling on Superior Spider-Man: 'Any ... When this creature enters abilities of the chosen card will work', so a Spider-Man copying Doomsday Excruciator also puts the library-exile ETB on the stack (Spider-Man was cast, satisfying 'if it was cast'). That copied trigger is the target; the Mind Swap exile is a decoy.
  • Brief's hint that Emeritus's attack trigger is the interaction point for its card draw: rejected. The draw is Ancestral Recall, a copy of its prepare spell created in exile by the static 'enters prepared' and cast as a spell; Scryfall ruling: 'If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared, and nothing will happen to ... the copy of its prepare spell in exile.' Tidebinder/Wasp blanks do not remove it; the attack trigger only matters for the second Recall.
  • Reviewer 1's Quantum Riddler warp-trap correction ('the source is a creature on the battlefield, so Riddler loses ALL abilities including flying'): not accepted as stated. CR 603.7d says a delayed triggered ability created by a spell has 'that spell' as its source; whether the permanent the spell became counts as that source for Tidebinder's artifact/creature/planeswalker check (CR 109.2: a card type without 'card' or 'spell' means a permanent) is not settled by any ruling in the data. The trap now hedges explicitly and tells the pilot to assume the worst case (a permanent 4/6 flyer) rather than asserting either outcome.
  • Reviewer 2's 'cut only 2-3 Hex' partially accepted: 3 out, 1 kept for the animated Reef / Wan Shi Tong / Spider-Man-copying-our-1-drops cases. Fully cutting it is not wrong, but the Reef kill in beginning of combat is a real post-ETB play.
  • Reviewer 1's Qarsi Revenant counter-timestamp citation (CR 613.7c) accepted in substance but cited only as CR 613.7 plus Tidebinder ruling 2 logic, since the exact sub-letter could not be verified against a rules file here.
Changes after review
  • how_they_win: removed the false claim that Harvester's discard mode yards Excruciator (oracle: '{1}{B}, Discard THIS card'); added Bitter Triumph's 'discard a card or pay 3 life' additional cost and Reef self-mill as outlets; noted Harvester's self-discard feeds Spider-Man.
  • Tidebinder target 1: split the post-counter plan by body type. Spider-Man copy is safe to kill; a real Excruciator card in the yard is Spider-Man fuel, so stun/Drowner-shuffle/leave blanked, or kill only with Ghost Vacuum available. Added a matching trap entry.
  • Tidebinder target 1: reframed bounce/Charm recursion as slow backup; the realistic repeat threat is Spider-Man #2 plus a second yarded Excruciator (a bounced Spider-Man cannot re-copy the exiled card).
  • Added Tidebinder targets: Spider-Man entering as a copy of Harvester (priority 2), Spider-Man as Deceit with U,U,B,B turning on both copied ETBs on a permanent 4/4 (priority 3), ward trigger of Emeritus/Gearhulk when our removal targets them (priority 8), Spider-Man copying our Enduring Curiosity (priority 13).
  • Added the hand-protection rule: fire Tidebinder at a Deceit {B}{B} or Oildeep Gearhulk discard trigger when Tidebinder is the card they would take, even as the only copy (Deceit entry, Gearhulk entry, hold_or_fire, combined_lines).
  • Emeritus target: added the hedged note that a Spider-Man copying the Emeritus card very likely enters prepared with a fresh Recall (static 'enters prepared'; no ruling on the exact combination), so check Spider-Man count before killing Emeritus.
  • Deceit evoke trap: added that the bounce/discard ETB still resolves when you counter the sacrifice trigger.
  • Quantum Riddler target: corrected 'two mana for nothing' to tempo, since CR 702.185a lets them cast the warped card from exile later with a fresh ETB. Riddler warp trap: replaced the flat 'permanent 4/6 flyer' claim with an explicit hedge on the delayed-trigger source question.
  • Qarsi Revenant (Wasp) after_effect: counters are never removed; abilities from counters placed before Wasp's effect stop applying, counters placed later keep working, and everything returns when Wasp leaves.
  • Wan Shi Tong entry and dont_bother: noted its 'opponent searches' trigger never fires against our 75.
  • Restless Reef target: added the pre-ETB self-targeted mill attack as a spare-Tidebinder-only counter; dont_bother updated to match.
  • Combined lines: removed the post-ETB Drowner shuffle (it deletes their loss clock and adds a card to their library); made Drowner shuffle a pre-ETB tool; added Kaito -2 / chump / Wasp-tap as the post-ETB damage plan.
  • Combined lines: added our own Ghost Vacuum as the anti-Spider-Man card (instant-speed graveyard exile in response to the Spider-Man cast; enables safely killing a blanked real Excruciator).
  • Combined lines: added the pre-ETB Recall counter note (worth Pierce/Nay even aimed at themselves), the Strategic Betrayal vs hold tie-break, and the post-ETB self-audit (Wan Shi Tong X, Map tokens, Kaito 0, our own Reef attacks).
  • hold_or_fire: made the turn-3 hold on the draw conditional on a creature card (Excruciator, or Harvester with a wide board) being in a graveyard; named the residual end-step Bitter Triumph risk and why it is accepted; added Arena stop/full-control requirements.
  • Wasp Reef target and timing: added the Arena beginning-of-combat stop requirement and the kept-Hex alternative (destroys the animated Reef permanently).
  • counts_recommendation: fixed the arithmetic (7 out / 7 in, with an optional 8th), stated the sideboard prerequisite (Goldfish stock side has zero Tidebinder; swap the dead Flashfreeze for Tidebinder), cut only 3 Hex with live targets listed, cut 2 Wan Shi Tong, added Ghost Vacuum ahead of Strategic Betrayal, added Negate to the MV-2 list, corrected the Sheriff/outlaw note for a Spider-Man copying Spyglass Siren (Pirate) and Requiting Hex hitting Spider-Man copies of our 1-2 drops.
  • Cavern of Souls trap: noted Ghost Vacuum still works through Cavern. Sorcery trap: added Bitter Triumph's discard cost as uncounterable by Tidebinder.
  • disputed: recorded the Riddler warp source hedge, the partial Hex disagreement, and the 613.7 citation caveat.
vs. Mardu Discard4.7% TB: premiumWasp: premium▼
A red-black-white aggro deck whose whole engine is discarding cards for value: Marauding Mako grows ("Whenever you discard one or more cards, put that many +1/+1 counters on this creature"), Inti impulse-draws off every discard event ("Whenever you discard one or more cards, exile the top card of your library. You may play that card until your next end step": one card per event, not per card), and Cool but Rude at level 2 turns every single discarded card into 2 damage to us. The outlets are free and instant-speed (Iron-Shield Elf and Hardened Academic "Discard a card:" abilities) plus Inti's and Cool but Rude's attack triggers, Tersa's ETB loot, and the random three-card upkeep discard that Casey Jones's ETB sets up one turn later. Bloodghast and Moonshadow are recursive/growing 1-2 drops, and the kill is Practiced Offense giving a fat Mako double strike on turn 4-5, or, once the Class is level 2, simply dumping their hand into Elf/Academic for 2 damage per card. Removal that actually touches our creatures: 2 Requiting Hex (MV 2 or less: Wasp, Siren, Beavers, Drowner, never Tidebinder) and 2 Erode main; side 1 Shoot the Sheriff (Tidebinder and Wasp are non-outlaws), 1 Slagstorm, 3 Strategic Betrayal (we choose the creature, so it only takes Tidebinder/Wasp when that is our only creature). Deathmark (green or white only) is dead against our all-blue/black creatures. 27 of 38 nonland cards are creatures, every one of them an ETB/trigger/activated-ability engine, and nothing in the 75 has hexproof or ward.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Almost every creature they play is a body whose value is a triggered or activated ability, nothing in the 75 has hexproof or ward, and their main-deck removal barely touches Tidebinder (Requiting Hex cannot target MV 3; only 2 Erode main). The default target is Inti's first trigger: it shuts a repeatable card engine off for as long as Tidebinder lives, denies that turn's discard, and leaves you an untapped 3/2 blocker in the same combat. Casey's and Tersa's ETBs are card-neutral for them (draw 3 / discard 3 at random; loot 2), so countering them is premium only when a discard payoff (Mako, Class at level 2, Inti, Monument) is already on the table; without one you are mostly paying 3 for a 3/2. Post-board, Monument to Endurance is an artifact that gets fully blanked and becomes priority 1 when it lands. Two caveats: Inti, Tersa, Casey and Carnage are legendary, so a second copy resets the blank (legend rule: they keep the fresh one, the blanked one dies, and the effect on it ends); and Voice of Victory's static stops you casting Tidebinder on their turn at all until it is dead or blanked, which makes the our-turn windows (Hardened Academic's trigger off our Strategic Betrayal / Ghost Vacuum, Class damage off an instant-speed Elf/Academic discard) the only ones left.
#1Inti, Seneschal of the Sun
"Whenever you attack, you may discard a card. When you do, put a +1/+1 counter on target attacking creature. It gains trample until end of turn." Counter the FIRST Inti trigger you see; if it is instead the discard trigger ("Whenever you discard one or more cards, exile the top card of your library. You may play that card until your next end step."), counter that one.
WhyInti is the repeatable card engine: every discard event becomes an extra card. Countering either trigger blanks Inti for as long as Tidebinder lives, so no more impulse cards and no more attack pumps. Countering the attack trigger before they discard also denies that turn's discard entirely (no Mako growth, no Class damage). This beats the one-shot ETB counters (Casey, Tersa) as the default because it is permanent value plus a blocker, not fuel denial.
WhenTheir declare attackers step with "Whenever you attack" on the stack (Cool but Rude's attack trigger will be there too; target Inti's). Tidebinder then blocks this combat as a fresh untapped 3/2. Alternatively their main phase with the exile-top-card trigger on the stack after an Elf/Academic/Tersa discard. On the play first window is their T3 (Inti cast T2 attacks T3); on the draw it is their T4, so a T2 Inti has usually attacked once already: still counter its next trigger.
AfterLoses both triggers; keeps the 2/2 body. Cards Inti already exiled stay playable until their next end step, and counters already placed stay.
Risk(a) If they respond to Tidebinder's trigger by discarding to Elf or Academic, Inti's discard trigger goes on the stack above Tidebinder's and resolves first, netting them one last card; accept it. (b) Inti is legendary and they run two: casting Inti #2 lets them keep the fresh copy and bin the blanked one, ending the blank. The blank buys turns, not the game; when Requiting Hex (Inti is MV 2) is in hand, kill Inti and save Tidebinder for Casey/Tersa/Monument.
#2Monument to Endurance (sideboard, 2)
"Whenever you discard a card, choose one that hasn't been chosen this turn, Draw a card. / Create a Treasure token. / Each opponent loses 3 life."
WhyIt is an artifact, so countering one trigger blanks the whole Monument for as long as Tidebinder lives. Against us it is their sideboard card-advantage-plus-reach engine (3 life per discard); a blanked Monument is a 3-mana do-nothing. When it is on the table, treat it as priority 1 above Inti.
WhenThe first discard trigger after it lands, usually in their main phase or at their declare attackers when Inti/Class triggers make them discard. Each discarded card is a separate trigger; countering any one blanks the rest.
AfterLoses everything; Treasures already made and cards already drawn stay. The Treasures' own sacrifice ability is a mana ability and can never be targeted.
RiskAnnul from our side is the cheaper answer if you see it coming; Tidebinder is the answer once it has resolved. Monument is not legendary, so a second copy does not reset the blank but does need its own answer.
#3Casey Jones, Vigilante
"When Casey Jones enters, draw three cards. At the beginning of your next upkeep, discard three cards at random."
WhyCard-neutral for them (three drawn, three discarded at random one turn later), so what you deny is selection plus the fuel: three random discards are +3 Mako counters, 6 Cool but Rude damage at level 2, three Monument triggers and an Inti impulse card. Because the delayed upkeep discard is created only when this ETB resolves (CR 603.7a), countering the ETB deletes the whole discard turn. Premium when any of those payoffs is on board; with none on board, take it only if nothing better is coming, since you are paying 3 for a 3/2 that traded a trigger for very little.
WhenTheir main phase, turn 3+, with the ETB trigger on the stack and (usually) their mana tapped out. First reachable window: their T3 on the play, their T4 on the draw. Not castable while Voice of Victory's static is live.
AfterCasey loses his only ability (already spent); keeps the 4/3 body. No delayed trigger ever exists, so no upkeep discard.
RiskThey can respond by discarding to Iron-Shield Elf or Hardened Academic to squeeze one discard payoff, but the draw three still never happens. Casey is legendary but has no ongoing ability to reset, so a second Casey is just a second ETB to answer.
#4Tersa Lightshatter
"When Tersa Lightshatter enters, discard up to two cards, then draw that many cards." (also strips "Haste" and "Whenever Tersa Lightshatter attacks, if there are seven or more cards in your graveyard, exile a card at random from your graveyard. You may play that card this turn.")
WhyThe ETB is a card-neutral loot, so the value is denying up to two discards (no Mako counters, no Inti card, no Class damage) plus haste: countering it in their precombat main means a summoning-sick Tersa cannot attack the turn she is cast. Her graveyard-recursion attack trigger is gone for as long as Tidebinder lives. Premium with a payoff on board, fine without one because of the lost haste attack.
WhenTheir precombat main phase with the ETB on the stack. Do it precombat (which is when she is always cast) so the lost haste costs them the attack.
AfterLoses haste, the ETB, and the attack trigger; keeps a 3/3 body. Cards already in their graveyard are unaffected.
RiskA 3/3 body still attacks from next turn on. Tersa is legendary and they run two: Tersa #2 resets the blank (they keep the new one), so the attack-trigger shutdown is temporary.
#5Cool but Rude (level 3 trigger)
"When this Class becomes level 3, search your library for a card, put it into your hand, shuffle, then discard a card at random."
WhyDenies a tutor (usually Practiced Offense or a fourth Mako) and the random discard that would feed every payoff. Countering the trigger is better than countering the "{1}{R}: Level 3" activation: the Class is stuck at level 3 with the tutor spent forever, whereas a countered activation can just be paid again next turn.
WhenTheir main phase after the "{1}{R}: Level 3" activation has resolved and the level-3 trigger is on the stack.
AfterNothing is blanked (Cool but Rude is an enchantment). Level 2's "Whenever you discard a card, this Class deals 2 damage to each opponent" keeps working.
RiskThis is a one-shot counter, not a shutdown; only take it when no creature engine is on the stack.
#6Moonshadow
"Whenever one or more permanent cards are put into your graveyard from anywhere while this creature has a -1/-1 counter on it, remove a -1/-1 counter from this creature."
WhyThe counters stay, so a blanked Moonshadow is frozen at whatever size it has reached and also loses Menace. A 1-drop that would become a 7/7 menace stays a 1/1 or 3/3 for as long as Tidebinder lives. Not legendary, so no reset.
WhenTheir turn, with the counter-removal trigger on the stack (a discarded creature/land card or a creature dying; instants and sorceries like Practiced Offense do not trigger it). Better the earlier it is: a Moonshadow with five or six counters is the ideal freeze.
AfterLoses Menace and the growth trigger; keeps its -1/-1 counters (stuck at current size). When Tidebinder dies it resumes growing but never gets the missed counters back.
Risk"This creature enters with six -1/-1 counters on it" is a replacement effect and is not a target; only the removal trigger is. Usually a Wasp job; use Tidebinder only when the trigger is on the stack and nothing better is around.
#7Carnage, Crimson Chaos
"When Carnage enters, return target creature card with mana value 3 or less from your graveyard to the battlefield. It gains 'This creature attacks each combat if able' and 'When this creature deals combat damage to a player, sacrifice it.'"
WhyDenies a free reanimated Mako/Tersa/Inti and strips Trample from a 4/3. Only one copy in the deck.
WhenTheir main phase with the ETB on the stack, whether cast normally or via Mayhem from the graveyard (Mayhem is a way of casting and cannot be countered by Tidebinder; the ETB can).
AfterLoses Trample and the ETB; keeps 4/3. Nothing is returned.
RiskLow frequency (1 copy); do not hold Tidebinder specifically for it.
#8Hardened Academic
"Whenever one or more cards leave your graveyard, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control." (the "Discard a card: This creature gains lifelink until end of turn" activation is a paid-cost trap, see traps)
WhyBlanking Academic removes Flying, haste, the free instant-speed discard outlet and the grow trigger. Normally a Wasp job, but this trigger has an OUR-TURN window: cast Strategic Betrayal (they exile their graveyard) or activate Ghost Vacuum and the trigger goes on the stack in our main phase, where Tidebinder is castable even under Voice of Victory. Once Cool but Rude is level 2, the outlet itself is the burn engine, so this jumps up the list.
WhenOur turn: in response to the trigger off our Strategic Betrayal or Ghost Vacuum activation. Their turn: when Bloodghast returns, Tersa exiles a card, Practiced Offense is flashed back, or Carnage is Mayhem-cast from the graveyard, with the counter trigger on the stack.
AfterLoses Flying, haste, the outlet, the trigger; keeps 2/1 and any counters.
RiskLow; not legendary, so no reset. Prefer Wasp (2 mana) when Wasp is free and Voice is not out.
#9Iron-Shield Elf (only paired with our removal)
"Discard a card: This creature gains indestructible until end of turn. Tap it."
WhyThe discard is a cost, already paid, so all their discard payoffs already triggered; countering it alone is poor. It becomes good only as a kill: we target Elf with Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff / Requiting Hex, they discard for indestructible, Tidebinder counters it, Elf dies and their discarded card is gone.
WhenTheir activation on the stack in response to our removal (either turn; on our turn this works under Voice). Our removal must still be on the stack below it.
AfterLoses the outlet; keeps 3/1 if it somehow survives. Indestructible granted by an earlier activation this turn is also removed (Tidebinder's effect has the later timestamp, CR 613.4 layer 6 timestamp order).
RiskCosts 4-5 mana total (Hex + Tidebinder = 4; Triumph/Sheriff + Tidebinder = 5) for one 2-drop, and the discard they paid still fed Mako/Inti/Class; only take it when the game is about Elf being the last blocker/attacker or the last free outlet under a level-2 Class.
#10Cool but Rude (level 2 activation)
"{1}{R}: Level 2"
WhyLevel-up is an activated ability and can be countered: they lose two mana and a turn of tempo before the 2-damage-per-discard mode comes online, but they can pay again later and the Class is never blanked (enchantment). Pure tempo; take it only when they tap low on a turn you had nothing else to counter and Tidebinder wants to be a body.
WhenTheir main phase with the level-up on the stack.
AfterNothing lost; Class stays level 1.
RiskWeakest real target; a countered level-up is just a Time Ebb on two mana.
Traps
Voice of Victory (sideboard, 3): Flash Tidebinder in on their turn to counter the Mobilize trigger or 'turn off' the can't-cast clause. "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn" is a static ability: it cannot be countered, and it means you cannot even cast Tidebinder (or Wasp, or any instant) during their turn while it is live. Deal with Voice on OUR turn: Requiting Hex it (MV 2) or Wasp it at sorcery timing / our end step, then Tidebinder is live again on their turn. Mobilize is a trigger but it fires on their turn, where you cannot cast. Abilities (Ghost Vacuum, Kaito, Map tokens) still work under Voice; only spells are stopped.
Cool but Rude: Counter a Class trigger and the Class loses all abilities. It is an enchantment, not an artifact, creature or planeswalker. Every counter against it (level-ups, attack loot, 2-damage pings, the level-3 tutor) is a one-shot; the Class keeps working afterward. Annul (counter target artifact or enchantment spell) is our real answer to it.
Practiced Offense: Counter the double strike / the flashback with Tidebinder. It is a spell; flashback is a way of casting, not an ability. Only a real counterspell stops it: Spell Pierce (noncreature, unless they pay {2}), We Say Thee Nay! (unless they pay {2}, or {4} with teamwork), Flashfreeze does NOT (Offense is white). Spell Snare misses it (MV 3, also on flashback). Wasp's tap is the Tidebinder-deck answer (see Wasp).
Carnage, Crimson Chaos (Mayhem): Counter the Mayhem cast from the graveyard. Mayhem is casting, not an ability. Only the ETB trigger after it resolves is a target.
Creature returned by Carnage (its granted sacrifice trigger): Counter the 'When this creature deals combat damage to a player, sacrifice it' trigger because it is on the stack and it is a creature. Legal, and it blanks the returned creature (a returned Mako stops growing), but it also saves the creature from being sacrificed, which is exactly what they want. Let the sacrifice resolve unless the returned creature is a blanked-and-still-huge threat you would rather freeze than lose 4-6 life to once.
Bloodghast: Counter the Landfall return and blank Bloodghast. "Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, you may return this card from your graveyard to the battlefield" is counterable, but the source is a card in the graveyard, not a permanent (ruling 3): nothing loses abilities and it simply triggers again on their next land. "This creature can't block" and the conditional haste are static. Three mana to delay a 2/1 one turn is not worth it; Ghost Vacuum exiling it in response is.
Marauding Mako (cycling): Counter the cycling to stop the discard. "Cycling {2} ({2}, Discard this card: Draw a card.)" has the discard as a cost, already paid when you get to respond, so every discard payoff has already triggered; you only deny one drawn card, and the Mako in the graveyard is not a permanent so nothing is blanked. The on-battlefield growth trigger ("Whenever you discard one or more cards, put that many +1/+1 counters on this creature") IS a legitimate target when you want to blank a Mako with Tidebinder rather than Wasp.
Iron-Shield Elf / Hardened Academic activations: Counter the 'Discard a card:' activation to stop the discard. The discard is the cost; it is paid before the ability goes on the stack, so Mako, Inti, Cool but Rude and Monument have already triggered. You only deny indestructible/lifelink (plus the blank). Worth it only to make our removal stick (see target 9).
Inti, Seneschal of the Sun (reflexive trigger): Wait to counter the 'When you do, put a +1/+1 counter...' part. By the time the reflexive trigger exists they have already discarded and every payoff has triggered. Counter the parent "Whenever you attack" trigger before it resolves. (If you missed it, countering the reflexive trigger still blanks Inti, since it is still Inti's ability, but you ate the discard.)
Casey Jones, Vigilante (upkeep trigger): Counter the delayed 'discard three cards at random' at their upkeep as a punish. Legal (its source is Casey, CR 603.7d, so Casey is blanked if still on the battlefield, which is worth nothing since he has no other ability), and it does deny the Mako/Class/Monument payoffs, but it also lets them KEEP the three cards, which is normally worse for us than the discard was. Only do it if the discards would literally kill you (level 2 Class plus Monument on board). The right target is the ETB the turn before.
Moonshadow (enters with counters): Counter 'enters with six -1/-1 counters' so... anything. A replacement effect, not a triggered ability; cannot be targeted (and stopping it would give them a 7/7 anyway).
Requiting Hex / Requisition Raid (blight, spree): Counter the blight -1/-1 counter or a spree mode. Additional costs of casting a spell, not abilities.
Godless Shrine / Blood Crypt / Sacred Foundry / Starting Town / Treasure tokens: Counter the 'pay 2 life or enters tapped', the 'pay 1 life: add mana', or a Treasure sacrifice. Shock-land text is a replacement effect ('As this land enters'); Starting Town's abilities and a Treasure's sacrifice are mana abilities and cannot be targeted.
Inti / Tersa / Casey / Carnage (legendary): The blank lasts for as long as Tidebinder lives, full stop. They are legendary and the deck runs 2 Inti and 2 Tersa. Casting a second copy lets them keep the fresh one under the legend rule; the blanked copy goes to the graveyard and the effect on it ends. Expect this from a competent pilot: the blank buys turns, not the game. Kill Inti with Requiting Hex when you can and save the blank for non-legendary engines (Mako, Academic, Elf, Moonshadow, Monument).
Not worth it
Bloodghast's Landfall return: legal but blanks nothing and recurs next land (ruling 3).Marauding Mako's cycling: discard already paid, card in graveyard cannot be blanked.Cool but Rude level 1 loot ("Whenever you attack, you may discard a card. If you do, draw a card"): one-shot, enchantment never blanks; only take it if no Inti/Casey/Tersa/Monument trigger will show up and the single discard would deal lethal Class damage.Cool but Rude level 2 damage trigger: stops exactly 2 damage once. Exception: at 2 life or less it is the only trigger that matters, and it is reachable on OUR turn (Elf/Academic discards happen at instant speed), so it works even under Voice of Victory.Tersa's attack trigger ("if there are seven or more cards in your graveyard, exile a card at random"): fine if it is what is on the stack, but far worse than her ETB; do not hold for it.Voice of Victory's Mobilize and its tokens' delayed 'sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step' trigger: both fire on their turn, where you cannot cast while Voice lives; if Voice is blanked Mobilize never triggers. Unreachable, ignore.Monument's Treasure tokens: sacrifice-for-mana is a mana ability, never targetable; the Monument trigger that made them is the target.Casey Jones's delayed upkeep discard: legal but usually helps them (they keep the cards); see traps.
The Wondrous Wasp
Their creatures are cheap bodies whose whole value is an ability (Mako's growth, Academic's flying/outlet, Moonshadow's counter removal, Inti's engine, Elf's free indestructible) and none of them has hexproof or ward, so a 2-mana flash blank always has a target. The tap matters because they only have sorcery-speed pump: Wasp at their beginning of combat on the Practiced Offense target wastes the double strike outright. Two rules shape the whole plan. First, Wasp is LEGENDARY: only one blank at a time. Casting Wasp #2 while #1 is out forces the legend rule before #2's trigger even goes on the stack; keep #2 and the old blank ends the moment #1 leaves, keep #1 and #2's trigger still taps but its 'for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield' effect never starts (same principle as Tidebinder ruling 1). So pick Wasp #1's target as the creature you want blanked for the rest of the game; the second copy is a tap, a blank-transfer, or redundancy after #1 dies. Second, the target list is state-dependent: while Cool but Rude is at level 1 or absent, Mako is #1; once the Class is level 2 (or Monument is out), every discard is 2 damage regardless of Mako's size, and the free instant-speed outlets (Iron-Shield Elf, Hardened Academic) become the burn engine, so blank the outlet first. Post-board Wasp is also the instant fix for Voice of Victory's can't-cast static, but it clears exactly one Voice. Wasp dies to Requiting Hex (MV 2) and Erode main, Slagstorm and Shoot the Sheriff side; Deathmark misses it (blue).
#1Marauding Mako (while Cool but Rude is level 1 or absent)
WhyThe main win condition. Blanking removes "Whenever you discard one or more cards, put that many +1/+1 counters on this creature" so it stops growing (existing counters stay: Wasp freezes, does not shrink). Tapping at their beginning of combat also eats an attack, which is the answer to Practiced Offense: a tapped creature cannot be declared as an attacker, so the double strike is wasted. RISK: with Elf or Academic on board they can discard any number of cards in response to Wasp's trigger for free, and Mako keeps every counter it gains before the blank lands (each discard is also 2 damage with the Class at level 2).
