Wasp Mako when their hand is small, or accept the growth for the freeze. Not legendary, so no reset. Once the Class is level 2, drop Mako to #3 behind the outlets.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, after they have cast their precombat spells (including Practiced Offense) and before attackers are declared. If Practiced Offense already resolved this turn, Wasp resolving afterward has the later timestamp and also strips the granted double strike. Our end step is the fallback for pure blank value (Mako untaps on their turn, so no tap value).
AfterLoses the growth trigger and cycling text; keeps P/T including every counter already on it. Any pump granted AFTER Wasp resolved (a later Practiced Offense) is kept (ruling 2), so Wasp does not make future Offenses fizzle, it only makes the current attack not happen. Requiting Hex still kills a frozen Mako of any size (mana value is 1 regardless of counters), as does Bitter Triumph; Shoot the Sheriff cannot (Shark Pirate is an outlaw).
#2Hardened Academic (becomes #1 once Cool but Rude is level 2 or Monument is out)
WhyTheir only flyer and a free repeatable outlet: "Flying, haste", "Discard a card: This creature gains lifelink until end of turn", "Whenever one or more cards leave your graveyard, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control." Blanked it is a ground 2/1 that cannot block Wasp or Spyglass Siren, cannot race with lifelink, and cannot feed discards for free, which under a level-2 Class is 2 damage per card in their hand at instant speed. With Enduring Curiosity out, a blanked Academic means Wasp connects every turn for a card. Not legendary.
WhenTheir beginning of combat (deny the haste attack the turn it is cast if it was cast precombat; deny a flying attack any later turn), or our end step for the blank alone. If they discard in response to Wasp's trigger, those discards happen before the blank; the blank still stops all future ones.
AfterLoses flying, haste, the outlet, the trigger; keeps 2/1 and counters.
#3Iron-Shield Elf (becomes #1-2 once Cool but Rude is level 2 or Monument is out)
WhyRemoves the free "Discard a card: This creature gains indestructible until end of turn. Tap it": under a level-2 Class that text is 2 damage per card at instant speed, and in general it is what stops our Requiting Hex, Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff and Day of Black Sun from killing it. If they activate Elf in response to Wasp's trigger, the indestructible was granted BEFORE Wasp's effect and is removed too (layer 6 timestamp order), so the response does not save it from a follow-up kill spell. Not legendary.
WhenOur turn (main phase, before our removal) or their beginning of combat to also stop a 3-power attack. Under Voice of Victory this is an our-turn play anyway.
AfterLoses the outlet (and any indestructible granted earlier); keeps 3/1.
#4Voice of Victory (sideboard)
Why"Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn" shuts off Tidebinder, Wasp, Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex and every counterspell on their turn. Wasp on OUR turn blanks the static (and Mobilize 2), reopening their turn for the whole flash plan. Requiting Hex also kills it (MV 2) and is preferred when in hand because Wasp is a single legendary blank: against two Voices, Wasp clears one and the other's static keeps the lock on, so Hex/Triumph/Day of Black Sun must kill one first.
WhenOur turn only: main phase or our end step (you cannot cast anything on theirs). Do it the turn it lands. If they kill Wasp on their turn, the static is back immediately for the rest of that turn.
AfterLoses the can't-cast static and Mobilize; keeps a 1/3 body. Warrior tokens already created are unaffected.
#5Inti, Seneschal of the Sun
WhyWhen Tidebinder is not available (on the draw before their T4, or Tidebinder already used), Wasp shuts off both Inti triggers: no impulse card off every discard, no attack counter/trample. The blank is what stops "Whenever you attack"; the tap only saves you Inti's 2 combat damage this turn (tapping Inti without blanking it would not stop the trigger firing off their other attackers). Inti is legendary and they run two: Inti #2 resets the blank, so prefer Requiting Hex on Inti when you have it and spend Wasp on a non-legendary engine.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, or our end step if you just want the engine off before their next turn.
AfterLoses both triggers; keeps 2/2. Cards already exiled by Inti remain playable until their next end step.
#6Moonshadow
WhyFreezes it at its current size and removes Menace: "Whenever one or more permanent cards are put into your graveyard... remove a -1/-1 counter" is gone for as long as Wasp lives. A 1-drop that would be a 7/7 menace stays small and blockable by Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers. Not legendary.
WhenAny time; best the turn before it would reach 5+ power. Beginning of combat if it is already big enough to matter as an attacker.
AfterLoses Menace and the growth trigger; keeps its -1/-1 counters (stuck at that size).
#7Tersa Lightshatter
WhyWasp cannot stop the ETB loot (a trigger already on the stack resolves regardless of its source losing abilities), but Wasp in response to the ETB taps her and removes Haste, so she cannot attack the turn she is cast, and "Whenever Tersa Lightshatter attacks, if there are seven or more cards in your graveyard, exile a card at random from your graveyard. You may play that card this turn" is gone while Wasp lives. Legendary: Tersa #2 resets it.
WhenIn response to her ETB in their precombat main, or their beginning of combat on a later turn once their graveyard is at 7+.
AfterLoses haste and the attack trigger; keeps 3/3.
#8Casey Jones, Vigilante (tap only)
WhyNo blank value: the ETB is already on the stack when you can respond and the upkeep discard is a delayed trigger that exists independently of Casey once created. But Casey is a 4/3, so Wasp at their beginning of combat is still a 4-damage tempo tap when nothing better is on the table and you are not saving Wasp for a real blank.
WhenTheir beginning of combat only; never in response to the ETB expecting to stop the draw.
AfterNothing meaningful lost (his only ability is spent); 4/3 body stays.
Traps
The Wondrous Wasp (second copy): Cast Wasp #2 on a second creature to have two blanks running. Wasp is a Legendary Creature. The legend rule is a state-based action checked before #2's trigger is even put on the stack, so you must bin one first. Keep #2: #1 leaves and its blank ends immediately ("for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield"), then #2 taps and blanks the new target (a blank transfer). Keep #1: #2 is already gone when its trigger resolves, so the tap happens but the loses-all-abilities effect never begins (duration already over, CR 611.2b; same logic as Tidebinder ruling 1). Two Wasps never equal two simultaneous blanks.
Any attacker: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to tap the biggest one. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat; it still deals damage. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or earlier) to stop an attack. Set a stop there on Arena/MTGO.
Practiced Offense target: Wasp in response to Practiced Offense strips the double strike. If Wasp resolves before Offense, the double strike is granted AFTER Wasp's effect began and is kept (ruling 2). The tap is the payload: a tapped creature cannot be declared as an attacker. Either respond (for the tap) or, better, let Offense resolve and Wasp at their beginning of combat: tapped AND the earlier-granted double strike is removed.
Bloodghast: Blank Bloodghast. "This creature can't block" is an ability; removing it lets Bloodghast block. You also remove a haste they only have when we are at 10 or less life. It recurs off every land anyway. Never target it.
Casey Jones, Vigilante: Wasp Casey to stop the draw three or the upkeep discard. The ETB is already on the stack when you can respond and resolves regardless; the upkeep discard is a delayed trigger that exists independently of Casey's abilities. Tidebinder is the answer to Casey's ETB; Wasp on Casey is only ever a beginning-of-combat tempo tap.
Creature returned by Carnage, Crimson Chaos: Blank the reanimated creature. It came back with "This creature attacks each combat if able" and "When this creature deals combat damage to a player, sacrifice it" granted before Wasp's effect; blanking removes those drawbacks and helps them. Blank Carnage itself only for Trample (low value).
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares (ours): Ninjutsu Kaito in by returning the unblocked Wasp. Wasp leaving the battlefield ends the blank immediately ("for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield"). Return Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers instead; same for Tidebinder.
Voice of Victory: Wasp Voice at their beginning of combat like everything else, or Wasp one Voice when two are out. You cannot cast spells on their turn while Voice's static is live, so Wasp it on our turn. And Wasp blanks exactly one Voice; a second Voice's static keeps the lock on, so kill one with Requiting Hex (or Bitter Triumph / Day of Black Sun X=2) before or instead.
Marauding Mako with counters: Wasp shrinks a big Mako. Counters stay: Wasp freezes a 6/6 Mako as a 6/6 vanilla. Requiting Hex still kills it (mana value 1 regardless of counters), as does Bitter Triumph; Shoot the Sheriff cannot (Pirate).
Inti / Tersa (legendary): Wasp on Inti or Tersa is a permanent shutdown. They run two of each; casting the second copy lets them keep the fresh, unblanked one and bin the blanked one, and Wasp's effect ends with it. Wasp is legendary itself, so you cannot even re-blank the new copy without moving your only blank.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Practiced Offense answer: let the sorcery resolve in their precombat main, then flash Wasp at their beginning of combat on the creature that got double strike/lifelink. It is tapped (cannot be declared as an attacker) and, because Wasp's effect has the later timestamp, it loses the granted double strike too. Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay! on the Offense itself (both 'unless they pay {2}', which a tapped-out T3-T4 aggro deck usually cannot) is the cheaper answer when you have it; Flashfreeze misses it (white) and Tidebinder can never touch the spell or its flashback.
  • T3 decision on the play: Kaito's ninjutsu costs {1}{U}{B}, the same three mana as Tidebinder. If you have an unblocked evasive attacker (Siren/Beavers) and Kaito in hand, ninjutsu Kaito on T3 (he enters attacking, -2 stuns their best creature, and he is a hexproof creature on our turn so their sorcery-speed answers miss him) and cast Tidebinder later. Hold three for Tidebinder on T3 only when Inti is already on board (its attack trigger is guaranteed to appear and you get the 3/2 blocker) or when you have no Kaito line. Holding for a possible Casey/Tersa with no payoff on their board is the losing line against T1 Mako/Moonshadow beats.
  • Kaito -2 into Floodpits Drowner: Kaito's -2 ("Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it") makes any creature a legal Drowner target. Drowner's "{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries" is INSTANT speed with no sorcery clause, so it works on their turn (including in response to Practiced Offense on the stunned creature, or to Erode/Hex aimed at Drowner). Drowner has vigilance, so it can attack and still shuffle. This is the permanent answer to a frozen-but-huge Mako or Moonshadow that Wasp has already blanked.
  • Floodpits Drowner as a flash blocker/stopper: flash Drowner at their beginning of combat (before attackers, same rule as Wasp: tapping an already-declared attacker does not remove it from combat). The target is tapped and stunned, so it misses this attack and stays tapped through its next untap step; Wasp on Mako meanwhile stops it growing, then Drowner shuffles it away on our turn while the stun counter is still on it.
  • Iron-Shield Elf kill: cast Bitter Triumph / Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff on Elf; when they respond with "Discard a card: This creature gains indestructible until end of turn. Tap it", flash Tidebinder and counter the activation. Elf dies and the discarded card is gone (their discard payoffs did trigger, since the discard was a cost). Wasp on Elf first (our turn) does the same more cheaply: the indestructible granted in response to Wasp is removed by Wasp's later timestamp, so the follow-up removal sticks.
  • Level-2 Class rule: the moment Cool but Rude hits level 2, stop thinking about Mako's size. Every discarded card is 2 damage, and Elf/Academic make discarding free and instant-speed, so Wasp/Tidebinder go on the outlet (Wasp blanks the 'Discard a card:' text; Requiting Hex kills either) before Mako or Inti. Tidebinder can counter one 2-damage trigger at 2 life or less, on our own turn if needed. The Class itself is answered only by Annul (counter target artifact or enchantment spell) before it resolves, or by their own Requisition Raid; we have no enchantment removal in the 75 once it is down.
  • Voice of Victory (post-board, 3 copies): the turn it lands, Requiting Hex it or Wasp it on OUR turn (main phase or our end step). Only then are Tidebinder, Bitter Triumph, Spell Snare etc. live on their turn again. Wasp clears exactly one Voice; if two are out, Hex/Triumph one and Wasp the other or the flash plan stays offline. If they kill Wasp on their turn, the lock is back for the rest of that turn. Under Voice, abilities still work: Ghost Vacuum, Kaito's loyalty abilities, Drowner's shuffle, and Tidebinder cast on our turn in response to Hardened Academic's trigger off our Strategic Betrayal / Ghost Vacuum, or to a Class damage trigger off an instant-speed Elf/Academic discard.
  • Their Strategic Betrayal (3 in side): 'Target opponent exiles a creature they control' means WE choose, so it only removes Tidebinder or Wasp when that is our only creature. Post-board, keep a second creature (Siren, Beavers, Drowner) on the battlefield whenever a blank is load-bearing, and it also exiles our graveyard (irrelevant to us).
  • Kaito lines: Kaito's -2 handles the biggest Mako for two turns while Wasp blanks the second threat (Academic/Inti); remember Wasp is one blank at a time. Ninjutsu Kaito by returning Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers, never Wasp or Tidebinder (returning them ends the blank). A blanked Hardened Academic cannot block flyers, so Wasp/Siren connect for the ninjutsu window and for Enduring Curiosity draws.
  • Removal map so Wasp/Tidebinder are held for the right things: Requiting Hex kills Mako, Inti, Elf, Academic, Moonshadow, Bloodghast (it comes back), Voice; Shoot the Sheriff CANNOT target Marauding Mako (Shark Pirate is an outlaw) but kills everything else; Bitter Triumph kills anything. Day of Black Sun with X=2 makes every MV-2-or-less creature lose all abilities then destroys them: it beats an Elf indestructible response (later timestamp), sweeps Mako/Moonshadow/Elf/Academic/Inti/Voice, but also kills our own Wasp, Siren, Beavers and Drowner; Bloodghast returns on their next land. When Hex is in hand for Mako, spend Wasp on Academic or Inti instead.
  • Duress feeds them: 'That player discards that card' is THEIR discard, so Mako grows, Inti impulses, a level-2 Class deals us 2 and Monument triggers. Duress is only clean on the play T1 (or before any payoff is on their board) to snag Cool but Rude or Practiced Offense; after that it is a liability. Our own Bitter Triumph discard is ours and is fine.
  • Graveyard hate: our Strategic Betrayal (they exile the creature of THEIR choice and their graveyard) and Ghost Vacuum turn off Bloodghast, Tersa's attack recursion, Carnage's ETB target, Mayhem and Practiced Offense flashback; Ghost Vacuum can exile Bloodghast in response to the Landfall trigger so nothing returns. Both make Hardened Academic's 'cards leave your graveyard' trigger fire on OUR turn, which is a Tidebinder window that works under Voice. After the yard is gone, Tidebinder's remaining targets are just the ETBs and Inti, so fire it more freely as a body.
  • Tidebinder at their declare attackers (countering Inti's trigger) enters untapped before blockers are declared and can block that same combat; a 3/2 eats a Mako with one counter or trades with Elf.
Hold or fire
TIDEBINDER: with 3 mana open on their turn, hold for the first of these to hit the stack: Monument's first discard trigger (post-board, when it is on the table) > Inti's first trigger (attack or discard) > Casey Jones ETB or Tersa ETB when a discard payoff (Mako, level-2 Class, Inti, Monument) is on their board > Cool but Rude's level-3 trigger > Casey/Tersa ETB with no payoff out. Do not hold past one of those for a better one; the deck is fast and the next window is a turn away. Fire it as a plain 3/2 body when (a) their hand is empty and no Inti/Monument is on board, or (b) you need the blocker to survive; never on our own main phase for tempo. T3 on the play: if you have Kaito plus an unblocked flyer, ninjutsu Kaito ({1}{U}{B}) instead of holding; hold only when Inti is already out or you have no Kaito line. On the draw the first window is their T4, so a T2 Inti has usually attacked once already; still counter its next trigger. Remember Inti/Tersa are legendary: the second copy resets the blank, so kill Inti with Requiting Hex when you can. If Voice of Victory is out and unblanked you cannot cast Tidebinder on their turn at all: kill or Wasp Voice on our turn first, or use the our-turn windows (Academic's trigger off Strategic Betrayal / Ghost Vacuum, a Class damage trigger off an instant-speed Elf/Academic discard), or cast it on our turn as a body if you must. WASP: one blank at a time (legendary), so Wasp #1 goes on the creature you want blanked all game. While Cool but Rude is level 1 or absent: fire early at their beginning of combat on Mako (or the haste Academic) as soon as it saves 3+ damage or stops a growth turn, and always before attackers are declared; Wasp Mako when their hand is small, because Elf/Academic discards in response grow it before the blank. Hold it when they have a big untapped creature plus cards in hand on T3+, because Practiced Offense is coming: let the Offense resolve, then Wasp the double-striker at beginning of combat. Once the Class is level 2 or Monument is out: the outlet (Elf or Academic) is the target, Mako is not. Post-board, the moment Voice of Victory resolves, Hex it if you can, else Wasp it on our turn; nothing else matters until it is off, and Wasp handles only one Voice. Wasp #2 is redundancy after #1 dies to Hex/Erode/Slagstorm, a pure tap while keeping #1, or a blank-transfer (keep #2, let #1 go) when a better target has appeared. Never ninjutsu Wasp or Tidebinder back to hand while their blank is doing work, and post-board keep a second creature out so their Strategic Betrayal cannot force you to exile the blanker.
How many to run
Game 1 the stock 1 Tidebinder + 2 Wasp is right (two Wasps is correct redundancy against 2 Hex + 2 Erode even though only one can blank at a time; Tidebinder is not legendary). Post-board default: bring in ONE Tidebinder for 2 total. Its premium targets are Inti (2), Monument (2, if boarded) and, with a payoff on board, Casey (2) and Tersa (2); Carnage is a 1-of and the ETBs are card-neutral one-shots. Their T1 Mako/Moonshadow and T2 Elf/Academic are answered by Requiting Hex (4 main), Wasp, Drowner, Shoot the Sheriff and Day of Black Sun, not by a 3-drop that needs a trigger on the stack, and 3 Voice of Victory in their side turns off casting it on their turn. Go to 3 Tidebinder only if they show Monument and do not show Voice. Always bring Flashfreeze x2 (counters Casey, Tersa, Inti, Mako, Carnage, Cool but Rude, Slagstorm; not Practiced Offense or Voice, which are white) and Annul x1 (Cool but Rude is an Enchantment, Class, 4 main, plus Monument: live regardless of what they show). Bring Day of Black Sun x2 (X=2 sweeps their whole early board through an Elf indestructible response, at the cost of our own 1-2 drops) and Strategic Betrayal / Ghost Vacuum (Bloodghast, Tersa recursion, Carnage, Mayhem, flashback, and each gives Tidebinder an our-turn window off Academic's trigger). Leave Duress out except possibly 1 on the play as a T1 play: after T2 their discard payoffs (Mako, Inti, Class, Monument) make every Duress a gift. Spell Pierce is fine but low priority (Offense x3, Class x4, Hex, Erode). Cuts: Wan Shi Tong x2 (slow), We Say Thee Nay! x2 (soft counter against a 1-2 drop deck and dead on their turn under Voice), then trim Enduring Curiosity to 3 and Shoot the Sheriff to 1 (cannot hit Mako) as needed. Keep Spell Snare: it hits Inti, Elf, Academic, Cool but Rude, Voice and Requiting Hex (14 MV-2 cards main).
Review trail: confidence high, 6 disputed, 25 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 2's Duress recommendation ('1-2 copies are reasonable post-board specifically for Cool but Rude / Practiced Offense'). Rejected as stated: Duress's oracle text is 'Target opponent reveals their hand... That player discards that card.' It is THEIR discard, so it triggers Marauding Mako ('Whenever you discard one or more cards'), Inti ('Whenever you discard one or more cards, exile the top card'), Cool but Rude level 2 ('Whenever you discard a card, this Class deals 2 damage to each opponent') and Monument ('Whenever you discard a card'). Kept only as a possible T1 play on the play, before any payoff is on the battlefield; Annul is the clean answer to the Class.
  • Reviewer 1's 'We Say Thee Nay! ... cannot be confirmed from the data.' Resolved rather than accepted: opponents.json carries its oracle text, 'Counter target spell unless its controller pays {2}. Counter that spell unless its controller pays {4} instead if this spell was cast using teamwork.' It does hit Practiced Offense (a sorcery spell); the page now states the pay-{2} clause.
  • Reviewer 2's 'Casey's ETB ... with no payoff on board, countering Casey's ETB gains close to nothing.' Partially accepted: reordered below Inti and flagged as premium only with a payoff out, but not demoted to dont_bother, because the drawn three can be discarded at instant speed to Elf/Academic the same turn (free 'Discard a card:' costs), so denying the ETB also denies that selection and those discards even when Casey is cast with mana tapped out.
  • Reviewer 2's 'Tapping it at beginning of combat also removes an attacker so the whenever-you-attack trigger cannot use it' was flagged as muddled, not wrong: 'Whenever you attack' (Inti) triggers once when they attack with one or more creatures regardless of Inti's status. Rewritten to say the blank stops the trigger and the tap only saves Inti's 2 damage.
  • Kept the claim that Wasp #2's trigger still taps but does not blank when Wasp #1 is kept under the legend rule: the trigger exists independently of its source once triggered (CR 603.10 / last known information), and the 'for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield' duration is already over when the effect would begin, so it does nothing (CR 611.2b), which is the same principle Tidebinder ruling 1 states for Tidebinder ('the source of the ability won't lose its abilities at all').
  • Kept 'Tidebinder's effect has the later timestamp' for indestructible granted by an earlier Elf activation: ability-adding and ability-removing effects both apply in layer 6 in timestamp order (CR 613.4, 613.7), so an earlier 'gains indestructible' is removed by a later 'loses all abilities'; a LATER grant would be kept (Tidebinder ruling 2).
Changes after review
  • Added Wasp is LEGENDARY throughout (summary, new trap, hold_or_fire, counts, Kaito line): one blank at a time; second copy is a tap, blank-transfer or redundancy; cited CR 611.2b and the Tidebinder ruling 1 analogy for why the kept-#1 case taps but does not blank.
  • Reordered Tidebinder targets: Inti's first trigger is #1 default, Monument #2 (#1 when on the table), Casey #3 and Tersa #4 flagged as card-neutral and premium only with a payoff on board; dropped the '3-for-1' framing.
  • Added the legend-rule reset caveat for Inti/Tersa (2 copies each) and Casey/Carnage to Tidebinder summary, Inti/Tersa entries, a new Tidebinder trap, Wasp Inti/Tersa entries, a new Wasp trap, and hold_or_fire.
  • Fixed Floodpits Drowner: its shuffle is instant speed with no sorcery clause and targets a creature with a stun counter; rewrote the Drowner combined line, added the Kaito -2 into Drowner shuffle line, and reworded the flash-blocker line so the pilot flashes it at beginning of combat.
  • Added the T3 Kaito ninjutsu ({1}{U}{B}) vs hold-Tidebinder tradeoff as a combined line and in hold_or_fire.
  • Added the state-dependent Wasp rule: Mako #1 only while Cool but Rude is level 1/absent; Elf/Academic outlets first once the Class is level 2 or Monument is out. Elf moved up to Wasp #3 and the outlet logic added to summary, hold_or_fire and a new combined line.
  • Added the Elf/Academic response-window risk to Wasp target #1 (Mako keeps counters gained before the blank; 2 damage each with Class level 2).

  • Revised counts: default +1 Tidebinder (2 total), 3 only with Monument shown and no Voice; Annul always in (Cool but Rude is an Enchantment, Class); Flashfreeze x2; Day of Black Sun x2; Duress out except possibly T1 on the play; named cuts.
  • Added Duress-feeds-their-payoffs point (all three reviewers missed it) as a combined line and in counts, and recorded the partial rejection of Reviewer 2's Duress suggestion in disputed.
  • Fixed how_they_win: Casey's ETB does not discard (upkeep delayed trigger does); Inti impulses rather than draws, one card per discard event; corrected the removal list vs us (Deathmark is dead, Strategic Betrayal only hits a lone creature, added side Sheriff/Slagstorm); added the level-2-Class hand-dump kill.
  • Fixed Wasp summary removal list (added Slagstorm, Shoot the Sheriff; noted Deathmark misses).
  • Removed the garbled 'Requiting Hex (no: MV 1, so Hex does hit it)' self-correction from the Mako-with-counters trap and Wasp #1 after_effect; now states Hex kills a frozen Mako at any size (MV 1), Sheriff cannot.
  • Fixed Elf-kill arithmetic to 4-5 mana and noted the paid discard still fed their payoffs.
  • Verified We Say Thee Nay! oracle text (soft counter, pay {2}/{4}) and rewrote the Practiced Offense trap and combined line to state it; noted Flashfreeze misses Offense (white) and Spell Snare misses it (MV 3).
  • Softened the Casey Wasp trap and added Casey as Wasp target #8 (beginning-of-combat tempo tap only).
  • Fixed the Wasp-on-Inti wording: the blank stops 'Whenever you attack', the tap only saves Inti's 2 damage.
  • Added Hardened Academic's OUR-TURN Tidebinder window (trigger off our Strategic Betrayal / Ghost Vacuum, castable under Voice) to target #8 and the graveyard-hate line.
  • Added trap for the Carnage-returned creature's granted sacrifice trigger (counterable, but countering it saves the creature).
  • Added Casey delayed-trigger source note (CR 603.7d) to the existing upkeep trap.
  • Added their Strategic Betrayal (we choose the creature; keep a second creature out) as a combined line and in hold_or_fire.
  • Added Treasure sacrifice (mana ability) and Mobilize token delayed sacrifice (unreachable under Voice) to traps/dont_bother.
  • Added the 2-life exception for countering a Cool but Rude level-2 damage trigger, reachable on our own turn, to dont_bother and the level-2 Class line.
  • Noted Moonshadow's trigger only counts permanent cards (Practiced Offense does not grow it) and that Monument is not legendary.
  • Added Day of Black Sun mechanics (loses all abilities then destroys, beats Elf indestructible, kills our own 1-2 drops) to the removal map.
vs. Boros Dwarves4.1% TB: premiumWasp: good▼
Cheap Dwarves plus Equipment. Leyline Axe often starts on the battlefield (turn 0); Kíli the Resourceful (T2) gets an "enduring story" once they control three artifacts/legendaries (Axe + Kíli + any 1-mana Equipment, usually T2-T3), after which the first equip each turn costs {0} and Kíli draws a card off the first Dwarf or Equipment that enters each turn ("This ability triggers only once each turn"). The beats come from one creature stacked with Axe (+1/+1, double strike, trample), Dragonfire Blade (+2/+2, hexproof from monocolored) and Doc Ock's Tentacles (+4/+4). Thorin, Mountain-king ({3}{R}, T4) is the payoff: his ETB moves every Equipment onto one creature and that creature then "deals damage equal to its power to up to one target creature", their only main-deck removal; post-board expect Erode ({W}: destroy target creature or planeswalker), Chainsaw and Shattered Acolyte. Dragonfire Blade is the card that beats us: hexproof from monocolored blanks Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex, Floodpits Drowner and The Wondrous Wasp on whoever wears it.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Best matchup in the format for Tidebinder. It is the only answer to a Cavern of Souls Thorin (Cavern stops counterspells on the creature spell, not counters on its ability), it is our only way to switch off Dragonfire Blade (counter the equip activation and the Blade, an artifact, loses all abilities including hexproof while Tidebinder lives), and it blanks Kíli, the engine. Ward never taxes it because it targets the ability, not the permanent. Two honest caveats: against a Thorin cast with ordinary mana, We Say Thee Nay! or Flashfreeze counters the whole card and is the better play; and a 3/2 dies to Thorin's damage trigger, Chainsaw, Erode or any double striker, and every blank ends the moment it leaves.
#1Thorin, Mountain-king
When Thorin enters, attach any number of target Equipment you control to target creature you control. When one or more Equipment become attached to that creature this way, that creature deals damage equal to its power to up to one target creature.
WhyOne trigger does their whole turn: a mass re-equip that dodges sorcery-speed equip costs plus their only main-deck removal, aimed at Enduring Curiosity, Beavers or a blocker. Tidebinder is the ONLY out when Thorin is cast with Cavern of Souls mana ('that spell can't be countered' protects the spell, not his ability). When they cast Thorin with ordinary mana (2 Caverns in 60), We Say Thee Nay! ({1}{U}, they cannot pay {2} on 4 lands) or Flashfreeze (side, Thorin is red) counters the spell itself, which is strictly better (no 3/4 body), and frees Tidebinder for the Blade equip or Kíli.
WhenThorin resolves, his ETB goes on the stack (often alongside Kíli's draw and Giott's loot). Flash Tidebinder with the ETB on the stack and target the ETB. Do NOT wait for the reflexive damage trigger: by then the Equipment has already moved. Watch which land they tap: Cavern's colored mana is the tell that only Tidebinder works. Mana math: on the play their T4 Thorin comes when we have 4 lands, so holding {2}{U} means no Curiosity and no ninjutsu-plus-anything that turn; on the draw we have exactly 3 lands during their T4, so holding Tidebinder means our whole T3 does nothing else.
AfterNo Equipment moves, no damage. Thorin stays as a 3/4 that has lost Trample and the (already spent) ETB. Equipment attached to him later grants normally (ruling 2). All of it comes back if Tidebinder dies.
RiskIf Kíli's trigger is also on the stack you only counter one; Thorin's is the one. Post-board, Erode in response to Tidebinder's trigger still lets the counter happen but Thorin keeps his abilities (ruling 1), and kills the 3/2.
#2Dragonfire Blade (equip activation)
Equip {4}. This ability costs {1} less to activate for each color of the creature it targets.
WhyThe Blade's 'hexproof from monocolored' is static and cannot be countered, but its equip activation can. Countering it makes the Blade (an artifact) lose ALL abilities while Tidebinder lives: no +2/+2, no hexproof, and no Equip ability either, so they cannot re-equip it (Kíli's {0} only replaces the cost of an equip ability that exists). That reopens Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex, Wasp and Drowner on their best creature. Only worth the card if you can convert: fire here ONLY when you hold mono-colored removal castable on the carrier this same turn cycle.
WhenTheir main phase, in response to the equip activation. The early window (T3) is almost always Kíli's once-per-turn free equip or a {1} equip onto Dwarven Mauler (Mauler's {2} discount); paid Blade equips otherwise cost {2}-{3} (Equip {4} minus one per color of the target). Countering the free one burns their discount for the turn, so a second equip that turn is full price.
AfterBlade stays where it was (if already attached, it stays attached there) and grants nothing. Only Thorin's ETB or Raubahn's attack trigger can move it while blank, and it still grants nothing wherever it lands. When Tidebinder leaves, every ability returns immediately, hexproof included.
RiskKill the formerly hexproof creature before Tidebinder can die. Post-board, Erode cast in response to your Sheriff/Hex kills Tidebinder, the Blade wakes up mid-stack and your mono-colored spell fizzles (illegal target); Bofur's adventure (hexproof and indestructible until end of turn) does the same job. Prefer Kaito -2 (two-color source, legal against hexproof from monocolored) or a sweeper for the kill, or bait the Erode first.
#3Kíli the Resourceful
Whenever another Dwarf or Equipment you control enters, draw a card. This ability triggers only once each turn.
WhyKíli is the engine: the draw trigger plus the static 'you may pay {0} rather than pay the equip cost of the first equip ability you activate each turn.' The static cannot be countered, but countering the draw blanks the whole creature, taking the free equip (and the Storied keyword) with it. Countering the once-per-turn trigger does not give them a second one that turn. Prefer this when Thorin is not coming, the Blade is not being equipped, and Requiting Hex is not in hand.
WhenThey cast a Dwarf or Equipment with Kíli out; the draw trigger goes on the stack. Respond with Tidebinder targeting the draw. Their own ETBs (Dáin's, Giott's loot) may sit alongside it; pick Kíli's. First possible window: their T3 on the play, their T4 on the draw.
AfterNo card drawn. Kíli becomes a 1/2 with no abilities: no more draws, no free equips while Tidebinder lives. The player keeps the enduring story (like the city's blessing, it is a designation 'for the rest of the game'), so Fíli's anthem still works if Fíli is out.
RiskKíli also dies to Requiting Hex ({B}, MV 2) and Shoot the Sheriff, so spending your one main Tidebinder here is a tempo play, not the only answer. Fine when it also stops a real draw and nothing better is coming.
#4Leyline Axe / Doc Ock's Tentacles (equip activations)
Leyline Axe: 'Equip {3}'. Doc Ock's Tentacles: 'Equip {5}'.
WhyCountering the equip blanks the Equipment (artifact) while Tidebinder lives: Axe stops granting '+1/+1 and has double strike and trample'; Tentacles stops granting '+4/+4'. If it was already attached to a creature, that creature loses the bonus on the spot. Whichever of the two carries their lethal or the double strike that would kill Curiosity is the target.
WhenTheir main phase, in response to the equip activation. Tentacles' own trigger ('Whenever a creature you control with mana value 5 or greater enters') never fires in this list (highest creature MV is 4), so Tentacles moves only via Kíli's free equip, Thorin's ETB, Raubahn's trigger, or a paid equip: {5}, or {3} onto Dwarven Mauler with his discount (Axe onto Mauler costs {1}). A paid Tentacles equip onto Mauler on T3-T4 is realistic.
AfterEquipment stays on the battlefield and stays attached where it already was, granting nothing and with no Equip ability while Tidebinder lives. Hone counters already on it: whether their +1/+0 survives the blank depends on how the CR defines hone counters, which the brief does not cover; assume the bonus MAY still apply and do not count on the blank removing it. Everything returns when Tidebinder leaves; they can equip a different Equipment meanwhile.
RiskTheir mana or free equip was spent, but the swing is smaller than Thorin/Blade. Only worth the one main copy if it prevents lethal or a double-strike hit that kills Curiosity.
#5Chainsaw (sideboard, 3)
When this Equipment enters, it deals 3 damage to up to one target creature. Also counterable: 'Whenever one or more creatures die, put a rev counter on this Equipment.'
WhyLikely to come in against us: 3 damage kills Tidebinder, Wasp, Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers, Floodpits Drowner and a fresh Enduring Curiosity (4/3). Countering the ETB saves the creature and blanks Chainsaw (artifact): no rev counters, no '+X/+0', no equip while Tidebinder lives. If you missed the ETB window, the rev-counter trigger is a second door to the same blank.
WhenChainsaw resolves, ETB on the stack targeting our creature. Flash Tidebinder, counter it. If they aim Chainsaw at nothing, let it go unless you want the blank anyway. Later, any time a creature dies with Chainsaw out, its rev trigger is another counterable window.
AfterChainsaw sits blank until Tidebinder leaves. Kíli's draw trigger off the Equipment entering still resolves unless that was your target instead.
RiskA second Chainsaw, Erode or Thorin's damage kills Tidebinder and wakes the first one up.
#6Raubahn, Bull of Ala Mhigo (attack trigger, or his ward trigger)
Whenever Raubahn attacks, attach up to one target Equipment you control to target attacking creature. Also: 'Ward, Pay life equal to Raubahn's power' (a triggered ability).
WhyTwo windows. (a) His attack trigger is a free instant-speed equip every combat. (b) When our Shoot the Sheriff, Requiting Hex, Kaito -2 or Wasp targets him, his ward trigger goes on the stack; Tidebinder counters the ward trigger, so we pay no life, Raubahn loses all abilities, and our spell resolves and kills him. Tidebinder itself is never taxed by ward because it targets the ability, not Raubahn.
When(a) Attackers declared, trigger on the stack: counter it, then kill him with a mono-black spell in the same combat. (b) Cast your removal at him, let ward trigger, flash Tidebinder targeting the ward trigger. Line (b) needs 5 mana total with Sheriff; Raubahn must not be wearing Dragonfire Blade (mono-black spells cannot target him then; Kaito -2 still can).
AfterNo Equipment moves (a) or no life paid (b). Raubahn is a 2/2 with no ward and no attack trigger while Tidebinder lives. Equipment already on him keeps its stats; anything equipped afterward grants normally.
Risk2 copies only. Blanking Raubahn does not remove a Blade's hexproof (that ability belongs to the Blade).
#7Third-copy-only bucket: Dwalin, Fíli, The Queen of Dale, Giott, Lavaspur Boots, Basilisk Collar, The Lonely Mountain
Dwalin: 'Whenever Dwalin enters or attacks, put a hone counter on each Equipment you control.' Fíli (side, 1): 'Whenever Fíli or another nontoken Dwarf you control enters, create a 2/2 red Dwarf creature token.' Queen (side, 2): 'Whenever an opponent casts their first noncreature spell each turn, you recruit.' Giott: 'Whenever Giott or another Dwarf you control enters and whenever an Equipment you control enters, you may discard a card. If you do, draw a card.' Lavaspur Boots: 'Equip {1}'. Basilisk Collar (side, 1): 'Equip {2}'. The Lonely Mountain: '{4}{R}, {T}: Create a 2/2 red Dwarf creature token... Activate only as a sorcery.'
WhyAll real but small. Dwalin: counters placed earlier stay, blank removes First strike. Fíli: blank removes the static 'creatures you control get +1/+1' (cannot be hit directly) and future tokens. Queen: blank stops our Sheriffs/Hexes feeding her, but Hex kills her for {B}. Giott: blank removes Double strike, which only matters if he carries Tentacles/Axe. Boots: blank removes haste and the ward {1} that taxes Wasp/Drowner/Kaito -2. Collar: blank removes deathtouch and lifelink. Lonely Mountain: eats their mana; the land keeps its abilities (not an artifact/creature/PW).
WhenETB or attack triggers as they hit the stack; equip activations in response; Lonely Mountain in response to the sorcery-speed activation. Post-board only, with the third Tidebinder, when none of targets 1-6 is coming this turn.
AfterCreature bodies stay (Dwalin 2/1, Fíli 2/2, Queen 2/1, Giott 1/1) with no abilities while Tidebinder lives; blanked Equipment grants nothing and cannot be equipped; tokens and counters already made stay.
RiskSpending Tidebinder here and then facing a Cavern Thorin with nothing is the most common way to lose the game.
Traps
Dragonfire Blade: Tidebinder the hexproof so we can target the creature. 'Equipped creature gets +2/+2 and has hexproof from monocolored' is a static ability of the Blade; it cannot be countered. The only hook is the Equip activation; countering that blanks the whole Blade.
Leyline Axe: Counter it entering on turn 0 or counter its double strike. 'If this card is in your opening hand, you may begin the game with it on the battlefield' is a static ability that never uses the stack, so there is nothing to counter; the stat/keyword grant is static too. Only 'Equip {3}' can be countered (Annul can counter the Axe as a spell if they cast it from hand).
Kíli the Resourceful: Counter the free equip. 'you may pay {0} rather than pay the equip cost' is a static cost alternative, not an ability on the stack. The equip activation itself (from the Equipment) is what you counter; doing so consumes the free equip for the turn. Blanking Kíli via his draw trigger also shuts it off.
Dwarven Mauler: Tidebinder has a target here. 'Equip abilities you activate that target this creature cost {2} less to activate' is static; Mauler has no activated or triggered ability. Only Wasp can blank him (or Requiting Hex kills him, MV 1).
Doc Ock's Tentacles: Wait for the 'Whenever a creature you control with mana value 5 or greater enters, you may attach' trigger. No creature in the 75 has mana value 5 or more (Thorin and Fíli are MV 4), so that trigger never happens. Tentacles moves via Kíli's free equip, Thorin's ETB, Raubahn's trigger or a paid equip ({5}, or {3} onto Mauler); counter those.
Thorin, Mountain-king: Let the ETB resolve and counter the damage trigger, keeping Tidebinder flexible. The reflexive trigger ('When one or more Equipment become attached to that creature this way, that creature deals damage...') fires after the Equipment has already moved. Countering it saves your creature but the mass equip stands. Counter the first trigger.
Thorin cast with ordinary mana: Tidebinder is the only thing that stops Thorin, so always hold it for him. Only a Cavern of Souls Thorin is uncounterable. Off normal lands, We Say Thee Nay! (they cannot pay {2} on 4 lands) or Flashfreeze (side; Thorin is red) counters the whole card. If you hold one of those and they have shown no Cavern mana, release Tidebinder for the Blade equip or Kíli.
Cavern of Souls: Cavern-cast Dwarves are safe from Tidebinder. 'that spell can't be countered' protects the creature spell only. Its ETB and other abilities are fair game. Cavern's own abilities are mana abilities and cannot be targeted at all.
Bofur, Reliable Guardian (adventure) / Erode: Counter the hexproof-and-indestructible or the destroy effect with Tidebinder. Both are spells (the Adventure half is cast as an instant). Tidebinder counters abilities only. Use We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce or Spell Snare (Bofur's adventure is MV 2 on the stack) for the adventure; Erode ({W}) needs Spell Pierce/WSTN.
Rest in Peace (sideboard): Counter the ETB and the graveyard problem is solved. You can counter 'When this enchantment enters, exile all graveyards', but the replacement effect 'If a card or token would be put into a graveyard from anywhere, exile it instead' is static and stays, and an enchantment never loses abilities to Tidebinder. RIP matters a lot: Enduring Curiosity is exiled instead of dying, so its 'When Enduring Curiosity dies... return it' never triggers and it becomes a one-shot 4/3. Answer RIP on the stack (Annul, Spell Snare MV 2, Spell Pierce, WSTN).
Sacred Foundry / Multiversal Passage / Inspiring Vantage / The Lonely Mountain (enters tapped): Counter the life payment or force it in tapped. 'As this land enters...' and 'enters tapped unless...' are replacement/static effects, not triggered abilities.
Raubahn, Bull of Ala Mhigo / Lavaspur Boots ward: Tidebinder gets taxed by ward. Ward triggers when the permanent 'becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls'. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, never the permanent, so no ward trigger and no life or mana tax. (Ward does tax Wasp, Drowner, Sheriff, Hex and Kaito -2.)
Not worth it
Dáin's Company ETB: a 2-mana dig for one card, and the blank only removes conditional lifelink from a 2/2 that Requiting Hex kills for {B}.Giott's loot for its own sake: card quality, not card advantage; Hex kills the 1/1. Only worth a third copy if Giott is the Tentacles/Axe carrier (removing Double strike).Lavaspur Boots' ward {1} when Wasp or removal targets the carrier: just pay the {1}. Tidebinder countering a {1} ward trigger is 3 mana to save 1.Rest in Peace ETB: the replacement effect stays and RIP does not lose abilities; fight it on the stack instead.Shattered Acolyte activation: the body is sacrificed either way and nothing loses abilities; only worth it if it is aimed at an enchantment-form Enduring Curiosity that is drawing you cards. Acolyte is a Warlock, so Shoot the Sheriff cannot target it (Hex can).The Lonely Mountain token: low-stakes 2/2 at sorcery speed and the land keeps its abilities; third-copy filler only.
The Wondrous Wasp
Two mana to blank Kíli at instant speed is the best thing Wasp does in any matchup, and tapping their Axe carrier at beginning of combat buys a full turn. Two caps: Dragonfire Blade's hexproof from monocolored means Wasp cannot target whoever wears it, and that is usually their best creature; and Requiting Hex kills Kíli, Giott, Dáin's, Dwalin and the Queen permanently for {B}, while a Wasp blank lasts only as long as a 2/1 survives Thorin's damage, Dwalin first strike, Giott double strike and Chainsaw. Pay Lavaspur Boots' ward {1}; Raubahn's ward costs life equal to his power, so hit him only as a 2/2.
#1Kíli the Resourceful
WhyBlanking Kíli removes both the draw trigger and the static free equip, the two things that make the deck run, without spending Tidebinder, and leaves a 2/1 flier that draws off Enduring Curiosity. Hex Kíli instead when Hex is in hand (permanent, {B}); Wasp Kíli when no Hex is available, when Hex is needed for another MV-2 body, or when you also want the evasive body.
WhenTheir upkeep or draw step, before they can cast a Dwarf or equip: Kíli is blank for their whole main phase, so no draw and no free equip that turn. Earliest: their T3 (on the play with 3 lands, on the draw with 2). Do NOT wait until a Kíli trigger is on the stack; Wasp on Kíli then does not stop the draw. Illegal if Kíli wears Dragonfire Blade; pay {1} if he wears Lavaspur Boots.
AfterKíli is a 1/2 with no abilities while Wasp lives. The player keeps the enduring story (a designation for the rest of the game), so Fíli's anthem still works, but Kíli's free equip and draws are gone. Do not ninjutsu Kaito off this Wasp while the blank is doing work; use Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers as the ninjutsu carrier.
#2The equipped attacker NOT wearing Dragonfire Blade (Leyline Axe / Tentacles carrier), pre-Thorin
WhyTapping before attackers stops the double strike, trample swing for a turn. The blank also strips keywords granted by Equipment attached BEFORE Wasp resolved (later timestamp wins): Axe's double strike and trample, Boots' haste and ward. The +X/+X from Equipment and hone counters stay (those are the Equipment's own abilities). Live mainly on T2-T3 or when they spread Equipment over two bodies; once Thorin has stacked everything on one creature, the Axe carrier IS the Blade carrier and this target stops existing (use Kaito -2, Day of Black Sun or Strategic Betrayal then).
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared, to stop the attack. If they already attacked, Wasp does not remove the creature from combat, but resolving it before blockers still removes double strike and trample from an attacker whose Axe was attached earlier, so it deals normal damage once. Pay {1} extra if it wears Lavaspur Boots.
AfterCreature tapped and stripped of its own abilities and of Equipment-granted keywords from earlier attachments, for as long as Wasp stays on the battlefield. Re-equipping the SAME Equipment to the SAME creature does nothing and gives no new timestamp (CR 701.3b), so Kíli's free equip, Raubahn's trigger or Thorin's ETB onto that body cannot restore the Axe's keywords; they must move the Axe to another creature and back (two equips, one free) or attach a DIFFERENT Equipment, which grants normally. Expect them to make a different creature the threat instead.
#3Giott, King of the Dwarves
WhyNative Double strike on a body that loves Tentacles (+4/+4 double strike is 10 damage). Blank removes Double strike and the loot trigger while Wasp lives. Hex kills him for {B}; Wasp him when Hex is not available or when he is about to swing.
WhenTheir beginning of combat or your end step. Blade-wearing Giott is untargetable.
After1/1 with no abilities. A different Equipment equipped to him afterward grants normally; re-equipping the same Axe to him does not (701.3b).
#4Dwalin, Weaponmaster
WhyTapping him before attackers skips 'Whenever Dwalin enters or attacks, put a hone counter on each Equipment you control' and the blank removes First strike, which lets your 2/1 and 3/2 bodies trade.
WhenTheir beginning of combat. The ETB trigger cannot be stopped by Wasp; only Tidebinder counters it.
After2/1 vanilla while Wasp lives; hone counters already placed keep working (they are on the Equipment).
#5The Queen of Dale (sideboard, 2)
WhyBlank her BEFORE you cast a noncreature spell and every Sheriff/Hex/Triumph that turn stops feeding her recruit trigger; Wasp itself is a creature spell, so casting it never triggers her. Two mana versus the 5-mana Tidebinder line. Hex also kills her (MV 2) if you can spare it.
WhenTheir end step or your upkeep, before your removal. If her trigger is already on the stack, Wasp does not stop it.
After2/1 with no abilities while Wasp lives; tokens already made stay.
#6Fíli the Pathfinder (sideboard, 1)
WhyBlank removes the static 'creatures you control get +1/+1' and the token trigger for as long as Wasp lives. Hex cannot kill him (MV 4); Sheriff can (Scout).
WhenTheir upkeep, before more Dwarves enter, or their beginning of combat to shrink the team before attacks.
After2/2 with no anthem, no future tokens while Wasp lives; tokens already made stay.
#7Raubahn, Bull of Ala Mhigo
WhyBlank removes 'Ward, Pay life equal to Raubahn's power' and his attach trigger, so mono-black removal hits him free afterward.
WhenOnly while he is a 2/2 (pay 2 life for ward). Once equipped, the life tax scales with power; skip him and use Tidebinder on his attack trigger or ward trigger instead.
After2/2 with no ward, no attack trigger while Wasp lives.
#8Dwarven Mauler / Dáin's Company / Dwarf tokens
WhyMauler's cost reducer and Dáin's lifelink are minor; tokens have nothing to blank. These are pure tap-a-blocker or tap-an-attacker plays when Wasp is only a tempo body.
WhenYour turn before attacks to remove a blocker, or their beginning of combat.
AfterTapped; Mauler loses the {2} equip discount, Dáin's loses lifelink, while Wasp lives.
Traps
Any creature wearing Dragonfire Blade: Wasp the Blade carrier. 'hexproof from monocolored' makes it an illegal target for Wasp (mono-blue), Floodpits Drowner, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph and Requiting Hex. Kaito's -2 (two-color source) can target it; so can Tidebinder countering the Blade's equip to switch the hexproof off. Day of Black Sun and Strategic Betrayal do not target.
Kíli the Resourceful (trigger already on the stack): Respond to Kíli's draw trigger with Wasp to stop the draw. An ability on the stack exists independently of its source; blanking Kíli does not remove or counter it. Only Tidebinder stops the draw. Wasp Kíli before their main phase instead.
Thorin, Mountain-king: Wasp Thorin in response to his ETB to stop the attach and the damage. Same rule: the ETB is already on the stack and resolves regardless, and the reflexive damage trigger is created by that resolving effect, not by Thorin's abilities. Wasp on Thorin only takes Trample from a body that already did its job.
An attacker already declared: Wasp taps it so it stops attacking. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. The blank still strips double strike and trample if the Axe was attached earlier, which is real, but the damage still comes.
Raubahn once equipped: Pay the ward and blank him. 'Ward, Pay life equal to Raubahn's power' scales with his equipped power; at 6+ power you are paying a quarter of your life for a tap. Use Tidebinder on the ward trigger instead if you must kill him.
Wasp holding a blank: Ninjutsu Kaito off the unblocked Wasp. Ninjutsu returns Wasp to hand and the blanked creature (usually Kíli) gets its abilities back at once. Ninjutsu off Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers while Wasp is doing work; bounce Wasp only when the blank no longer matters.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Tidebinder counters Thorin's ETB, then Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph on Thorin the same turn cycle; a 3/4 with no Trample is not worth protecting and the Equipment never moved. If they cast Thorin off normal lands and you hold We Say Thee Nay! or Flashfreeze, counter the spell instead and keep Tidebinder.
  • Tidebinder counters a Dragonfire Blade equip (their free Kíli equip is burned for the turn), the Blade goes blank, then Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff / Floodpits Drowner the formerly hexproof creature before Tidebinder can die. Post-board, Erode in response kills Tidebinder, wakes the Blade and fizzles your mono-colored spell, so prefer Kaito -2 (legal against hexproof from monocolored) or a sweeper for the kill, or bait Erode first.
  • Kaito -2 (tap plus two stun counters) is the removal that ignores Dragonfire Blade: Kaito is blue-black, so his ability is not from a monocolored source. Save it for the Blade carrier and use Wasp/Drowner on the other attacker. Day of Black Sun for X=2 ({2}{B}{B}) kills every MV-2-or-less body regardless of Equipment (Kíli, Dáin's, Giott, Dwalin, Raubahn, Mauler, tokens) and does not target; Thorin and Fíli need X=4.
  • Hex Kíli when Hex is in hand. Otherwise Wasp on Kíli at their upkeep (blank before their main phase), then Hex the blank Kíli when convenient; the Wasp keeps flying for Enduring Curiosity draws. Never ninjutsu Kaito off a Wasp that is holding Kíli blank; use Siren or Beavers.
  • Shoot the Sheriff (or Hex, or Kaito -2) at an equipped Raubahn without the Blade, his ward trigger ('Pay life equal to Raubahn's power') goes on the stack, Tidebinder counters the ward trigger: no life paid, Raubahn is blanked, your removal resolves. Five mana for a two-for-one on their carrier.
  • Spell Snare counters MV 2: Dáin's Company, Giott, Kíli, Dwalin, Raubahn, Chainsaw, The Queen of Dale, Shattered Acolyte, Rest in Peace and Bofur's adventure half ({1}{W}, MV 2 while on the stack as an Adventure), unless the creature was cast with Cavern of Souls mana. Annul (side, 1) and We Say Thee Nay! hit Dragonfire Blade, Leyline Axe, Tentacles, Boots, Chainsaw and Rest in Peace as spells; Cavern does not protect noncreature spells.
  • Rest in Peace kills our engine, not our graveyard: under RIP Enduring Curiosity is exiled instead of dying, so it never returns as an enchantment. Fight RIP on the stack (Annul, Spell Snare, Spell Pierce, WSTN); once it resolves, play Curiosity as a 4/3 flash blocker only when you can protect it.
  • Strategic Betrayal (side) and Day of Black Sun (side) are the non-targeting outs to a Blade carrier when Tidebinder is not in hand; Betrayal is best when they have exactly one creature, since they choose what to exile.
  • Post-board with 3 Tidebinder: first copy on the earliest convertible Dragonfire Blade equip or Kíli trigger, second held for Thorin's ETB (Cavern or no counterspell in hand), third at Chainsaw's ETB if it aims at Curiosity, Kaito's ninjutsu carrier or another Tidebinder.
Hold or fire
Mana math first. On the play, their T3 is the first live Tidebinder window (we have 3 lands): Kíli's free Blade equip or Kíli's draw. Their T4 Thorin comes when we have 4 lands, so holding {2}{U} for him means no Enduring Curiosity and no ninjutsu-plus-anything on our T4; say that cost to yourself before holding. On the draw, Tidebinder cannot answer anything before their T4 (we have only 2 lands during their T3), and holding it for their T4 means our T3 does nothing else (even a {B} Hex leaves only 2 up); decide on our T3 whether to skip development for the Thorin/Blade window or to develop and accept Tidebinder comes online their T5. Hand rule for the single main copy: fire it on the Dragonfire Blade equip ONLY if you hold mono-colored removal you can cast on the carrier this same turn cycle (Hex for MV 2 or less, Sheriff, Triumph) and the carrier is their real threat; otherwise hold for Thorin's ETB. If you hold We Say Thee Nay! or Flashfreeze and they have not shown Cavern of Souls colored mana, you do not need Tidebinder for Thorin: counter the spell and release Tidebinder for the Blade, Kíli or the Axe equip that carries their damage. Do not spend it on Kíli's draw if Requiting Hex is in hand; do spend it there if you have no other play and the draw is real. Never deploy it as a bare 3/2 before you have seen Thorin or a convertible Blade equip, except to block lethal. Post-board, count Erode: sequence kills so Erode in response does not wake the Blade (Kaito -2 or a sweeper), and know that Erode on Tidebinder in response to its own trigger still lets the counter happen but the source keeps its abilities (ruling 1). Wasp: Hex Kíli when you have Hex; otherwise Wasp Kíli at their upkeep, there is no reason to hold it since the engine is on the battlefield by T2. If Kíli is not out, hold Wasp for their beginning of combat and tap the Axe/Tentacles carrier that is not wearing the Blade (a window that closes once Thorin stacks everything on one body), or post-board blank The Queen of Dale before your removal turn. While a Wasp is holding a blank, ninjutsu Kaito off Siren or Beavers, not the Wasp.
How many to run
Tidebinder: bring both sideboard copies in (3 total, up from 1). This deck offers more premium targets than any other: a Cavern-cast Thorin ETB that nothing else in our 75 stops, equip activations on Dragonfire Blade / Leyline Axe / Tentacles that blank the Equipment, Kíli's engine trigger, and Chainsaw's ETB post-board, and it ignores ward and hexproof. Also bring the 1 Annul (Blade, Axe, Tentacles, Boots, Chainsaw and Rest in Peace are all artifact/enchantment spells). Cut 2 Wan Shi Tong (its 'whenever an opponent searches' never triggers here: Dáin's Company looks rather than searches, and Erode makes US search) and 1 Bitter Triumph (blanked by the Blade like all our mono-black removal, and the life/card cost hurts against double strikers). Do NOT cut Spell Snare (it hits 14 main-deck creatures plus Chainsaw, Queen, Acolyte, RIP and Bofur's adventure post-board) and do not cut a land (23 lands with three 3-mana flash spells to hold). Optional: 2 Flashfreeze (counters a non-Cavern Thorin, Raubahn, Dáin's, Giott, Dwalin, Chainsaw; not Kíli, Blade or Erode) for the last Bitter Triumph and one Spyglass Siren if you expect long games. Wasp: keep 2, do not add. Kíli is a fine target but also dies to Requiting Hex, the Blade carrier is immune, Raubahn taxes life, and a 2/1 dies to Chainsaw, Erode and every double striker; its value is tempo plus Curiosity draws, which two copies already provide.
Review trail: confidence high, 6 disputed, 17 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Kept: ward never taxes Tidebinder. Ward (CR 702.21b) triggers when the permanent 'becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls'; Tidebinder's trigger targets an activated or triggered ability on the stack, never the permanent, so Raubahn's 'Ward, Pay life equal to Raubahn's power' and Lavaspur Boots' 'ward {1}' never tax it. They do tax Wasp, Drowner, Sheriff, Hex and Kaito -2, which target the creature.
  • Kept: Doc Ock's Tentacles' triggered ability is dead in this list. 'Whenever a creature you control with mana value 5 or greater enters' cannot fire because the highest mana value creature in the 75 is 4 (Thorin {3}{R}, Fíli {3}{W}); the ability_tags hint listing it as live is mechanical noise. The Tidebinder hook on Tentacles is 'Equip {5}' (or {3} onto Dwarven Mauler).
  • Kept: the ability_tags omission of equip activations on Dragonfire Blade ('Equip {4}. This ability costs {1} less...'), Lavaspur Boots ('Equip {1}'), Doc Ock's Tentacles ('Equip {5}') and Chainsaw ('Equip {3}') is a regex miss; equip is an activated ability (CR 702.6a) and countering it makes the Equipment lose all abilities while Tidebinder remains.
  • Hone counters on a Tidebinder-blanked Equipment: neither reviewer 1's 'maybe rules-defined like +1/+1 counters' nor reviewer 3's 'later counters keep working' is asserted. The reminder text 'Each hone counter on an Equipment grants +1/+0 to equipped creature' reads as a granted ability (layer 6), which a blank would remove subject to timestamps, but the CR definition of hone counters is not in the brief or the deck file, so the guide hedges: assume the bonus may survive and do not build a line on the blank removing it.
  • Reviewer 2's cut list is accepted with one qualification: Spyglass Siren is not cut in the base plan (it is a ninjutsu carrier that lets Wasp keep holding a blank, per the ninjutsu-off-Wasp conflict the same reviewer raised); it is only the optional cut if Flashfreeze comes in.
  • Corrected my own citation: the Aura/Equipment re-timestamp on attachment is CR 613.7d, not 613.7c (613.7c is the zone-entry timestamp). The conclusion is unchanged: Equipment grants attached before Wasp resolves are removed, later attachments of a different Equipment are kept, and re-attaching the same Equipment to the same creature does nothing and gives no new timestamp (CR 701.3b).

vs. Orzhov Lifegain4% TB: situationalWasp: good▼
Turn 1 gain fuel (Hinterland Sanctifier "Whenever another creature you control enters, you gain 1 life", Case of the Uneaten Feast "Whenever a creature you control enters, you gain 1 life"), turn 2 payoffs (Amalia "Whenever you gain life, Amalia Benavides Aguirre explores", Starscape Cleric "Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life", Essence Channeler / Leonardo "+1/+1 counter" per gain, Lunar Convocation end-step drain), turn 3 Haliya (fuel plus "draw a card if you've gained 3 or more life this turn"), Moseo (end-step Infusion reanimation) and Zoraline (recursion on enter or attack). Every creature they cast becomes 1-3 lifegain events and each event fires every payoff, so one 1-drop can be 3 explores plus 3 drain. Their two anti-Dimir lock pieces are STATIC and untouchable by Tidebinder: Voice of Victory (3 copies, MV 2) "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn", which switches off our instant-speed game on the turn nearly all their triggers happen, and Clarion Conqueror (2) "Activated abilities of artifacts, creatures, and planeswalkers can't be activated", which turns off Kaito's loyalty abilities, Floodpits Drowner's shuffle, Map tokens and Ghost Vacuum (Restless Reef, a land, still animates). They kill by drain (Cleric, Convocation, sideboard Enduring Tenacity) and by out-grinding 1-for-1 removal with Moseo / Zoraline / Case / Emptiness recursion. Their ways to end a Tidebinder or Wasp blank: instant speed Erode (2 copies, {W}) and post-board Shoot the Sheriff (2) and Pyrrhic Strike (1, {2}{W}, kills MV 3+, so Tidebinder but not Wasp); sorcery speed Emptiness evoked for {B}{B} (2 copies, 2 mana, three -1/-1 counters), Strategic Betrayal (1 main, 1 SB), and post-board Ultima (2, "Destroy all artifacts and creatures. End the turn"). Expect a blank to last about one turn cycle.
Tishana's Tidebinder
The targets are excellent: every payoff is a CREATURE with a lifegain or recursion trigger, so a countered trigger sticks as a full blank (Moseo loses Infusion, Amalia loses ward and explore, Cleric loses the drain, Tenacity loses its drain AND its return-from-death), and Tidebinder targets the ABILITY on the stack, never Amalia herself, so her Ward-Pay 3 life does not trigger against it. The problem is timing. Voice of Victory (3 copies) forbids casting Tidebinder on their turn, which is when almost every counterable trigger happens; roughly 40% of games have a Voice on the battlefield by their turn 3 and about half by turn 6. Their cheapest undo effects (Erode for {W}, Emptiness evoked for {B}{B}) cost 1-2 mana against our 3. So: Tidebinder is premium in the games where Voice is absent or already dead, reliably good at THEIR END STEP (Moseo Infusion, Haliya draw) when we hold mana for flash Enduring Curiosity anyway, and a sorcery-speed 3/2 while an un-blanked Voice is out. Rank targets by whether the counter costs them a whole card or a mana sink, not by how famous the card is; whenever the blanked creature is worth killing, kill it the same turn while the blank is live (Amalia untaxed, Tenacity gone for good).
#1Moseo, Vein's New Dean
Infusion: "At the beginning of your end step, if you gained life this turn, return up to one target creature card with mana value X or less from your graveyard to the battlefield, where X is the amount of life you gained this turn." (also the ETB "create a 1/1 black and green Pest creature token")
WhyTheir recursion engine against our 1-for-1 removal, 3 copies, and Shoot the Sheriff cannot kill Moseo (Warlock = outlaw; only Bitter Triumph does in our main deck). Countering Infusion with its graveyard target already chosen saves that Amalia / Zoraline / Haliya / Voice outright AND blanks Moseo, which switches off every future Infusion while Tidebinder lives. The end-step timing is the window we naturally hold 4 mana for (Tidebinder or flash Curiosity, decide when the trigger appears). Countering the Pest ETB instead denies the token and blanks Moseo but saves no card.
WhenBeginning of their end step: Infusion goes on the stack with its target chosen; Tidebinder in response. Requires Voice absent, dead or Wasp-blanked. ETB alternative: their main phase as Moseo resolves, Pest trigger on the stack.
AfterLoses flying and Infusion; keeps 2/1 body. A Pest already made keeps its own "Whenever this token attacks, you gain 1 life" (separate permanent). Creatures already returned stay.
RiskPrefer Infusion (saves a card) over the ETB unless Tidebinder is very likely to survive many turns; either way follow with Bitter Triumph if you can, since Sheriff/Hex cannot kill him.
#2Starscape Cleric
Offspring ETB: "when this creature enters, create a 1/1 token copy of it"; and "Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life."
WhyThe drain is the real clock against us; each gain event is a point of damage per Cleric. Countering the offspring ETB after they sank {1}{B}+{2}{B} into it denies the token AND blanks the original before any of that turn's gains resolve (mana stays spent). Without offspring, counter its first drain trigger of a chain instead.
WhenOffspring: their main phase as Cleric resolves; the token ETB and the Sanctifier/Case/Haliya gain triggers go on the stack together, respond targeting the token trigger so Cleric is blank before any gain resolves. Drain: in response to its first "each opponent loses 1 life". Both are their-turn plays (Voice absent/blanked), except drains caused by lifegain on OUR turn (Farm Animals dying, Leonardo lifelink block).
AfterLoses flying, the drain and "This creature can't block" (so it CAN block while blanked). Keeps 2/1 body. A token that already resolved is a separate permanent and keeps its drain.
RiskOnly 2 copies main; a hardcast Cleric with no offspring dies to Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff for less.
#3Amalia Benavides Aguirre
"Whenever you gain life, Amalia Benavides Aguirre explores. Then destroy all other creatures if its power is exactly 20."
WhyFour copies, the card you see most, but the explore is incremental value (a counter and a look), and by the time we hold 3 mana on their turn she has usually explored several times already; the blank does not shrink her. Take her explore when (a) a lifegain chain is about to fire with her as the main payoff, or (b) you plan to kill her the same turn: the blank removes Ward-Pay 3 life, so Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff / Kaito -2 / Drowner are untaxed while Tidebinder lives. Ward never triggers against Tidebinder itself (it targets the trigger, not the permanent).
WhenTheir turn, right after a creature resolves and the first Sanctifier/Case/Haliya gain resolves: explore goes on the stack, Tidebinder in response (Voice absent/blanked). OUR-turn window immune to Voice: when Floodpits Drowner's ETB or Kaito -2 targets her, her Ward trigger goes on the stack; Tidebinder counters the WARD trigger (Amalia is its source, so she is blanked), you pay no life, and the Drowner shuffle afterwards is untaxed too.
AfterLoses ward, the explore trigger and the 20-power wrath while Tidebinder lives; keeps every +1/+1 counter. Explores already resolved stay. Ward returns the moment Tidebinder leaves.
RiskErode / Shoot the Sheriff on Tidebinder at instant speed restores her mid-turn; if you are going to kill her, do it with the removal already in hand, not next turn.
#4Zoraline, Cosmos Caller
"Whenever Zoraline enters or attacks, you may pay {W}{B} and 2 life. When you do, return target nonland permanent card with mana value 3 or less from your graveyard to the battlefield with a finality counter on it." (plus "Whenever a Bat you control attacks, you gain 1 life.")
WhyRecursion plus a 3/3 flying vigilance body that blocks our fliers. Let the enter/attack trigger resolve, let them PAY {W}{B} and 2 life, then counter the reflexive "When you do" trigger: mana and life stay spent, nothing returns, and Zoraline (the reflexive trigger's source) is blanked.
WhenAfter the first trigger resolves and they pay: the reflexive trigger goes on the stack targeting a graveyard card; Tidebinder then. Their main phase or declare-attackers, so Voice must be absent/blanked. Countering the first trigger (before payment) also blanks her but wastes the tax.
AfterLoses flying, vigilance, the Bat-gain and both recursion triggers; keeps 3/3. Anything already returned stays.
Risk1 copy; Bat Cleric (not an outlaw), so Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph also answer her (MV 3, Hex cannot).
#5Emptiness
"When this creature enters, if {W}{W} was spent to cast it, return target creature card with mana value 3 or less from your graveyard to the battlefield." / "When this creature enters, if {B}{B} was spent to cast it, put three -1/-1 counters on up to one target creature." Evoke {W/B}{W/B}.
WhySorcery-speed creature, 2 copies. Evoked for {W}{W} (2 mana) it is a rebuy of Amalia / Moseo / Haliya / Voice / Conqueror; evoked for {B}{B} (2 mana) the counters kill Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner, Siren or Beavers and shrink a 4/3 Curiosity to 1/0. Countering the relevant ETB wastes the whole card (the evoke sacrifice still happens) or saves our creature. Key case: when Wasp is blanking Voice, the lock is off during their turn, so a {B}{B} Emptiness aimed at Wasp is a Tidebinder target that keeps the lock off.
WhenAs Emptiness resolves, its ETB trigger(s) and (if evoked) the sacrifice trigger go on the stack; target the reanimate or the -1/-1 trigger, never the sacrifice. Their main phase: Voice absent/blanked only. A 6-mana hardcast that paid both {W}{W} and {B}{B} puts BOTH triggers on the stack; Tidebinder counters one and blanks Emptiness, the other still resolves.
AfterEvoked: Emptiness is blanked but the already-stacked sacrifice trigger still sacrifices it; they spent 2 mana for nothing. Hardcast: vanilla 3/5 while Tidebinder lives.
RiskKaito is not a creature on their turn, so the -1/-1 mode never hits him; count what it can actually kill before spending the counter.
#6Haliya, Guided by Light
"At the beginning of your end step, draw a card if you've gained 3 or more life this turn." (her ETB "Whenever Haliya or another creature or artifact you control enters, you gain 1 life" is the lesser target)
WhyThe end-step draw is the Haliya window that lines up with mana we hold at their end step anyway: countering it denies the card and blanks her (no more gain-on-enter fuel, no more draws) while Tidebinder lives. 3 copies, hardcast only.
WhenBeginning of their end step with the draw trigger on the stack (its condition is checked as it triggers and resolves; if they gained 3+ it is there). Voice absent/blanked. If they warped her for {W} she leaves at end step anyway: do not spend Tidebinder on a warped Haliya.
AfterVanilla 3/3 while Tidebinder lives; loses both triggers. Life already gained stays.
RiskRarely the best target if Moseo Infusion is also on the stack; Infusion saves a card, this denies one.
#7Enduring Tenacity (post-board only, 1 copy)
"Whenever you gain life, target opponent loses that much life." and "When Enduring Tenacity dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control. It's an enchantment."
WhyDoubles every gain into damage and normally survives removal as an enchantment. Countering its first drain blanks it; a blanked Tenacity that dies has no death trigger (leaves-the-battlefield triggers look back at the permanent as it last existed, with no abilities), so Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph then remove it permanently.
WhenIn response to its first "target opponent loses that much life" trigger (their turn unless the gain happened on ours), then kill it while Tidebinder is still on the battlefield, ideally the same turn.
AfterVanilla 4/3 while Tidebinder lives; no drain, no return. If you do not kill it before Tidebinder leaves, it is a full Tenacity again.
RiskSideboard 1-of: never hold Tidebinder waiting for it. Its return trigger is also counterable if you missed the blank, but that only keeps it dead once (source in graveyard, nothing blanked).
#8Case of the Uneaten Feast
"Solved, Sacrifice this Case: Creature cards in your graveyard gain 'You may cast this card from your graveyard' until end of turn."
WhyTheir big rebuy turn. The Case is sacrificed as a cost, so countering the ability leaves them with no Case and no permission: a clean 1-for-1 that erases a turn plan. (The end-step solve check is also a counterable trigger, but that only delays solving.)
WhenTheir main phase when they activate the Solved ability, before it resolves. Requires Voice absent/blanked; the creatures they then cast from the graveyard are spells, not abilities, so miss the activation and there is nothing left to counter.
AfterCase is an enchantment: nothing loses abilities (it is gone anyway). Creatures already cast from the graveyard stay.
RiskOnly reachable without Voice; do not hold Tidebinder waiting for it.
#9Curious Farm Animals
"{2}, Sacrifice this creature: Destroy up to one target artifact or enchantment." (its "When this creature dies, you gain 3 life" is a separate trigger)
WhyTheir only answer to an ENCHANTMENT-FORM Enduring Curiosity (1 main, 2 more in the sideboard). Counter the destroy only when Curiosity is already an enchantment (or a Map token you need is the target): if Curiosity is still a creature when destroyed it dies and its own trigger returns it as an enchantment that still draws, so countering that first activation saves only the 4/3 body. Farm Animals stays sacrificed either way.
WhenIn response to the activation. The death trigger (gain 3) triggered when they paid the cost and sits above the destroy; Tidebinder counters one or the other, not both. Take the destroy unless the 3 life is lethal-relevant through Cleric/Tenacity. Their Clarion Conqueror forbids this activation entirely.
AfterNothing loses abilities (source is in the graveyard). Curiosity survives; they still gain 3 unless that was the trigger you countered.
RiskLow frequency; a 3-mana trade for a 1-drop is only good when it saves the draw engine.
#10Body plays: Hinterland Sanctifier / Essence Channeler / Leonardo / Seam Rip / Haliya ETB gain
Sanctifier "Whenever another creature you control enters, you gain 1 life"; Channeler "Whenever you gain life, put a +1/+1 counter" and "When this creature dies, put its counters on target creature you control"; Leonardo "Whenever you gain life, put a +1/+1 counter on Leonardo"; Seam Rip "When this enchantment enters, exile target nonland permanent an opponent controls with mana value 2 or less"; Haliya's enter gain.
WhyCast Tidebinder on these only when you wanted a 3/2 anyway; the countered ability is 1 life, one counter, or a 1-drop's exile. Two of them are real when they come up: Sanctifier as their ONLY gain source on the battlefield (blank it and every payoff is switched off), and Channeler's die trigger when you kill a big one on OUR turn (counters vanish instead of moving; castable under Voice because it is your turn). Blanking Leonardo also removes lifelink, a gain source nothing else turns off. Seam Rip's ETB saves Wasp / Drowner / Siren but the Rip is an enchantment and is not blanked.
WhenIn response to the specific trigger; their-turn ones need Voice absent/blanked, the Channeler die trigger on our turn does not.
AfterSanctifier vanilla 1/2; Channeler and Leonardo keep their counters (no more growth, Leonardo no lifelink, Channeler no conditional flying/vigilance or counter transfer); Seam Rip does nothing but is not blanked.
RiskRequiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff kill each of these creatures for less; the ability is filler value.
Traps
Voice of Victory: Flash in Tidebinder to counter the lock or the Mobilize trigger, or to counter the Mobilize tokens' end-step sacrifice. "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn" is a static ability and cannot be targeted. Mobilize 2 ("Whenever this creature attacks, create two tapped and attacking 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens") IS triggered, but it triggers on their turn, when the static forbids casting Tidebinder (if Wasp has blanked Voice, there is no Mobilize left). The tokens' "Sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step" is a delayed trigger; countering it gifts them two 1/1s. Answer Voice with Requiting Hex ({B}, MV 2) / Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph on your turn, Spell Snare on the stack, or Wasp as fallback.
Clarion Conqueror: Tidebinder it to turn Kaito / Drowner back on. It has no activated or triggered abilities at all (Flying and "Activated abilities of artifacts, creatures, and planeswalkers can't be activated" are static), so Tidebinder has nothing to target. Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph kill it (Dragon, MV 3, Hex cannot); Wasp blanks it. Conqueror does NOT stop Tidebinder or Wasp, whose effects are triggered, nor Restless Reef (a land).
Emptiness (evoke): Counter the evoke sacrifice trigger. Evoke's "sacrifice it" is a triggered ability and can be countered, but doing so gifts them a 3/5 body for 2 mana while their reanimate or -1/-1 ETB still resolves. Always target the ETB effect, never the sacrifice.
Emptiness (speed and cost): Treat Emptiness as 6-mana or instant-speed removal.
