Daydream / Practiced Offense / Erode / Get Lost (and their flashback casts): Counter the blink, the pump, or the removal with Tidebinder. These are spells, not abilities; flashback is a static that permits casting. Tidebinder counters nothing here. The only hooks are a Repartee trigger from Stirring Hopesinger (target 4) or the re-ETB of the blinked creature after Daydream resolves (target 1). Use Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! for the spell itself.
Quantum Riddler / Starfield Shepherd cast for warp: Counter the warp exile trigger ('Exile this creature at the beginning of the next end step') so their 2-mana 4/6 stays. The end-step exile is a delayed triggered ability and it IS targetable, but countering it is a gift: they keep a 4/6 or 3/2 flier permanently, which is exactly what they cast Daydream to achieve. Never target it. Also, blanking a warped creature does not stop the exile: the delayed trigger was created by the warp cost when the spell was cast, it is not an ability printed on the permanent, so a blanked warped Riddler still leaves at end step (same as a dashed creature under a lose-all-abilities effect).
Quantum Riddler cast for warp (not yet Daydreamed): Counter the ETB draw of a Riddler they just warped in. It is exiled at end step regardless and they may recast it from exile later with a fresh ETB; you spent 3 mana to deny one card (two at most) from a body that was leaving anyway. If they then Daydream it in response, the Daydreamed Riddler is a NEW object and your blank is gone too. Save Tidebinder for a Riddler that is staying. (Wasp in response to the warp ETB does trim the draw from 2 to 1 when they have one or fewer cards in hand, by removing the replacement effect before the draw resolves; marginal, see dont_bother.)
Quantum Riddler: Counter the 'draw that many cards plus one' bonus draw. It is a replacement effect ('if you would draw... instead'), not a trigger. It can only be removed by blanking Riddler via its ETB (Tidebinder) or via Wasp.
Sage of the Skies: Counter the copy once it is on the stack, or expect Sage itself to be blanked. Once the trigger resolves the copy is a spell, and Tidebinder cannot counter spells. And because the trigger's source is a spell on the stack, not a permanent, the Sage that resolves keeps flying and lifelink (ruling 3).
Glen Elendra Guardian / Flitterwing Nuisance: Counter 'This creature enters with a -1/-1 counter on it' to make it enter bigger or counterless. 'Enters with' is a replacement effect, not a trigger. Nothing to target. (Their own Daydream does make it enter counterless: the +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters annihilate, and it cannot activate until it gets a new counter.)
Starfield Shepherd: Counter the tutor while Wan Shi Tong, Librarian is on our battlefield. Shepherd's search is an opponent searching their library: Wan Shi Tong ('Whenever an opponent searches their library, put a +1/+1 counter on Wan Shi Tong and draw a card') gets a counter and we draw. Let it resolve. Erode's basic-land search does NOT trigger Wan Shi Tong: 'Its controller may search' means the controller of the destroyed permanent, i.e. us.
Morningtide's Light (sideboard, 2): Counter the 'At the beginning of the next end step, return those cards to the battlefield' delayed trigger. 'Exile any number of target creatures' usually exiles OUR creatures as a fog; countering the return strands our own creatures in exile forever, and the source is a sorcery so nothing loses abilities. Know its other uses: it targets creatures so it triggers Repartee (counterable, target 4); exiling their OWN blanked or stunned creature is a Daydream-style reset (new object, blank and stun gone); and exiling our Wasp or Tidebinder ends the blank at once (ours return tapped at end step with a fresh ETB, so Wasp re-taps something and Tidebinder can counter whatever is on the stack then).
Rest in Peace (sideboard, 4): Counter the ETB 'exile all graveyards'. The source is an enchantment, so nothing loses abilities, and the replacement 'If a card or token would be put into a graveyard from anywhere, exile it instead' keeps working. You save only what is in the yards right now. They may board it in against Enduring Curiosity (a Curiosity that is exiled instead of dying never returns as an enchantment); Annul or Spell Pierce on the spell is the real answer.
Multiversal Passage / Hallowed Fountain / Abandoned Air Temple / Starting Town: Counter the 'enters tapped' or 'as this land enters, choose / pay 2 life' text. All static or replacement ('As this land enters'); nothing triggers.
Not worth it
Starfield Shepherd ETB on a warp cast: it leaves at end step and can be recast from exile with a fresh tutor; they run 4 Momo so denying one search rarely matters. A Daydreamed Shepherd (permanent 4/3 flier) is a legal ETB target but low value; Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff kill it.Momo's +1/+1 trigger when you hold Requiting Hex: Hex answers Momo for {B} and permanently, and Momo and Flitterwing Nuisance are the ONLY creatures in their 75 Hex can target (everything else is MV 3 or 5; warp does not change mana value), so Hex is always free for Momo.Abandoned Air Temple activation unless the pump changes combat this turn: the land keeps its ability and you traded a card for 4 of their mana.Flitterwing Nuisance activation or draw triggers: one copy, and its draws need combat damage to connect through our fliers first.Any ETB of a creature you are about to Day of Black Sun anyway. Day of Black Sun ('Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures') at X=3 kills Momo, Nuisance, Sage and its token copies (MV 3), Hopesinger, Guardian and Scholar regardless of how many +1/+1 counters they carry; it does NOT kill Quantum Riddler or Starfield Shepherd (both MV 5, warp cost does not change MV). Those two are Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff / Drowner jobs.Get Lost's Map tokens: they are created for the controller of the destroyed permanent, i.e. us, so there is nothing of theirs to counter; and a Map's explore ability sacrifices the token as a cost, so even in theory nothing would be blanked.Wasp on Steamcore Scholar or Flitterwing Nuisance: legal, strips flying/vigilance or the activated ability, but a 2/2 is not worth the Wasp unless it is their only flying blocker on our turn (Wasp target 6 covers that).Wasp in response to a warp-cast Riddler's ETB when they hold one or fewer cards: blanking Riddler before the draw resolves removes the 'plus one' replacement, so they draw 1 instead of 2, but the body leaves at end step and Wasp is better saved for the Riddler that stays.
The Wondrous Wasp
Cheap, and it fits this matchup's shape: a 2/1 flash flier blocks and trades in a deck of 1/1 and 2/3 fliers, there is no hexproof or ward anywhere in their 75, and the blank removes STATIC abilities Tidebinder can never reach: Momo's discount, Riddler's extra-draw replacement, Guardian's whole counter plan. Wasp is also the only one of our two cards that is castable on their turn 2, which is when this deck's best things happen. Best uses: (1) blank the permanent Quantum Riddler the turn it lands; (2) on the play, tap Momo at their turn-2 upkeep so Springleaf Drum has no untapped creature to tap and the discount is gone, collapsing both nut lines; (3) blank an armed Glen Elendra Guardian on our turn so removal resolves (Guardian can only counter noncreature spells, so it cannot stop Wasp or Wasp's trigger). The weaknesses are all {W}: Daydream (exile and return = new object, blank ends, +1/+1 counter, ETB again), Morningtide's Light from the side, and Erode / Get Lost on Wasp, which end the blank at once; if they kill Wasp with its trigger still on the stack the target is still tapped but never loses abilities at all. A Wasp blank is a tempo play, not a lock.
#1Quantum Riddler (permanent: after Daydream or a hard cast)
WhyTheir biggest flier and their late-game card engine, and it can be on the battlefield as early as their turn 2 (Momo turn 1; turn 2 warp Riddler for {U}, Daydream for {W}). Wasp is our only on-curve answer that turn on the play. Tapping it at their beginning of combat stops 5 (or double-strike) flying damage; blanking removes flying and the 'draw that many cards plus one' replacement. It becomes a 5/7 ground wall that Beavers, Siren and Wasp fly over and Enduring Curiosity or Tidebinder can block.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step (before attackers) to stop the attack, or in response to the Daydream re-entry ETB draw if they have one or fewer cards in hand (blanking before the draw resolves cuts it from 2 cards to 1). Our turn if we just want it blanked and out of the air before we swing. Never on a warp-cast Riddler that has not been Daydreamed (it leaves at end step). For a permanent kill, Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff (Sphinx is not an outlaw) or Floodpits Drowner (ETB stun, then {1}{U},{T} shuffle) are the real answers; Requiting Hex cannot target it (MV 5).
AfterLoses flying, ETB, replacement draw. Keeps body and counters. If Practiced Offense later gives it double strike or lifelink, it keeps that grant (gained after the effect began).
#2Momo, Friendly Flier at their turn-2 upkeep (on the play, when Momo is their only creature)
WhySpringleaf Drum's cost is '{T}, Tap an untapped creature you control'; a Wasp-tapped Momo cannot pay it, and the blank removes the static 'costs {1} less' that Tidebinder cannot touch. With Momo their only creature that is often 2 mana denied, and it shuts off both turn-2 nut lines at once: Drum + discounted Sage copy, and warp Riddler for {U} + Daydream. They are left with two lands and full-price cards.
WhenTheir upkeep (after untap, before draw). On the play that means holding {1}{U} on our turn 2 instead of developing; worth it when Momo is on their board and nothing else is. Later in the game the same play (upkeep, tap their only untapped creature) still taxes Drum and the discount. Requiting Hex ({B}) kills Momo permanently and has almost no other target in their deck, so when Hex is in hand use Hex on our turn and save Wasp; Wasp at upkeep is for when Hex is not available or when denying THIS turn's Drum mana matters more than the permanent kill.
AfterLoses flying, the discount, the +1/+1 trigger. Keeps 1/1 body and any counters. Stays tapped through their turn, so Drum is off for the turn.
#3Glen Elendra Guardian (on the battlefield WITH a counter)
WhyBlank it and it cannot pay '{1}{U}, Remove a counter from this creature' while Wasp lives, so Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Kaito hard-casts, Duress, Day of Black Sun and our counterspells all resolve. They cannot fight this with Guardian: Wasp is a creature spell and its trigger is an ability, not a spell. Only their single Spider-Sense can counter the trigger. Check the counters first: a Guardian with no counter (Daydreamed, or pumped while holding its -1/-1) is already disarmed.
WhenOur main phase, BEFORE casting the removal spell we want to protect, or at their end step. Wasp ETB targets Guardian; when it resolves, cast the removal. Do not cast the removal first and Wasp in response: that is Tidebinder's job (countering the activation), Wasp does not counter. Erode / Get Lost are instants, so casting Wasp on our turn only dodges Daydream; if they kill Wasp in response to its trigger, Guardian is tapped but keeps its abilities and can still activate (no {T} in its cost). Protect a load-bearing Wasp with Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay!, or bait the single counter with a cheap spell instead.
AfterLoses flying, flash, the counter ability. Keeps the -1/-1 counter (2/3 body) and stays tapped this turn. If they Daydream it, the +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters annihilate: the new Guardian has its abilities back but NO counter, so it still cannot activate until Practiced Offense, Repartee or Abandoned Air Temple gives it one.
#4Stirring Hopesinger
WhyBlank before their sorcery turn and Daydream / Practiced Offense / Morningtide's Light no longer pump the team; lifelink gone makes the race winnable.
WhenTheir upkeep or our end step, so the blank is in place before their main phase. Blanking in response to a Repartee trigger does nothing to that trigger (Wasp does not counter; the trigger already exists and resolves).
AfterLoses flying, lifelink, Repartee. Keeps 1/3 body and existing counters.
#5Sage of the Skies (or its token copy)
WhyThe lifelink flier they most often put double strike on. Tap it before attackers to remove a turn of lifelink damage; the blank strips flying and lifelink.
WhenTheir beginning of combat. A Practiced Offense cast afterwards still gives it double strike or lifelink (kept, gained after), and the +1/+1 counter still lands.
AfterLoses flying, lifelink. Keeps 2/3 body and counters.
#6Any single untapped flier of theirs on OUR turn
WhyPure tempo: tap their only flying blocker at our beginning of combat so Spyglass Siren / Dream Beavers / Wasp connect, which is how Enduring Curiosity draws and how Kaito ninjutsus in.
WhenOur beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared. If they Erode Wasp in response, the tap still happens (only the ability loss fails).
AfterWhatever that creature was, it is a tapped body with no abilities until Wasp dies or they Daydream / Morningtide's Light it.
Traps
Any attacking creature: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to stop the attacker. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat; it still deals damage. To stop an attack, Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or earlier). Wasp after attackers are declared is still legal and sometimes right for a different reason: the attacker loses flying (so Enduring Curiosity, Drowner or Tidebinder can block it on the ground) and loses printed lifelink; a Practiced Offense grant from earlier in the turn is kept.
Quantum Riddler / Starfield Shepherd cast for warp: Wasp the warped body. It is exiled at the beginning of the next end step by a delayed trigger created by the warp cost, not by an ability printed on the creature, so it leaves whether or not it is blanked. If they Daydream it in response, the returned creature is a new object and your blank did nothing.
Daydream (main, and flashback from the graveyard): Treat a Wasp blank as permanent. 'Exile target creature you control, then return that card to the battlefield' makes a new object: the blank ends, it gets a +1/+1 counter, its ETB fires again, Momo triggers again. Each Daydream does this twice (flashback {2}{W}). If the blank matters, kill the Daydream target in response with Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff (Requiting Hex only if the target is Momo or Flitterwing Nuisance) and Daydream fizzles; Hopesinger's Repartee trigger still resolves. Exception: Daydream on a Guardian or Nuisance is a bad play for them (counters annihilate, it returns disarmed), so do not fear it.
Practiced Offense: Blank the creature they are about to give double strike so the double strike does nothing. Abilities gained after Wasp's effect began are kept (ruling 2). The blank only removes the printed flying/lifelink; the Offense grant and its counters land in full.
Erode / Get Lost: Assume a blank is safe because you did it on your own turn. Both are instants. Casting Wasp on our turn only dodges sorcery-speed Daydream. Erode ({W}) on Wasp with the trigger still on the stack means the target is tapped but the 'for as long as Wasp remains' duration has already ended, so no ability loss ever applies; Erode on a resolved Wasp ends the blank instantly. Never build a turn that dies to one white mana: hold Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! for the Erode, or accept Wasp as 2-mana bait for their 1-mana removal.
Spider-Sense (sideboard, 1 copy): Nothing they can do about a resolved Wasp blank. Web-slinging {U} lets them return a TAPPED creature they control to hand as part of the cost; the Wasp-tapped, blanked creature is perfect fodder, resetting it to a recast and countering our next triggered ability (Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner ETB, Curiosity draw, Beavers drain) for one mana.
Glen Elendra Guardian in their hand: Wasp pre-empts a Guardian they have not cast yet. Guardian has flash; from hand they can cast it in response to our removal and activate immediately. That line needs {2}{U} + {1}{U} = 5 open mana on our turn, so it is a late-game concern, not an every-turn one; when they do pass with 5 up, bait with the cheap spell first. Wasp handles a Guardian already on the battlefield; a flashed-in Guardian is a Tidebinder job (counter the activation).
Glen Elendra Guardian with no counter: Spend Wasp or Tidebinder on a Guardian that has already activated, been Daydreamed, or been pumped. Its ability needs 'Remove a counter from this creature' as a cost. A Guardian whose -1/-1 counter was spent, or annihilated by a +1/+1 counter from Daydream / Practiced Offense / Repartee / Abandoned Air Temple, is a 3/4 flier that cannot counter anything until it gets a new counter. Kill it with Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff when convenient; do not blank it.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Turn-2 collapse (on the play): with Momo as their only creature, hold {1}{U} on our turn 2 and flash Wasp at their turn-2 upkeep targeting Momo. Drum has no untapped creature to tap, the warp discount is gone, so neither the warp Riddler + Daydream line nor the Drum + Sage copy line is castable that turn. Hex Momo on our turn 3.
  • Answer the permanent Riddler: when Daydream returns a Riddler (or one is hard-cast), Wasp its ETB draw if they hold one or fewer cards (draw shrinks to 1, Riddler blanked), otherwise Wasp at their beginning of combat. With three lands and the ETB on the stack, Tidebinder does the same and leaves a 3/2 body. Then kill or shuffle it: Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff, or Floodpits Drowner (its own ETB stun, then {1}{U},{T} shuffle next turn; the shuffle needs the stun counter, not the blank).
  • Wasp then remove: our main phase, Wasp targeting an armed Glen Elendra Guardian (they cannot respond with Guardian: creature spell, ability trigger). When the blank resolves, Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff the permanent Riddler or the double-strike Sage. Erode on Wasp in response still lets Guardian activate, so have Spell Pierce up or a bait spell ready.
  • Counter the counter: cast our removal with {2}{U} still open. If Guardian activates, flash Tidebinder targeting the activation: our spell resolves, they lose the mana and the counter, and Guardian is a vanilla 3/4 while Tidebinder lives. Cost to us: 'Its controller draws a card' would have given US a card, and countering the ability forfeits it. Cheaper version: bait the one counter with Requiting Hex on Momo or a Duress first, then cast the real spell.
  • Open the skies for Kaito: at our beginning of combat, Wasp taps their only untapped flier. Attack with Spyglass Siren / Dream Beavers / Wasp; with an unblocked attacker, ninjutsu Kaito, Bane of Nightmares ({1}{U}{B}) and Enduring Curiosity draws off each connect. Their blockers are all fliers, so this is usually the only way a ninjutsu window opens against this deck.
  • Blanked equals blockable: a creature Wasp or Tidebinder has blanked has no flying, so Tidebinder (3/2), Enduring Curiosity (4/3) and Floodpits Drowner can block it, and our fliers go over it. Kaito can never block: he is a creature only during OUR turn.
  • Wan Shi Tong farms Shepherd: with Wan Shi Tong, Librarian on the battlefield, never counter Starfield Shepherd's ETB search; it gives us a +1/+1 counter and a card. Erode's land search does not trigger him (we are the searcher).
  • Fizzle the Daydream: their Daydream resets our Kaito -2 / Drowner stun and any Wasp or Tidebinder blank. If they Daydream a stunned or blanked creature, Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff the target in response (Requiting Hex only if the target is Momo or Flitterwing Nuisance; everything else is MV 3 or 5) and Daydream fizzles. Stirring Hopesinger's Repartee trigger still resolves; counter that with Tidebinder if the team pump would matter.
  • Let them pump a fresh Guardian: any +1/+1 counter (Practiced Offense, Repartee, Abandoned Air Temple, Daydream) landing on a Guardian or Nuisance that still holds its -1/-1 counter annihilates the pair and leaves it unable to activate. Do not Tidebinder a Repartee or Air Temple trigger that is about to disarm their Guardian for you.
  • Sideboard shape: Day of Black Sun X=3 (5 mana) kills every creature in their 75 except Quantum Riddler and Starfield Shepherd (both MV 5; counters are irrelevant, it checks mana value), and also kills our own MV-3-or-less creatures, so cast it when they have the token/flier board and we have Kaito or Curiosity (MV 4, safe). Duress takes Daydream / Practiced Offense / Erode. Spell Pierce protects a load-bearing Wasp, Tidebinder or Kaito from Erode / Get Lost. Spell Snare has zero main-deck targets (their spells are MV 1, 3 or 5; warp and flashback do not change MV) and only finds Get Lost / Spider-Sense / Disdainful Stroke / Rest in Peace postboard: cut it. Requiting Hex hits only Momo and Nuisance: trim to 2. Disdainful Stroke (2 side) counters our Kaito, Enduring Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong with X of 2+, and Day of Black Sun; Tidebinder (MV 3) and Wasp (MV 2) are safe from it. Flashfreeze and Annul stay out (Annul hits only Drum and Rest in Peace).
  • Their double-spell turn with {2}{U} up (turn 3+ on the play, 4+ on the draw): wait for the second spell. If it is Sage of the Skies, Tidebinder the copy trigger; if it is Daydream with Hopesinger out and the target is not a Riddler, Tidebinder the Repartee trigger; if it is Daydream on a Riddler, let Daydream resolve and Tidebinder the re-entry draw so the now-permanent Riddler is blanked.
Hold or fire
Make the hold decision at the END of our main phase from the board, not from what might happen. Tidebinder: leave {2}{U} up into their turn only when one of these is true on board: (1) a Riddler or Shepherd is sitting in warp exile, or they have a Riddler on the battlefield and Daydream mana, so a permanent Riddler ETB is likely next turn; (2) Momo and an untapped Springleaf Drum are on their board with Sage / Hopesinger possible AND we have already developed (Kaito or Enduring Curiosity already in play); (3) an ARMED Guardian (counter still on it) is on their board and we plan removal next turn (then the Tidebinder mana is held on OUR turn, alongside the removal). Otherwise develop (Kaito, Drowner, Curiosity, removal) and drop Tidebinder as a 3/2 with no target at their end step whenever the mana would go unused. Never point it at a warped creature's ETB or exile trigger, a land, a spell, or a counterless Guardian. Wasp: on the play with Momo as their only creature, hold {1}{U} on our turn 2 for their upkeep (Drum + discount denial); otherwise fire pre-emptively on OUR turn when the blank protects a removal spell (armed Guardian), at THEIR upkeep when the blank taxes their turn (Momo, Hopesinger before their sorceries), at THEIR beginning of combat when the goal is stopping an attack (permanent Riddler, double-strike Sage), at OUR beginning of combat when the goal is connecting (Curiosity, ninjutsu). Never fire on a warped body or expect a tap after attackers are declared to stop damage. On the draw: they are a turn ahead, their turn-2 Riddler or Sage copy lands before we can react, and lifelink fliers punish slow starts; Wasp is a flash flying blocker first (trade with Momo / Nuisance, chump Sage, blank the Riddler when it sticks) and Tidebinder goes down as a body at their end step unless window (1) is live. In every case assume Erode ({W}) can end any blank at any time, and that Erode in response to the ETB leaves the target tapped but not blanked: never build a turn that dies to one white mana.
How many to run
Keep 2 The Wondrous Wasp main, do not add a third: it is a cheap flash flier in a flier matchup, there is no hexproof or ward in their 75, it is the only one of the two castable on their turn 2 (when the permanent Riddler and the Sage copy actually happen), and it blanks the statics (Momo discount, Drum via the tap, Guardian counter plan, Riddler draw engine) Tidebinder cannot touch. Keep 1 Tishana's Tidebinder main and leave both sideboard copies out: its windows (permanent Riddler ETB, Guardian activation, Sage copy, Repartee) are real but mostly open from their turn 3-4 on, most of their engine is static, spell, mana ability or a warp body that leaves anyway, the 3/2 ground body is poor against fliers, and 3-mana reactive plays are slow against a deck of {W} and {1}{W} threats. The slots that actually free up are Spell Snare (2, zero main-deck MV-2 targets) and 2 of the 4 Requiting Hex (only Momo and Flitterwing Nuisance are legal targets); bring in 3 Duress (Daydream / Practiced Offense / Erode), 2 Spell Pierce (protects blanks and Kaito from Erode / Get Lost, taxes Daydream), and 1 Day of Black Sun (X=3 kills everything but Riddler and Shepherd, MV 5; it also kills our own small creatures, so it is a reset not a one-sided sweeper), with the sixth cut a judgment call.
Review trail: confidence medium, 4 disputed, 25 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 1, Day of Black Sun: the finding said Guardian (3/4) and pumped creatures survive '-3/-3' and that counters push toughness past 3. Day of Black Sun's oracle text (verified in opponents.json) is 'Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures.' It is mana-value based, so toughness and +1/+1 counters are irrelevant; a Guardian at 3/4 still dies to X=3 (MV 3). The draft's claim was wrong for a different reason (Starfield Shepherd, MV 5, also survives), which reviewers 2 and 3 identified correctly and which is fixed.
  • Reviewer 2, Guardian's realistic prey 'Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Kaito hard-cast, Enduring Curiosity and our counterspells': Enduring Curiosity is an Enchantment Creature spell ({2}{U}{U}, 'Enchantment Creature, Cat Glimmer'), and Guardian reads 'Counter target noncreature spell', so it cannot counter Curiosity. Curiosity removed from the Guardian prey list; Duress and Day of Black Sun (noncreature) added instead.
  • Reviewer 1, 'Flitterwing Nuisance post-activation draw triggers are a better window than countering the activation': the source analysis is correct (a delayed triggered ability created by an activated ability has the same source, so Nuisance is blanked either way), but countering the activation denies EVERY draw for the turn while the mana and counter stay paid, whereas countering one delayed trigger denies a single draw. Kept the activation as the primary window and listed the delayed trigger as an alternative, not an upgrade.
  • Reviewer 2, bait line naming Spell Pierce as a cheap spell to eat Guardian's counter: Spell Pierce needs a noncreature spell to target and cannot be cast into an empty stack, so it is not usable as bait. Bait examples kept to Requiting Hex on Momo and Duress.
Changes after review
  • FATAL fix: Requiting Hex ('Destroy target creature with mana value 2 or less') removed as an answer to Sage, Hopesinger, Guardian and Riddler everywhere (Tidebinder target 2 why, Wasp Momo timing, Fizzle-the-Daydream line, hold_or_fire); stated that Momo and Flitterwing Nuisance are its only legal targets in their 75 and that warp does not change MV.
  • Added that Shoot the Sheriff cannot target Flitterwing Nuisance (Faerie Rogue is an outlaw).
  • Fixed the direction of Guardian's 'Its controller draws a card': the countered spell's controller (us) draws; countering the activation forfeits our card. Tidebinder target 2 why and the Counter-the-counter combined line rewritten.
  • Fixed the Daydreamed Guardian claim: +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters annihilate as a state-based action, so it returns with abilities but no counter and cannot activate; same for Nuisance. Added a 'Guardian with no counter' Wasp trap, a check-the-counters instruction on both Guardian targets, and a 'let them pump a fresh Guardian' combined line.
  • Removed Kaito from the blocker list (creature only during our turn); noted explicitly that he can never block.
  • Day of Black Sun corrected to its mana-value text: X=3 kills everything except Quantum Riddler AND Starfield Shepherd (MV 5); counters irrelevant; also kills our own MV<=3 creatures. Fixed in dont_bother, the sideboard combined line, and counts_recommendation.
  • how_they_win rewritten: turn 1 is Momo OR Drum (not both); added that Momo's discount applies to warp costs (Riddler {U}, Shepherd {W}) and that both nut lines (warp Riddler + Daydream permanent 5/7; Drum + discounted Sage copy) are turn-2 plays.
  • Re-ranked the permanent Quantum Riddler to #1 for both Tidebinder and Wasp; Guardian moved to Tidebinder #2 and Wasp #3; added realistic turn windows (Tidebinder from their turn 3 on the play / 4 on the draw; Wasp castable on their turn 2 on the play).
  • Added new Wasp target #2: Momo at their turn-2 upkeep denies Springleaf Drum ('Tap an untapped creature you control') and the discount together; added as the first combined line and to hold_or_fire.
  • Sage copy timing corrected: fastest copy is turn 2 (Momo, then Drum + discounted Sage) and is unanswerable; Tidebinder window is turn 3+/4+.
  • Floodpits Drowner corrected: shuffle needs 'target creature with a stun counter on it', supplied by Drowner's own ETB or Kaito -2, unrelated to any blank. Removed the implied Wasp/Tidebinder synergy; presented Drowner as the cleaner permanent-Riddler answer.
  • Added the CR 611.2b timing: Erode / Get Lost in response to Wasp's (or Tidebinder's) ETB trigger means the tap (or counter) still happens but no ability loss ever applies; corrected the Erode trap that implied casting on our turn was safe (only dodges sorcery-speed Daydream).
  • Wasp 'any attacking creature' trap expanded: post-declaration Wasp still strips flying (ground block possible) and printed lifelink; it just does not remove the attacker from combat.
  • 'Permanently disarm' softened to 'while Tidebinder lives; spent counter stays spent; later +1/+1 counters re-arm it once Tidebinder is gone'.
  • Morningtide's Light expanded: triggers Repartee (targets creatures), resets their own blanked/stunned creatures, and can exile our Wasp/Tidebinder (blank ends; ours return tapped at end step with fresh ETBs).
  • Rest in Peace note corrected: it does matter against Enduring Curiosity (exiled instead of dying, no glimmer return), so 'they probably do not board it in' was replaced with the real answer (Annul / Spell Pierce on the spell).
  • Added Flitterwing Nuisance delayed draw triggers as an alternative Tidebinder window (source is Nuisance, so it is blanked either way), ranked below countering the activation.
  • Added Spell Snare has zero main-deck targets (all their spells are MV 1, 3 or 5) and named the cuts (2 Snare, 2 Hex) and the ins (3 Duress, 2 Spell Pierce, 1 Day of Black Sun) in counts_recommendation and the sideboard line.
  • Added Disdainful Stroke (side, 2): counters Kaito, Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong X>=2, Day of Black Sun; Tidebinder and Wasp are safe from it.
  • Added Get Lost Map tokens (ours, and the Map sacrifices itself as a cost) and Wasp on Scholar / Nuisance and Wasp on a warp Riddler's ETB (2-card to 1-card trim) to dont_bother with reasons.
  • Noted that Erode / Get Lost aimed at Kaito as a planeswalker does not target a creature and so does not trigger Repartee.
  • Flash-Guardian-from-hand trap now states it needs 5 open mana on our turn and is a late-game concern.
  • Wan Shi Tong's oracle text now verified from the data (opponents.json) and quoted; Erode 'Its controller may search' reasoning kept.
  • hold_or_fire rewritten as end-of-our-main-phase board checks (warp exile / Daydream mana; Momo + untapped Drum with development already done; armed Guardian with removal planned) instead of conditions that only become true on their turn; added the turn-2 Wasp hold on the play.
  • Warp trap clarified: the exile is a delayed trigger created by the warp cost when cast, not an ability printed on the permanent, so a blanked warped creature still leaves (dash precedent); still never counter it.
vs. Golgari Midrange2.1% TB: goodWasp: situational▼
A near-creatureless Golgari control deck whose real engine is Unholy Annex plus Firdoch Core: Core is a Kindred Artifact, Shapeshifter with changeling (every creature type, so it IS a Demon), and once Annex and Core are both down (turn 3 + turn 4 in either order) the end-step trigger reads "draw a card, each opponent loses 2 life and you gain 2 life" every turn, no 6/6 required. Core also taps for any color, so the turn after it lands they have 5 mana: Ritual Chamber (6/6 flying Demon token), Deadly Cover-Up (sweeper), or Fel plus a 1-drop arrive on their turn 4, with Together as One (converge X = 4-5: draw, burn any target, gain) and hardcast Deceit (5/5 that bounces and/or strips a card) from turn 5. Deceit is also evoked for 2 as a Hymn on turns 2-4. They protect all this with 13 maindeck answers (3 Requiting Hex, 2 Shoot the Sheriff, 1 Bitter Triumph, 4 Deadly Cover-Up, 1 Maelstrom Pulse, 1 Nowhere to Run, Fel's -3) and 3 Duress, and finish with the Demon, Fel's emblem, and manlands (Restless Cottage/Reef, Great Hall, Ba Sing Se earthbend). Structurally the engine is an enchantment plus a mana ability, so Tidebinder cannot turn it off; it skips one Annex trigger per copy, blanks a Core only if they animate it, and its best work is countering the Chamber Demon, a Deceit ETB or a Fel activation.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Four live targets recur in almost every game: the Ritual Chamber Demon trigger (5 mana wasted, no 6/6), the Unholy Annex end-step trigger while Core or a Demon token is out (deny a card and a 4-point swing, at the exact moment you want to flash a body anyway), any Deceit ETB (the discard or the bounce), and the first Fel loyalty activation (locks every ability on him). A fifth, rarer jackpot is Core's own {4} animation: Core is an artifact, so countering it strips changeling and Core stops being a Demon, flipping Annex to cost them 2. Everything else in the 75 is a spell, a mana ability, a static, a replacement effect or a land that is not a creature when the counter resolves. Do not hold mana on turns 2-4 hoping to catch an evoked Deceit; fire Tidebinder when you already have {2}{U} up for another reason. The body will eat Hex, Sheriff or Cover-Up; the countered ability is the permanent gain.
#1Unholy Annex // Ritual Chamber (Ritual Chamber door)
"When you unlock this door, create a 6/6 black Demon creature token with flying."
WhyFive mana for nothing this turn and no 6/6 flier. The token itself dies to Requiting Hex (MV 0), Sheriff or Drowner later, so the counter is a Time Walk, not the whole game; take it when you have {2}{U} up, never at the cost of a Kaito or Curiosity turn.
WhenTheir main phase, from the turn they have 5 mana (their turn 4 if Core landed on 3). Two arrivals: (a) they cast the Ritual Chamber half for {3}{B}{B}; the Room enters with that door unlocked and the trigger goes on the stack; (b) with Annex already on the battlefield they pay {3}{B}{B} as a special action (no stack) to unlock the Chamber door, then the trigger goes on the stack. Flash Tidebinder with the trigger on the stack. On the play you have 4 lands on their turn 4, so holding {2}{U} costs you a 1-drop; on the draw you have 3 lands and holding means skipping Kaito, so do not.
AfterSource is an enchantment (Room), so it loses nothing (ruling 3). Case (a): they have a Room whose Annex door is still locked, so no end-step trigger until they pay {2}{B} more. Case (b): Annex keeps drawing each end step and drains you if Core or another Demon is out, otherwise costs them 2. No 6/6 ever exists.
RiskOrdinary: they Sheriff or Cover-Up the 3/2 afterwards. Nothing about the counter is undone.
#2Unholy Annex (Annex door, while they control Firdoch Core or a Demon token)
"At the beginning of your end step, draw a card. If you control a Demon, each opponent loses 2 life and you gain 2 life. Otherwise, you lose 2 life."
WhyLive from their turn 4 in most games because Core is a Demon (changeling). Countering one instance denies a card and a 4-point life swing, and their end step is where you want to flash a creature anyway (it untaps and attacks with Enduring Curiosity out). Repeats every turn; this stops one instance, so it is the default EOT use when no Chamber, Deceit or Fel window is coming.
WhenTheir end step, trigger on the stack. Check what they control first: Core or a Demon token means the trigger drains you; if neither is out the same trigger costs them 2 life and you should let it resolve.
AfterEnchantment source: loses nothing. Next end step it triggers again.
RiskIf they control no Demon (Core blanked or gone, no token), countering saves them 2 life; only do it then as an EOT body deploy.
#3Deceit (evoked for {U/B}{U/B} or hardcast for {4}{U/B}{U/B})
"When this creature enters, if {B}{B} was spent to cast it, target opponent reveals their hand. You choose a nonland card from it. That player discards that card." and/or "When this creature enters, if {U}{U} was spent to cast it, return up to one other target nonland permanent to its owner's hand."
WhyEvoked with black: countering the discard leaves them 2 mana and a card down for nothing (the evoke sacrifice still resolves). Hardcast: with 4 Core, 2 Watery Grave, 2 Restless Reef and Multiversal Passage a six-mana Deceit routinely has both {U}{U} and {B}{B} in it, so BOTH triggers go on the stack (bounce Kaito or Curiosity AND discard). Tidebinder counters exactly one: default to the discard (permanent card loss); take the bounce instead when it targets a Kaito that has ticked up or a Curiosity you cannot recast next turn.
WhenDeceit enters; its ETB trigger(s) and, if evoked, the sacrifice trigger go on the stack. Respond with Tidebinder targeting the discard or bounce trigger, never the sacrifice trigger. Realistically you cannot have {2}{U} open on their turn 2, or their turn 3 on the draw; the evoke counter is a bonus that happens when you already have mana up (their turn 4+ on the play, 5+ on the draw). Hardcast Deceit on turn 5+ is the version you will actually counter most games. A Tidebinder sitting in hand while you are tapped out is itself a legal Deceit discard target.
AfterEvoked: Deceit is sacrificed anyway; our hand is intact. Hardcast: Deceit is a creature at resolution and loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives, which changes nothing on board; it is a vanilla 5/5. Answer it with Floodpits Drowner (tap, stun, shuffle), Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff (Elemental Incarnation is not an outlaw). Requiting Hex cannot target it (MV 6).
RiskNone beyond the body dying later. If they remove Tidebinder the countered trigger stays countered.
#4Professor Dellian Fel
Any loyalty ability: "+2: You gain 3 life.", "0: You draw a card and lose 1 life.", "−3: Destroy target creature.", "−6: You get an emblem with 'Whenever you gain life, target opponent loses that much life.'"
WhyCountering ANY loyalty activation strips Fel of every ability while Tidebinder lives, so he cannot activate again. Counter the first one you can, whichever it is: the 0 and the +2 are the ones you will actually see with mana open. The -3 is the nicest window (loyalty already paid down to 2, your creature survives) but never let a 0 or +2 resolve waiting for it. The -6 is the one that must not resolve: the emblem is permanent, cannot be removed, and turns every Annex drain, Together as One and blight-Hex lifegain into a second drain.
WhenTheir main phase, sorcery speed: as soon as they activate (loyalty counters already added or removed), respond with Tidebinder targeting the ability on the stack. With Core on 3, Fel plus a 1-drop is a turn-4 play and the -6 can come as early as turn 5 after a +2.
AfterFel is a planeswalker at resolution, so he loses all four loyalty abilities until Tidebinder leaves. Loyalty counters stay. A blank Fel at 2 dies to Wasp, Kaito, a Beaver or any two attackers (a lone Spyglass Siren only takes him to 1); at 5 or 7 attack him with everything before they kill Tidebinder.
RiskOne copy main. If they answer Tidebinder, Fel wakes up with whatever loyalty he has; if he is at 6+ and you cannot hold Tidebinder, Bitter Triumph him at end of turn instead.
#5Firdoch Core
"{4}: This artifact becomes a 4/4 artifact creature until end of turn."
WhyJackpot when it happens: they waste 4 mana, and because Core is an artifact at resolution it loses ALL abilities while Tidebinder lives, including changeling and "{T}: Add one mana of any color". No changeling means Core is no longer a Demon, so Annex flips from draining you 2 to costing them 2 each end step, and they lose a rainbow source for Together as One and Deceit's colors. Rare (they animate as a blocker or late attacker, and not into open {2}{U} if they respect Tidebinder), but strictly the best lock in the matchup.
WhenIn response to the animation activation, on their beginning of combat or when they animate to block on your turn.
AfterNot a creature this turn; no mana, no changeling, no animation until Tidebinder leaves. If they also control a Demon token, Annex still drains; kill the token with Hex.
RiskDo not confuse with its mana ability, which can never be targeted. They can un-blank Core by killing Tidebinder.
#6Nowhere to Run
"When this enchantment enters, target creature an opponent controls gets -3/-3 until end of turn."
WhySaves Enduring Curiosity, a Beaver, Drowner or Siren. Fair trade (their 2 mana vs our 3), not premium; take it when the saved creature matters or you wanted the EOT body anyway.
WhenETB trigger on the stack, at instant speed since Nowhere to Run has flash (1 main, 1 more postboard).
AfterEnchantment source: it keeps its static (our creatures targetable through hexproof, ward does not trigger). Only the -3/-3 is stopped.
RiskKaito rarely needs saving: on our turn he is a 3/4, so -3/-3 leaves him 0/1 and alive.
#7Rare bonuses: The Soul Stone, Restless Cottage/Reef attack trigger, Ghost Vacuum (side), earthbended land, Qarsi Revenant Renew (side)
Soul Stone: "{6}{B}, {T}, Exile a creature you control: Harness The Soul Stone" and its harnessed "∞, At the beginning of your upkeep, return target creature card from your graveyard to the battlefield." Cottage: "Whenever this land attacks, create a Food token and exile up to one target card from a graveyard." Reef: "Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards." Ghost Vacuum: "{6}, {T}, Sacrifice this artifact: Put each creature card exiled with this artifact onto the battlefield under your control..." Earthbended land: the delayed trigger "When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped." Revenant: "Renew, {2}{B}, Exile this card from your graveyard: Put a flying counter, a deathtouch counter, and a lifelink counter on target creature."
WhyAll are clean blowouts you will see maybe one game in ten. Soul Stone harness: 7 mana, the tap and the exiled creature all stay paid, and the Stone (an artifact) loses indestructible, its mana ability and both other abilities while Tidebinder lives; countering the ∞ upkeep trigger does the same. Cottage/Reef attack trigger: at resolution the land is an animated creature, so it loses everything including its mana ability, and stays blank after it reverts (the affected set locks when the effect begins); Reef also loses its granted deathtouch. Ghost Vacuum: after they pay 6 and sacrifice it, our exiled Curiosity/Beavers/Drowners stay exiled for good. Earthbended land: the return is a delayed trigger; counter it when the land dies (Sheriff, Hex, combat, or their own Cover-Up/Day of Black Sun) and the land is gone permanently. Renew: the Revenant is exiled from the graveyard as a cost either way and the counters never land.
WhenEach on the stack at the obvious moment: harness activation or upkeep trigger; declare-attackers step; Vacuum activation (sorcery speed); the return trigger after the land dies; Renew activation (sorcery speed).
AfterSoul Stone: blank artifact while Tidebinder lives (a returned Deceit would be a vanilla 5/5 anyway, it was not cast). Cottage/Reef: still a 4/4 attacker this turn (Tidebinder does not remove it from combat), then a land with no abilities until Tidebinder leaves; Requiting Hex on the animated land (MV 0) is the permanent answer and should come first if you have it. Vacuum: already sacrificed, nothing to blank. Earthbended land: source is a card in the graveyard or exile, nothing loses abilities, it just stays there. Renew: source is a card in exile, nothing loses abilities.
RiskThey will not fire Soul Stone or Vacuum into open {2}{U} if they know you hold Tidebinder; that stalls the card, which is fine.
Traps
Together as One, Deadly Cover-Up, Duress, Requiting Hex (blight), The End, Day of Black Sun, Vicious Rivalry, Inside Information: Counter the converge draw/damage, the sweep, the cost reduction, collect evidence or blight with Tidebinder. All are spells or additional/alternative costs of spells, not abilities. Only a real counterspell (We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce, Annul on Annex/Core/Soul Stone/Tome) stops them.
Unholy Annex // Ritual Chamber (door unlock, casting a half): Counter the {3}{B}{B} unlock itself so the Chamber stays locked, or counter the Room as it is cast. Paying to unlock a door is a special action that does not use the stack; casting the half is a spell. The only targetable pieces are the "When you unlock this door" Demon trigger and the end-step trigger.
Firdoch Core, Great Hall ("{T}, Pay 1 life: Add one mana of any color"), The Soul Stone, Verges, Watery Grave/Overgrown Tomb/Multiversal Passage: Counter a mana activation to Stone Rain them, or counter the "you may pay 2 life" to make a shockland enter tapped. Mana abilities (could add mana, no target, not loyalty; the life payment does not change that) cannot be targeted. "As this land enters" is a replacement effect, not a trigger. Core only becomes counterable through its {4} animation.
Firdoch Core changeling, The Soul Stone indestructible, Nowhere to Run static, Qarsi Revenant flying/deathtouch/lifelink: Target the static ability directly so Core stops being a Demon, so Bitter Triumph can kill the Stone, so Kaito is protected, or to ground the Revenant. Statics and keywords cannot be targeted. Core loses changeling and the Stone loses indestructible only as a SIDE EFFECT of countering their activated/triggered ability (Core's {4}, the Stone's harness or ∞). Nowhere to Run keeps its static whatever you counter. Wasp is the tool for a Revenant on the battlefield.
Deceit: (a) Counter the evoke sacrifice trigger to "punish" the evoke. (b) Countering the ETB removes Deceit. (a) The discard trigger is already on the stack and exists independently of its source, so it still takes your card, and they keep a 5/5 for 2 mana (it loses abilities but keeps 5/5). (b) Countering an ETB never removes the permanent: hardcast Deceit stays a vanilla 5/5; evoked Deceit leaves only because the separate sacrifice trigger resolves.
Restless Cottage / Restless Reef / Great Hall of the Biblioplex / Ba Sing Se (animation activations): Counter the {2}{B}{G}, {2}{U}{B}, {5} or Earthbend activation to blank the land. When that counter resolves the land is not a creature, so per ruling 3 it keeps all abilities; you only waste their mana. To blank a manland counter its ATTACK trigger while it is animated, or Wasp it while animated. Core is different only because it is an artifact at all times.
Professor Dellian Fel (emblem): Wait and counter the emblem's "Whenever you gain life, target opponent loses that much life" trigger. You can counter one instance (an emblem is not a permanent, nothing loses abilities), but the emblem can never be removed and triggers on every lifegain. Stop the −6 activation, or Bitter Triumph Fel before he reaches 6.
Not worth it
Mazemind Tome: the scry/draw activations deny one card and lock a 2-mana artifact; its "four or more page counters" exile is a state trigger, so countering it only delays the exile and the 4 life until Tidebinder leaves (the condition is still true and it triggers again). EOT body use only.Great Hall of the Biblioplex {5} animation: 5 mana for a 2/4 and the land is not a creature at resolution; let them do it. Its granted "+1/+0 whenever you cast an instant or sorcery" trigger is counterable once it is a permanent 2/4 and would then blank the land, but that is a lot of setup.Ba Sing Se Earthbend activation: 3 mana for a hasty 2/2 land; Ba Sing Se itself is not a creature at resolution so nothing is stripped. Countering it does prevent the recursion delayed trigger from ever being created, which only matters if you have nothing better that turn.Restless Reef mill-four trigger on its own: the mill is irrelevant; counter it only for the land-blanking side effect, and Hex the animated Reef first if you have it.Intimidation Tactics cycling, Ghost Vacuum's "{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard", and a Food token's "{2}, {T}, Sacrifice: gain 3 life" (the Food is sacrificed as a cost, so they gain nothing): all negligible; the Food matters slightly only with a Fel emblem out.Unholy Annex end-step trigger when they control NO Demon (no Core, no token): you deny a card but save them 2 life; only as an EOT body deploy.Nowhere to Run ETB aimed at Kaito: he is 3/4 on our turn and survives at 0/1.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp is mainly a 2-mana flash flier for Enduring Curiosity triggers against a deck with almost no blockers; it dies to Requiting Hex (MV 2), Shoot the Sheriff (Human Hero is not an outlaw), Nowhere to Run, Deadly Cover-Up and Fel's -3. Its Sting has exactly one target here that nothing else answers: an ANIMATED Firdoch Core, which Hex cannot hit (MV 3) and Sheriff cannot hit (changeling makes it an outlaw). Tapping it and stripping its abilities removes changeling (Core stops being a Demon, so Annex costs them 2 instead of draining you), its mana ability and its animation for as long as Wasp lives, even after it stops being a creature. Every other Sting target (Demon token, animated manlands) is mana value 0 and dies permanently to a 1-mana Hex, so Wasp is the fallback there, not the plan. Deceit on the battlefield has already used both ETBs and evoke, so there is nothing for the Sting to turn off; tapping it is a one-turn fog at best. No hexproof or ward anywhere in their 75.
#1Firdoch Core, animated ({4}: 4/4 artifact creature until end of turn)
WhyThe only Sting target Hex (MV 3) and Sheriff (outlaw via changeling) cannot answer. Wasp taps the 4/4 so it neither attacks nor blocks, and it loses changeling, "{T}: Add one mana of any color" and the animation ability while Wasp lives. No changeling means Core is not a Demon: Annex flips to "you lose 2 life" for them unless they also have a Demon token. Bitter Triumph, Floodpits Drowner and Kaito's -2 are the other outs; Wasp is the cheapest and the only one that also turns off Annex.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, right after the animation resolves and before attackers are declared (needs {1}{U} open on their turn). If they animate to block on your turn, flash Wasp precombat and tap it. The lose-all-abilities effect locks onto the object and keeps applying after end of turn when it is no longer a creature.
AfterTapped 4/4 this turn (the animation effect persists). From end of turn: a noncreature artifact with no abilities at all, no mana, no changeling, no re-animation, until they remove Wasp; then it wakes up and is a Demon again.
#2Demon token (from Ritual Chamber), only when you have no Hex, Sheriff, Drowner or Triumph
WhyFallback only: Requiting Hex kills the token (MV 0) permanently for {B} at instant speed, Sheriff for 2, Drowner shuffles it away; Wasp costs 2, is temporary and ends the moment they Hex/Sheriff/Nowhere to Run the 2/1. If it is all you have, tapping it before attackers skips a 6-point flying hit and it loses flying while Wasp lives, so Siren, Beavers and Wasp attack over it.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared. On your turn, precombat, to remove it as a blocker.
AfterA 6/6 ground vanilla while Wasp lives. It is still a Demon (creature type is not an ability), so Annex still drains 2 each end step. It stays tapped through your next turn (it untaps only in their untap step), which is the only reason Kaito, a 3/4 with no evasion, can attack past it; once it untaps, a flightless Demon still blocks Kaito. When they kill Wasp it flies again.
#3Restless Cottage / Restless Reef / Great Hall of the Biblioplex, animated (fallback to Hex/Sheriff)
WhyOnce animated it is a creature and Wasp can target it; the effect keeps applying after it reverts, so the land loses "{T}: Add..." and its animation for as long as Wasp lives, and Reef loses its granted deathtouch. But Hex destroys any animated land outright (MV 0) and Sheriff hits Horror/Shark/Wizard; use those first. Wasp is right when you are out of them or want the flying body anyway.
WhenTheir beginning of combat immediately after the animation resolves, before attackers. If they animate to block on your turn, tap it then.
AfterStill a 4/4 (or 2/4) this turn, tapped, no abilities. From next turn a land with no abilities until Wasp leaves. Great Hall's animation is permanent, so it stays a blank 2/4 land creature.
#4Earthbended land (Ba Sing Se target)
WhyWasp strips its mana ability and the haste the earthbend gave it; that is all. The 0/0 base P/T is a P/T-setting effect (not an ability) and the +1/+1 counters stay, so it remains a 2/2, and the "When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped" clause is a delayed trigger created by the earthbend effect, not an ability of the land, so it still returns when it dies. Worth it only as a Stone Rain on a land they earthbent for mana; to kill it for good you need Tidebinder on the return trigger, not Wasp.
WhenAny time you have {1}{U} (the animation is permanent); before attackers if it is attacking.
AfterTapped 2/2 land creature with no mana ability and no haste while Wasp lives; still comes back tapped if it dies.
#5Qarsi Revenant (sideboard, 2)
WhyStrips flying, deathtouch and lifelink so it neither races in the air nor trades up nor drains. Hex cannot target it (MV 3); Sheriff can (Vampire), so Wasp is the second-best answer and stops it from blocking our fliers.
WhenTheir beginning of combat before attackers, or your precombat to clear a blocker.
AfterA 3/3 vanilla while Wasp lives. Flying/deathtouch/lifelink counters placed by Renew BEFORE Wasp's effect began are turned off (the counters stay on the creature and work again once Wasp leaves); counters placed AFTER Wasp's effect began still grant their keywords (ruling-2 logic).
Traps
Deceit (as it enters): Flash Wasp to blank Deceit and stop the discard or bounce trigger. The trigger is already on the stack and exists independently of its source; blanking Deceit does not counter it. Only Tidebinder (or We Say Thee Nay! on the Deceit spell) stops it.
Demon token vs Firdoch Core (Annex check): Blank the Demon TOKEN to turn off Annex's drain. Annex checks "If you control a Demon"; Demon is a creature type, not an ability, so a blanked token is still a Demon. Blanking an animated CORE does work, because Core is a Demon only through changeling, which is an ability.
Any attacker after attackers are declared: Wasp an attacking Demon or manland to remove it from combat. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat; it still deals damage. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or tap a would-be blocker on your turn).
Firdoch Core / The Soul Stone / manlands, not animated: Wasp them to shut off mana or changeling. Wasp targets creatures only; an un-animated artifact or land is not a legal target. Wait for the animation, or use Tidebinder on Core's {4}.
Earthbended land: Wasp it, then Shoot the Sheriff it for good. The return-to-battlefield clause is a delayed trigger created by the earthbend effect, not an ability the land has, so "loses all abilities" does not touch it; the land comes back tapped. Only Tidebinder countering that trigger when it dies keeps it dead.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Their turn 4, Core already down, 5 mana: if you have {2}{U} open (on the play) and they cast Ritual Chamber or pay to unlock it, flash Tidebinder on the Demon trigger. They are down 5 mana; Annex keeps draining you as long as Core lives, so follow with Kaito on your turn and pressure. If instead they Deadly Cover-Up an empty board, Tidebinder was never needed.
  • Their end step from turn 4 on, Core (or a Demon token) out, Annex trigger on the stack, nothing else worth holding for: flash Tidebinder on the Annex trigger. Deny the card and the 4-point swing; untap and attack with Enduring Curiosity out for the extra card. This is the default use of a Tidebinder that would otherwise rot.
  • Hardcast Deceit with both triggers on the stack (bounce Kaito or Curiosity, discard your best card): Tidebinder the discard by default; take the bounce if it is a Kaito with real loyalty or a Curiosity you cannot replay. Then Floodpits Drowner taps and stuns the vanilla 5/5 and shuffles it away, or Bitter Triumph it. Requiting Hex cannot target it (MV 6).
  • Evoked Deceit while you happen to have {2}{U} up (their turn 4+ on the play): Tidebinder the discard trigger, never the sacrifice trigger; they lose 2 mana and a card. If you are tapped out, accept the discard; Spell Snare cannot hit Deceit (MV 6 even evoked) and Spell Pierce does not counter creatures. Do not hold mana on turns 2-4 for this.
  • Fel lands and activates (0 to draw, +2 to gain, or -3 on Curiosity or a Beaver): Tidebinder counters whichever it is; Fel is a blank permanent until they kill Tidebinder. Swing Kaito, Wasp, Beavers into him next turn. If he is at 6+ and you cannot hold Tidebinder, Bitter Triumph him at end of turn.
  • They animate Firdoch Core ({4}) to block or attack: Tidebinder the activation (Core loses changeling, mana and animation while Tidebinder lives; Annex now costs them 2) or, if you only have Wasp, flash it before attackers and tap the 4/4 for the same lock while Wasp lives. Hex (MV 3) and Sheriff (outlaw) cannot touch an animated Core; this is the one place Wasp's Sting is the best card.
  • Demon token on board: Requiting Hex it on your turn (MV 0, {B}) or Sheriff it; do not spend Wasp on it unless you are out of Hex, Sheriff, Drowner and Triumph. Wasp at their beginning of combat is the fallback and still skips one 6-point hit.
  • They animate Restless Cottage or Reef at beginning of combat: Hex it (MV 0) if you have one. Otherwise flash Wasp before attackers to tap it and blank the land while Wasp lives, or let it attack and Tidebinder the "Whenever this land attacks" trigger for the same land-blanking effect, blocking the 4/4 with Beavers.
  • Kaito on their turn: Deadly Cover-Up and Fel's -3 miss him (he is not a creature on their turn), but Bitter Triumph, Maelstrom Pulse, The End (side) and Together as One's X damage to any target all kill a planeswalker Kaito. Nowhere to Run can target him through hexproof on your turn but only shrinks him to 0/1.
  • Hand attack: Duress cannot take Tidebinder or Wasp (noncreature only), so expose We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Snare and Spell Pierce to Duress and keep the creature-counters as the interaction that survives it. Deceit's discard takes ANY nonland card and postboard Intimidation Tactics exiles an artifact or creature card, so a Tidebinder in hand is safe only from Duress; when they show Tactics mana and nothing better is coming, flash it at end of turn rather than hold it.
Hold or fire
Never hold {2}{U} on turns 2-4 hoping to catch an evoked Deceit or a turn-4 Chamber; Kaito on 3 and Curiosity on 4 are worth more, and a Tidebinder in hand is itself a Deceit discard target. The clock to watch is Firdoch Core: Core on their turn 3 means 5 mana on their turn 4, which is when Ritual Chamber, Deadly Cover-Up or Fel plus a 1-drop arrive. On the play, cast Kaito turn 3, then on turn 4 choose between Curiosity and holding {2}{U} based on whether Core is on their board (Core out = Chamber or Cover-Up live next turn; holding is reasonable). On the draw, do not try to hold for their turn 4 at all: deploy Kaito, let Requiting Hex (token is MV 0), Shoot the Sheriff or Floodpits Drowner answer a Demon token, and start holding Tidebinder from their turn 5 for Deceit, Chamber or Fel. Priorities when you do have mana up on their turn: Chamber Demon trigger, any Deceit ETB (discard by default, bounce if the target is a valuable Kaito or Curiosity), the first Fel activation of any kind, Core's {4} animation. If none of those comes, countering the Annex end-step trigger while Core or a Demon token is out is always an acceptable EOT use, and a plain EOT 3/2 into an empty board with Curiosity out is fine when they tapped out on turn 3-4; a single main copy that never finds a trigger is worth more as damage than in hand. Wasp: treat it as a 2-mana flash flier for Curiosity triggers; fire it at their end step whenever you have spare {1}{U} and they are low on removal mana. The only reasons to hold it are an animated Core you can tap before attackers (its one job Hex and Sheriff cannot do) or a Demon or manland when you are out of Hex. Both bodies die to Hex, Sheriff, Cover-Up and Fel's -3; the countered ability is the permanent gain, the lock is temporary.
How many to run
Game 1: stock 1 Tidebinder and 2 Wasp is fine. Postboard, the interaction that actually beats their engine is 3 Duress (takes Annex, Chamber, Together as One, Cover-Up or Fel and shows the hand), 2 Spell Pierce (Annex, Core, Together as One, Cover-Up, Fel) and 1 Annul (Annex, Core, Soul Stone, Tome): bring those six in first. Tidebinder: the stock sideboard in the data file has zero copies (the note says Karsten's aggregate carries 2), so this is conditional: if your sideboard has 2 Tidebinder, bring them in as the seventh and eighth cards, to 3 total. Cuts, in order: 2 The Wondrous Wasp, 2 Spell Snare (live only against Sheriff, Triumph, Nowhere to Run, Tome, Soul Stone, Strategic Betrayal), then 2 Requiting Hex (keep 2 for the Demon token and animated manlands, which Hex answers better than any Wasp does). Cutting Wasp to zero is fine here: its only non-dominated Sting target is an animated Core, and postboard it dies to Hex, Sheriff, Nowhere to Run x2, Cover-Up and Intimidation Tactics; keep one only if you want a third evasive Curiosity carrier. Two caveats cap the value of stacking three Tidebinders: their sideboard brings 2 Intimidation Tactics (exiles an artifact or creature card from your hand, so Tidebinder and Wasp are no longer Duress-safe), and Deadly Cover-Up with collect evidence 6, The End and Ancient Vendetta each exile every remaining copy of a card once one Tidebinder is in your graveyard. Value the extra copies as redundancy against Tactics and as more chances to line up one counter, not as a plan that needs a second one later.
Review trail: confidence medium, 5 disputed, 19 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Earthbended land (reviewer 1 finding 7 vs reviewer 3 finding 1): adopted reviewer 3's classification. The oracle text of Earthbend puts "When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped" inside the resolving effect, so it is a delayed triggered ability (CR 603.7a), not an ability granted to the land, and "loses all abilities" does not touch it; my draft's "Sheriff kills it for good" line and my disputed[] bullet calling it a granted ability were wrong and are removed. Kept reviewer 1's operational point, because a delayed triggered ability is still a triggered ability and the rules brief lists delayed triggers among counterable abilities: Tidebinder can counter the return trigger when it fires, the land stays in the graveyard or exile, and nothing loses abilities (source is not a permanent, ruling 3). Rejected reviewer 1's wording that the land "gains" the trigger.
  • Reviewer 2 asked for the Tidebinder verdict to drop toward "situational". Kept the enum at "good" but rewrote the summary and hold_or_fire to their framing (fire when it lines up, never hold mana on turns 2-4, Duress/Pierce/Annul rank above extra copies). Reason: with Core counted as a Demon (changeling, confirmed on the card's type line "Kindred Artifact, Shapeshifter"), the Annex end-step trigger is a live target from their turn 4 in most games, on top of Chamber, Deceit and Fel; four recurring windows plus the Core-animation lock is more than any "situational" card offers, even though the engine itself (enchantment plus mana ability) is out of Tidebinder's reach.
  • Reviewer 2's aside that Nowhere to Run "can target Kaito through hexproof on ours": true (its static lets them target through hexproof and the ETB targets "target creature an opponent controls"), but -3/-3 on a 3/4 Kaito leaves a 0/1 that survives, so it is not a kill; recorded as such in the combined line rather than listed among the spells that kill him.
  • Reviewer 2's list of our sideboard (3 Duress, 2 Spell Pierce, 1 Annul, 1 Ghost Vacuum, 1 Strategic Betrayal, 1 Vren, 2 Day of Black Sun, 2 Flashfreeze, 2 Faebloom Trick) matches the file's our_stock_list_sept_2026 and contains no Tidebinder; the counts recommendation is therefore conditional on the Karsten-aggregate 2 copies, as the file's note describes.
  • Hint-block (ability_tags) items rejected against the oracle text, carried over from the draft: Nowhere to Run tagged with Ward/Hexproof keywords (those words appear in its static about OPPONENTS' creatures; only Flash is its own keyword); Deceit's evoke sacrifice trigger, The Soul Stone's ∞ upkeep trigger and Ritual Chamber's "When you unlock this door" trigger all missing from the tags (added; the Chamber trigger is target #1); the Ba Sing Se return clause tagged as Ba Sing Se's own trigger (it is a delayed trigger created by the Earthbend effect, see above); Restless Cottage/Reef animation activations tagged as ordinary targets (they are traps for the ability-loss purpose, ruling 3); Great Hall's "{T}, Pay 1 life: Add one mana of any color" correctly tagged as a mana ability despite the life payment.
Changes after review
  • FATAL fix: Firdoch Core is a Kindred Artifact, Shapeshifter with changeling, so it is a Demon for Unholy Annex; rewrote how_they_win around the Annex + Core drain/draw engine online from turn 4, changed every "if they control a Demon" conditional to "Core or a Demon token", and made the Annex end-step trigger with Core out a live #2 target instead of a dont_bother.
  • Added Firdoch Core's {4} animation as Tidebinder target #5 with the changeling consequence (Core stops being a Demon, Annex flips to costing them 2, mana and animation gone) and made animated Core the #1 Wasp target because Requiting Hex (MV 2 or less) and Shoot the Sheriff (non-outlaw; changeling makes Core an outlaw) cannot touch it.
  • Fixed the clock: Core on 3 gives 5 mana on their turn 4, so Chamber/Cover-Up/Fel windows open a turn earlier; rewrote timing windows, hold_or_fire on-the-play/on-the-draw advice, and removed the 'never flash before turn 5' rule in favour of a guideline.
  • Merged evoked and hardcast Deceit into one target with honest timing (evoke counter is a bonus, not something to hold for on turns 2-4) and added the both-triggers case: a 6-mana Deceit with {U}{U} and {B}{B} spent fires both the bounce and the discard; Tidebinder counters one; default discard, exception valuable Kaito/Curiosity. Added Restless Reef as a blue source.
  • Fel: reframed to 'counter the first activation you see' with the 0 and +2 named explicitly; -3 kept as the nicest window, -6 as the must-stop; fixed 'dies to any attack' (Spyglass Siren is 1/1) and noted the turn-4/turn-5 arrival with Core.
  • Split the #1 after_effect into the two arrival cases (casting the Chamber half leaves the Annex door locked, so no end-step trigger; unlocking on an existing Annex keeps the draw/drain).
  • Wasp list rewritten: Demon token and animated manlands demoted to Hex/Sheriff fallbacks (all MV 0), Kaito-blocking nuance corrected (a flightless Demon can block a 3/4 Kaito; only the tapped state protects him), Deceit fog entry dropped into the summary, Renew counters corrected (counters stay, the keywords they grant are turned off), and 'no on-board abilities' reworded.
  • Earthbended land corrected in both halves: the return clause is a delayed trigger created by the Earthbend effect, unaffected by Wasp/Tidebinder ability loss; removed 'Sheriff kills it for good'; added Tidebinder countering the return trigger when the land dies as a rare bonus.
  • Mazemind Tome exile trigger corrected: state trigger, countering only delays the exile and the 4 life until Tidebinder leaves.
  • Added Ghost Vacuum's {6}, {T}, sacrifice reanimation as a rare Tidebinder target (our exiled creatures stay exiled) and the Food token sacrifice to dont_bother.
  • Requiting Hex vs Deceit corrected from 'waste' to 'illegal target (MV 6)'.
  • Duress-proofing line corrected: only Duress cannot take Tidebinder/Wasp; Deceit's discard (any nonland card) and postboard Intimidation Tactics (artifact or creature card) can.
  • Kaito safety corrected: Cover-Up and Fel -3 miss him on their turn, but Bitter Triumph, Maelstrom Pulse, The End and Together as One's damage kill him; Nowhere to Run only shrinks him.
  • Evoke-sacrifice trap completed: countering the sacrifice also lets the discard trigger resolve.
  • Answer count corrected from '20+' to 13 maindeck answers plus 3 Duress, with per-card counts from the file.
  • Counts recommendation rewritten: conditional on the sideboard actually holding 2 Tidebinder (stock file has zero), Duress/Spell Pierce/Annul come in first, cuts are Wasp x2, Spell Snare x2, then 2 Hex; Wasp to zero is fine; added Intimidation Tactics and Cover-Up/The End/Vendetta name-exile caveats.
  • Trimmed for cognitive load: Tidebinder targets from 10 to 7 (five primary, Nowhere to Run, one rare-bonus bundle), traps from 14 to 7 grouped by rule, Wasp targets from 5 to 5 re-ranked, Wasp traps 4 to 5 (added earthbend and the token-vs-Core Annex distinction).
  • Added Hex-first notes wherever Tidebinder or Wasp on an animated land was recommended.
  • Recorded all accepted/rejected reviewer points in disputed; confidence kept at medium because the first draft had a fatal blind spot and the rewrite has not been re-reviewed.
vs. Boros Tokens2.1% TB: situationalWasp: good▼
Turn 1 Stadium Headliner or Political Triumph, turn 2 Voice of Victory or Belladonna Took, turn 3 Warleader's Call or Hop to It, then Song of Totentanz for X with haste. Every creature token that enters fires three engines at once: Warleader's Call ("Whenever a creature you control enters, this enchantment deals 1 damage to each opponent"), Belladonna Took ("Whenever a token you control enters... gain 1 life / draw a card / +1/+1 counter on each creature") and Political Triumph ("scry 1 and put a plan counter"; the fourth counter draws and gives the team a counter). Mobilize on Voice and Headliner and Dalkovan Encampment make the attack itself produce tokens, so the engines fire on THEIR turn. The card that shapes the matchup is Voice of Victory's static "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn": while an un-blanked Voice is on the battlefield we cannot flash in Tidebinder or Wasp, or cast We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Snare, Requiting Hex or Bitter Triumph, on their turn. Every creature in their main deck (Voice, Took, Headliner) and Queen post-board has mana value 2 or less, so Requiting Hex on our own turn is the default answer; Wasp and Tidebinder are the backups.
Tishana's Tidebinder
The triggers you want (Took, Triumph, Mobilize) almost all happen on THEIR turn, and Voice of Victory's static stops you casting Tidebinder then. Tidebinder is a real card here only (a) on your own turn when they act at instant speed (Headliner's sac-ping at your creature, Queen's recruit off your removal spell, Frontline Rush tokens, Fountainport activations, Marvel's same-turn power-up) or (b) on their turn once Voice is blanked, dead or never cast (then Took's trigger off Hop to It / Song / Headliner-Mobilize tokens, Headliner's Mobilize 1 itself, Encampment's attack trigger). In most games it is a sorcery-speed 3/2 or a Headliner-ping counter. Never hold 3 mana for it ahead of Kaito or Enduring Curiosity; it is the spare-mana play. Keep the main copy, do not board in more.
#1Belladonna Took
Whenever a token you control enters, you gain 1 life if this is the first time this ability has resolved this turn. If it's the second time, draw a card. If it's the third time, put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control.
WhyTook is their card-advantage and anthem engine and it is a creature, so countering one trigger blanks the whole card while Tidebinder lives. A countered trigger did not resolve, so it does not advance the first/second/third count: when three tokens enter at once (Hop to It, Last Ronin's Technique, Song X=3) three triggers stack; counter any one and only two resolve (life, card), so the +1/+1-counter payoff is denied whichever one you pick. Cheaper alternative when the tokens come from an instant-speed Frontline Rush on your turn: Requiting Hex on Took in response to Rush, before the tokens enter, stops every trigger for 1 mana.
WhenWith the Took trigger on the stack, after the token(s) have entered. YOUR turn: Frontline Rush cast at instant speed (two Goblins = two Took triggers), a Fountainport Fish or Treasure at your end step (a Treasure is a token, so it triggers Took but not Call or Triumph). THEIR turn: only if no un-blanked Voice is on the battlefield; then Hop to It / Song / Last Ronin's Technique tokens in their main phase or Headliner's Warrior in declare attackers. On the draw a their-turn window before turn 4 is rare; on the play, Wasp on Voice turn 3 opens their turn 3.
AfterLoses the token trigger entirely (no life, no cards, no counters) while Tidebinder is on the battlefield. Keeps its 2/2 body, keeps +1/+1 from Warleader's Call (an external static), keeps +1/+1 counters already placed; cards already drawn stay drawn. Other Took triggers already on the stack still resolve (abilities on the stack exist independently of their source), which is why the count-denial trick above matters.
RiskTorch the Tower (2 damage) or Erode kills the 3/2 Tidebinder and Took wakes up immediately. The trigger you countered stays countered.
#2Stadium Headliner (sac-ping)
{1}{R}, Sacrifice this creature: It deals damage equal to the number of creatures you control to target creature.
WhyTheir only creature-based removal and the thing that kills Enduring Curiosity, Dream Beavers, Wasp or Tidebinder itself. It is the ability they most often activate on OUR turn (to clear a blocker or kill an attacker), so this is the Tidebinder window that actually recurs. Countering it saves the creature and Headliner is already sacrificed (cost). Card-for-card plus a 3/2 body.
WhenIn response to the activation on YOUR turn. On their turn under an un-blanked Voice you cannot respond. Headliner is in the graveyard when Tidebinder's trigger resolves, so nothing is blanked (ruling 3).
AfterNothing on the battlefield changes except your creature lives. Headliner stays sacrificed.
RiskNone; a clean 1-for-1 with a body attached.
#3The Queen of Dale (sideboard, expected vs Dimir)
Whenever an opponent casts their first noncreature spell each turn, you recruit. (Draw a card, then discard a card. If you discarded a nonland card, create a 1/1 white Human Soldier creature token.)
WhyQueen is their anti-control card: every removal spell or counterspell we cast gives them a loot and often a token (which then triggers Took, Call and Triumph). She is a creature, so countering the recruit blanks her completely. Wasp or Requiting Hex do the job pre-emptively for less mana; Tidebinder is the backup when you have neither.
WhenYour turn: cast your first noncreature spell (Requiting Hex, Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Duress), let the recruit trigger go on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it. Tidebinder itself is a creature spell and does not trigger her. Under Voice you cannot cast noncreature spells on their turn anyway, so the trigger only appears on your turn.
AfterQueen becomes a 2/1 (3/2 with Call) with no text while Tidebinder lives. The token she might have made is never created.
RiskNone beyond Tidebinder dying to Torch or Erode.
#4Stadium Headliner (Mobilize 1)
Mobilize 1 (Whenever this creature attacks, create a tapped and attacking 1/1 red Warrior creature token. Sacrifice it at the beginning of the next end step.)
WhyHeadliner is on the battlefield (attacking) when this resolves, so countering it blanks Headliner: the sac-ping is gone while Tidebinder lives, the Warrior is never created, and every Took, Call and Triumph trigger that token would have caused never happens. It competes with countering Took's trigger off that same token: choose Headliner's Mobilize when the sac-ping is the threat to Curiosity or Kaito, choose Took's trigger when Took is the bigger engine (usually).
WhenTheir declare-attackers step, with the Mobilize trigger on the stack, and ONLY when no un-blanked Voice of Victory is on the battlefield (Voice blanked by Wasp, stunned, dead or never cast).
AfterHeadliner is a 1/1 (2/2 with Call) attacker with no text while Tidebinder lives; no Warrior, no engine triggers. Once Tidebinder leaves, Headliner can sac-ping again.
RiskTidebinder is then a 3/2 in front of an anthem'd attacking board; expect it to be Torched.
#5Political Triumph
When the fourth plan counter is put on this enchantment, sacrifice it, draw a card, and put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control.
WhyOne-shot payoff and the sacrifice is part of the effect, not a cost. Counter it and Triumph stays on the battlefield holding four counters; a fifth counter is not the fourth, so the payoff never triggers again. Their card becomes a scry-1 machine for good. No blank (enchantment, ruling 3). Do NOT track plan counters for this; take it only if it lines up (Triumph on 3 counters and a token about to enter on your turn, e.g. a Fountainport Fish at your end step or an instant-speed Frontline Rush).
WhenThe scry/plan-counter trigger resolves and places the fourth counter; THEN the fourth-counter trigger goes on the stack. Respond to that second trigger, not the first. Your turn, or their turn with no un-blanked Voice.
AfterTriumph remains with 4 plan counters and still scrys per creature entering; it never draws the card or hands out counters. Nothing loses abilities.
RiskLow. Only a second Political Triumph gets the value back.
#6Dalkovan Encampment (attack trigger)
{2}{W}, {T}: Whenever you attack this turn, create two 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens that are tapped and attacking. Sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step.
WhyThe activation itself is not the target; it only sets up a delayed trigger. Let them pay {2}{W} and tap the land, then counter the delayed 'Whenever you attack this turn' trigger in declare attackers: two Warriors, two Took triggers, two Call pings and two Triumph counters never happen and their mana is spent. Land source, so nothing is blanked.
WhenTheir declare-attackers step with the delayed trigger on the stack, only when no un-blanked Voice is on the battlefield. If they activate on your turn (rare) you can also counter the activation, but the attack trigger is the better moment.
AfterNo tokens; Encampment is unchanged (land).
